# Pruv (formerly Vow) v0.1 Experiment Results (2026-11-10)

> Rename (2026-11-10): the language name changed from Vow to **Pruv** (§14). Sections up to §13 are records from the time, so they keep the old name and paths. Old-to-new path mapping: `vowc/vowc.py` → `pruvc/pruvc.py`, `*.vow` → `*.pruv`, `solutions/vow_try1`·`vow_agg` → `pruv_try1`·`pruv_agg`, `g1/solutions/ref_vow` → `ref_pruv`, `solve/vow` → `solve/pruv`, runtime error prefix `vow:` → `pruv:`. The bench result JSON files (`mutants*.json`, `results_*vow*.json`, `g1/results_*.json`) and the `solve/` input copies (SPEC·PROBLEMS) are preserved under the old name.

## 1. Independent AI worker (Claude sonnet, written in one shot without running anything)
| | First attempt | After 1 fix |
|---|---|---|
| Rust | 10/10 pass | - |
| Vow (seeing only the 6-page spec) | 9/10 (p03 overflow proof failed, not a real bug) | 10/10 |

- p03: from a single compiler JSON counterexample (`len=0, i=i64::MAX`), the worker fixed the invariant and passed.
- The worker did not write the key behavioral contract for p04 (minimum index)·p06·p10 → logic bugs in those parts cannot be caught by Vow either.

## 2. Mutation testing (83 small bugs injected into solutions/vow_try1, bench/mutants.json)
| Result | Count |
|---|---|
| Mutants that are wrong under tests (including 20 infinite loops) | 79 |
| └ Vow rejects at compile time | **73 (92%)** |
| └ Vow lets through | 6 |
| Mutants that pass tests (same behavior) | 4 (1 of them rejected by Vow = false positive) |

- rustc alone lets all 79 through compilation (because the types match). Out-of-range accesses show up in Rust as panics at run time, but the other logic bugs silently produce wrong values.
- The 6 that were missed are all value bugs such as `+`↔`-`, `<`↔`<=` in functions without a behavioral contract (sum, maximum subarray sum, count, dot product).
- Rejection reasons: invariant preservation failure 30, out of range 14+6=20, invariant initiation 14, postcondition 4, termination 3, other 2.
- 1 false positive: p02 `i = 1`→`0` behaves correctly but is rejected because it conflicts with the invariant `1 <= i`.

## 3. Speed (1,000,000 inputs, median of 21 runs)
The Rust generated by Vow, the worker's Rust, and the reference Rust were mostly comparable within measurement noise (up to ±40% even between identical code). On p04 Vow came out slower once (93ms vs 67ms), but no conclusion can be drawn before repeated measurements.

## 4. Conclusion and limitations
- Hypothesis "fewer errors, same speed": the result was **same speed, and errors drop substantially in proportion to the contracts written**.
- Ultimately the effect of this language depends on how strongly the AI writes contracts. The next step is to make aggregates like sum and max usable in contracts (specification functions).
- Limitations: the 10 problems are easy. There is only one model as the worker. Mutation testing was run only on solutions I wrote. The compiler (written by me) has not yet been audited. If the verifier has a soundness bug, the numbers change.

## 5. Stage 2: aggregates for contracts (2026-11-10)
Syntax `sum/count/max/min(k in lo..hi: e)` (SPEC §6). The aggregate is treated as an undefined z3 function F(lo, hi, outer bound variables),
and at each place it is used, "unfold one step" facts (shrinking/extending the upper and lower ends, sum·count empty range = 0) are added as lemmas.
Ends containing a bound variable are not moved, which prevents matching loops. The proof is first attempted without lemmas, and if that fails the lemmas are added and it is retried.

| | 1st round (no contracts, mutants.json) | Aggregate-contract implementation (mutants3.json) |
|---|---|---|
| Mutants wrong under tests | 79 | 79 |
| └ Vow rejects at compile time | 73 (92%) | **79 (100%)** |
| └ of those, rejected by timeout (verify_unknown) | 0 | 0 |
| Rejections among same-behavior mutants (false positives) | 1 / 4 | 1 / 4 (same p02 `i = 1`→`0`) |

- Solutions: `bench/solutions/vow_agg/` (aggregate ensures·invariants added to vow_try1 only for p01·p06·p07·p09). All 4 proved in 0.1~0.3 seconds, re-grading 10/10.
- It catches all 6 that were previously missed (p01 `+`→`-`, p06 `cur + v`→`cur - v`, p07 `<`→`<=`·`c + 1`→`c`, p09 `+`→`-`·`*`→`+`).
- p06 (maximum subarray sum) cannot be proved when written with `max` (an inductive fact like "max(e + c) = max(e) + c" is needed). Instead,
  writing it as "greater than or equal to every partial sum (forall) and equal to one of them (exists)" makes it provable.
- The intermediate implementation (which put lemmas into every proof, mutants2.json) also caught 79/79, but 4 p06 mutants were rejected by timeout
  and so had no counterexample. After splitting the proof into two passes, that became 0.
- Regression suite: tests/must_reject 20/20 still rejected, examples unchanged. 5 deliberately wrong aggregate contracts (outer-variable body, nested aggregate, empty max, etc.) were all rejected.
- Audit (Claude sonnet): soundness must-fix 0; after the fixes, the confirmation audit had must-fix 0. Both audits judged only by reading code because the shell was blocked, and I did the reproduction with the tests above.
- Limitation: because unfolding is one step per place, correct contracts that compute several steps of a fixed range, like `sum(k in 0..3: j) == 3*j`, cannot be proved (timeout).
  Facts such as middle element of a range <= max, or splitting a sum at an arbitrary point, are also not known automatically. I wrote the aggregate contracts myself. Whether an independent AI writes aggregate contracts on its own has not yet been measured.

## 6. Stage 0: safe mode and public function boundary checks (2026-11-10)
Two holes fixed:
1. With `--no-verify` (no proving), it still emitted `get_unchecked` and silently wrapping arithmetic → safe mode: checks indexing, arithmetic, and alloc size and panics on violation (`vow: index out of bounds`, `vow: arithmetic error`). `/` `%` use the same domain as the verifier (divisor > 0, dividend >= 0).
2. Nothing stopped Rust code from calling a generated `pub fn` in violation of requires → a function with requires becomes `pub fn f` (checks requires at run time, `vow: requires violated`) + a private `f__vow_body`. In proof mode, inner calls go directly to the body. Contracts are evaluated in i128 with Euclidean division. `--trust-callers` turns off the check.

- Tests: `tests/run_all.py` 57/57 (reject 24, examples in three modes 10, runtime 23).
- Audit (Claude) must-fix 2: generated temporary names (`vow_k1`, `vow_i`) shadowed user variables of the same name, bypassing the requires check, and in proof mode an out-of-range `get_unchecked` (abnormal termination reproduced); safe-mode array writes wrote wrong values. → The parser now rejects names with the `vow_` prefix or containing `__` (`reserved_name`).
- Confirmation after the fix (Claude) must-fix 0. Rust keywords and `_` names (rustc compile failure), which had been recommendations, were also rejected as reserved words, and I directly reproduced and fixed that the contract helper did not compile under Rust 2015 (the default of `rustc -O x.rs`) (`usize::try_from`).
- Mutation testing (`bench/mutants4.json`, vow_agg): 79/79 rejected, false positives 1/4 unchanged. As the reference build (`--no-verify`) became safe mode, 9 mutants that had been timeouts (undefined-behavior infinite loops, etc.) turned into `wrong` (panic).
- Speed (vow_agg, ms, median of 21 runs): with `--trust-callers` it is the same as before (within error of the Rust reference). With checks on, problems with an element-range forall get slower (p01 0.20→1.13, p05 0.30→0.76, p09 0.43→1.20). p04 writes its sortedness precondition as a double forall, making an O(n^2) check at n=10^6, so the performance stage timed out. Speed comparisons are measured with `--trust-callers`.
- Remaining: replace heavy requires (sorting, etc.) with an O(n) check of the same meaning (a proven equivalence transformation), a length upper bound for numeric-range quantifiers, and also putting the contract domain (out-of-range indices, etc.) into verifier proof obligations to align meaning with the runtime check.

## 7. M1 = C2: LLVM IR backend (2026-11-10)
`vowc --backend llvm` emits text LLVM IR (`.ll`) and Rust bindings (`.rs`) together. The runtime support code is `vowc/runtime.c`, compiled by clang. The spec is in `docs/M1_LLVM_SPEC.md`. The reference is the Rust backend (`codegen.py`), which was not modified.
- The test harness keeps the Rust driver as is and puts the bindings into `mod sol`. The two object files (gen.o, rt.o) are attached to rustc as link-args. Returned arrays are allocated with the Rust allocator and passed as `Vec` without copying.
- Regression suite (`tests/run_all.py --backend both`) 102/102 (110/110 after wrap-up, 33 runtime tests)
  - Reject 24, and per backend examples 10, runtime 29.
  - New tests r24·r25: evaluation order. r26~r29: array deallocation paths. The deallocation-path tests use runtime.c `-DVOW_COUNT_ALLOCS` to check that the allocation count equals the free count.
- Proof facts in the IR
  - Proof-mode body: arithmetic is `add/sub/mul nsw`, array access is `getelementptr inbounds`, division is `udiv/urem` (the verifier proves the domain).
  - Array arguments: `noalias nocapture readonly`. The basis is the ownership rule of check.py (no modifying, storing, or returning arguments).
  - Safe-mode bodies have no `nsw` (confirmed by test).
- bench vow_agg `--trust-callers`: 10/10 on both backends.
  - Speed was compared against the Rust reference inside the same executable, because the reference time fluctuates between runs.
  - Same: p01·p02·p05·p06·p07·p10, p08 (0.20ms vs 0.20ms after fixing it to a zero-initialized allocation).
  - LLVM faster: p03 (1.17x relative to the reference, Rust implementation 1.48x), p09 (0.79x, Rust implementation 1.0x).
  - **LLVM slower: p04 by 4~5%.** Measured twice. Rust implementation 33.4~33.5ms, LLVM implementation 34.9~35.3ms. It is a problem that makes a million calls. I presumed it is because the Rust driver cannot inline a function across the FFI boundary, and did not verify this. This one problem fails to meet the M1 speed criterion (within error or faster).
- Mutation testing (`bench/mutants5_llvm.json`, build before the audit fixes)
  - All 81 verifier verdicts are the same as the Rust implementation (mutants4). 79/79 rejected, false positives 1/4.
  - 4 test results differ, where a Rust implementation timeout became `wrong` in the LLVM implementation. They are `i = i + 1`→`i = i` in p01·p05·p09, and `lo < hi`→`lo <= hi` in p04.
  - I reproduced p01 alone. In the non-terminating loop the accumulator overflows and it stops with `overflow in +` (101). I take it that the LLVM implementation reaches that point within the 120-second limit. I did not confirm whether running the Rust implementation to the end gives the same error.
  - There is one more reason for the difference. A panic in the LLVM implementation is C `exit(101)`, so the whole process ends, whereas the Rust implementation continues with the next input via `catch_unwind`. Both are "not a pass", so the verdict is the same.
- Audit (Claude Opus) must-fix 1
  - In proof-mode `a[i] = v`, the reference evaluates v first, but the LLVM implementation evaluated i first. Calling a `! io` function inside the expression changes the output order (reference 2,1,2, LLVM 1,2,2). I reproduced it directly and fixed it.
  - Recommendations fixed at the same time: flush Rust stdout before a panic, zero-initialized allocation, deallocation-path execution tests, narrow the IR check to the body function, internal errors as exit 3 and classified as crash in mutation testing, CPU options per architecture.
- Confirmation after the fix (Claude Opus): must-fix 0.
  - It judged the noalias·readonly attributes to be sound.
  - Remaining recommendations:
    - Make the proof-mode nsw check strict at the instruction level.
    - Strengthen the deallocation tests: proof-mode implementation, temporaries inside while conditions·return expressions·index assignments, zero-length arrays.
    - If a leak and a double free occur together, the counts cancel out and it is missed.
- Wrap-up (same day, after commit d6aa190)
  - Re-ran mutation testing after the audit fixes (`bench/mutants6_llvm.json`): exactly the same as mutants5. The verifier verdicts are the same as the Rust implementation, and the 4 timeout→wrong cases are also unchanged.
  - Added deallocation tests r30~r33, making 33 runtime tests. Both backends passed, and the Rust implementation output matched the hand-computed expected values.
    - r30: while local arrays, reassignment, temporaries in proof mode
    - r31: temporaries inside while conditions·return expressions·index assignments
    - r32: zero-length arrays
    - r33: return in both branches of if/else
  - I could not determine the cause of p04. Because of other work, the load average was 12~34 and measurements fluctuated.
    - I measured the ratio against the reference inside the same executable four times and took the median. Rust implementation 1.08, Rust implementation with inlining of public functions blocked 1.22, LLVM implementation 1.32.
    - The call boundary (no inlining) explains part of the difference but apparently not all of it. The remaining candidates are the cost of returning Option through an out-pointer and differences in the body code.
    - It needs to be measured again on a quiet machine.
- p04 follow-up (return Option·Result in `{i64,i64}` registers at the external ABI)
  - Removed the out-pointer. It is for macOS arm64·SysV only (spec §4).
  - Regression suite 110/110.
  - Measured four implementations five times each and took the median of the ratio against the reference inside the same executable. The load average was again 15~21.

    | Implementation | Relative to reference |
    |---|---|
    | Rust implementation | 1.14 |
    | Rust implementation, inlining blocked | 1.13 |
    | LLVM implementation, out-pointer | 1.21 |
    | LLVM implementation, register return | 1.16 |

  - Register return reduced the gap with the Rust implementation from about 6% to about 1.5%. That is within the fluctuation between repetitions (±10%).
  - Verdict: the remaining gap is within measurement error. I consider the M1 speed criterion met by this. However, I could not confirm it on a quiet machine.
- Remaining
  - Measure p04 once more on a quiet machine.
  - Windows x64 ABI: 16-byte struct returns must be lowered (M3).
  - Windows·Linux link tests. The M1 artifacts are for macOS arm64 development.

## 8. M2 = G1: structs·enums (2026-11-10)
Design `docs/M2_G1_SPEC.md`, language `SPEC.md` §6a. Fields are only i64·bool·Option/Result<i64>·other user types (array fields, recursive types, `[Point]`, `Option<Point>` are deferred to G2·G3). Nested field assignment on `let mut` locals, statement `match`, contract-only `is_variant`·`variant_field`.
- Verifier: z3 Datatype. `variant_field` proves its domain following the actual evaluation order (short-circuiting, early exit of forall/exists) (`variant_domain`). Changing one field in a loop forgets that entire variable.
- Two backends: Rust uses `Copy` types and i128 contract counterpart types; LLVM uses an aggregate value that flattens the tag + all variant payloads. The external boundary is a pointer to an i64 word array. The M1 Option/Result ABI is unchanged.

Five soundness gates:
| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| 1. SPEC within one page | SPEC.md §6a |
| 2. Reject wrong programs·wrong contracts | all 36 `tests/must_reject/g1_*` rejected at the expected stage·code (the runner also checks the stage·code in `.json`) |
| 3. Keep existing numbers | `run_all --backend both` 250/250. Verdicts for the 81 mutants (LLVM, vow_agg) are all the same as M1 (`mutants6_llvm.json`), all 71 wrong under tests rejected (`bench/mutants7_g1_llvm.json`). bench 10 problems correct (both backends) |
| 4. 1 audit + 1 confirmation (Claude opus) | must-fix defects 0. Among recommendations, fixed test gaps (8 reproductions added), allowing `E::V()`, diagnostic keys, and repeated conversion of Rust runtime requires. Verification time for 47 existing programs HEAD 1.27~1.30s → 1.22~1.25s |
| 5. Independent AI measurement | below |

Independent AI (Claude sonnet, only SPEC.md and `bench/g1/PROBLEMS.md`, written in one shot without running anything), 5 problems in `bench/g1/`:
| | First attempt | After 1 fix |
|---|---|---|
| Vow G1 | 4/5 (g01 rejected) | 5/5 (both backends, 307~310 tests passed each) |
- g01: the code was right and the contract was wrong. It wrote "if two rectangles strictly overlap, area > 0", which is false for a rectangle of width 0. The verifier rejected it with that counterexample, and the worker fixed the precondition after seeing the counterexample and passed. This is a case of catching a wrong promise, which is the desired behavior.
- At that time the counterexample's struct values came out as z3 internal names (`m1t1!Rect(...)`), so I converted them to Vow syntax (`Rect { lo: Point { x: .., y: .. }, .. }`). The error passed to the retry was cleaned up by the main agent into the same form.
- The worker wrote behavioral contracts on its own (g02·g04 per-variant results, g05 first negative condition, g03 min/max with forall/exists instead of aggregates).

Remaining
- p04 speed: on this day's machine (a recording ffmpeg using 1.5 CPUs), even the previous commit hits the 120-second limit. Correctness (307/307, both backends) was confirmed, and speed will be re-measured on a quiet machine.
- The LLVM entry also converts user-type parameters not used in requires into contract values (deferred on the expectation that it disappears at -O2).
- The flattened enum layout grows with many variants. After measuring, decide on a union layout·register return of small types.

## 9. Stage 0 remaining tasks: contract domain·runtime iteration budget·O(n) sortedness precondition (2026-11-10)
Decision `docs/M0_CONTRACTS.md` (design draft by Codex gpt-6-astra, revised by the main agent), spec `SPEC.md` §6·6a·7.
1. **Contract domain as verifier proof obligations.** Inside contracts, `a[i]` (range), `/` `%` (divisor != 0, contract division is Euclidean), `some_val`·`ok_val`·`err_val` (tag), and empty `max`/`min` (lo < hi) are proved in short-circuit·quantifier reach order (new error code `contract_domain`, variant_field uses the existing `variant_domain`). requires is proved using only earlier requires; ensures·invariant use clauses of the same kind up to the previous one. Now, for proven programs, the public function runtime check no longer produces `requires undefined` because of the domain.
   - Only the variant_field domain goes into assumptions. Assuming other domains as well loaded nested-quantifier index ranges into every query, and the p06 proof went from 0.44 seconds to 69 seconds (0.61 seconds after reverting).
   - The callee requires domain is not re-proved at the call site (the callee proves it, audit recommendation).
   - Intended semantic change: unguarded `max`·`variant_field` requires (r20, g1_r06) are rejected in default mode. The two runtime tests use `--no-verify` only, and r21 is preceded by `len(a) >= 1`.
2. **Runtime iteration budget.** Instead of a static upper bound, the entire requires check at a single public function entry shares 2^30 quantifier·aggregate iterations. If exceeded, `vow: requires undefined: contract iteration budget exceeded` (101, both backends). Expressions skipped by empty ranges·short-circuiting do not consume it.
3. **Sortedness precondition O(n).** `forall i in 0..len(a): forall j in i(+1)..len(a): a[i] <= a[j]` is recognized by AST (`codegen.match_sorted_requires`) and only the runtime check loops with `a[k-1] <= a[k]`. Both backends convert it the same way wherever it is in the expression (right of a short-circuit, inside an outer quantifier). The verifier uses the original expression. At 200,000 elements the double form exceeds the budget while the converted form passes (`sort_nested_big`).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Regression `run_all --backend both` | 341/341 (the previous 250 plus added reject·runtime tests). New reject tests cd01~cd19 rejected with the intended codes |
| Existing solutions | vow_agg 10·examples·g1 ref_vow·indep_try2 all proved unchanged (indep_try1/g01 rejected with the same `ensures` as before) |
| bench vow_agg | both backends 9/10 normal through performance, p04 exceeds 120 seconds in the performance stage (machine load, same on the previous commit), correctness 307/307 on both backends (noperf) |
| Mutants (LLVM, `bench/mutants8_m0_llvm.json`) | verdicts·error codes for 81 all the same as `mutants7_g1_llvm.json` |
| g1 | ref_vow (LLVM)·indep_try2 (Rust) 5/5 |
| Audit (Claude opus) | must-fix 0, recommendations 6: reflected removing call-site domain duplication, adding 11 test gaps, and the SPEC update. Domain computation duplication·aggregate lemma accumulation (performance) left since measurements show no impact. Confirmation after the fix (Claude opus) must-fix 0, recommendations 3 (test polishing: make cd15 fail only at entry, cd19 paired test, `%` Euclidean runtime test) reflected |

- The p04 performance section calls the public function n times, so even if the check becomes O(n) the total is O(n^2). Algorithm speed comparisons continue to use `--trust-callers`.
- Limitations: after a loop modification and after a loop, the non-variant domain of the invariant is not assumed (same as before, sound but proves less). The runtime check uses i128, so even a mathematically true contract gives `requires undefined` if an intermediate value overflows (it does not let a false requires through). The sorting transformation covers only these two forms. The iteration budget value (2^30) is a policy value.
- Next: random program fuzzing (`bench/fuzz/`, independent oracle + cross-check of Rust·LLVM × proof·safe 4 modes, plan in M0_CONTRACTS section D).

## 10. Test harness speed and differential fuzzing (2026-11-10)
1. **Test harness.** `tests/run_all.py` runs each test as one task in a thread pool (CPU count) and prints results in the original order (format·`--only`·`--backend`·exit code unchanged). runtime.c is compiled only once per variant. Added the hidden internal option `vowc --contract-budget N` (test only, outside SPEC) and the runtime json fields `contract_budget`·`ll_contains`. The budget test that used to loop 1 billion times was reduced to a budget of 1000 (`qb_budget_edge` is the boundary: passes at 1000 iterations, fails at 1001), and the actual default 2^30 is pinned by `qb_across_requires` through the generated-code text of both backends (confirmed that changing the default to 2^21 fails both backends). Full regression of both backends 214 seconds (before the change, under load) → 47~51 seconds.
2. **Fuzzer** `bench/fuzz/` (generate·oracle·run·README). The oracle is an independent parser·arbitrary-precision interpreter that does not use vowc, and programs that pass verification are run in Rust·LLVM × proof·safe 4 modes and cross-checked. Bundling 25 programs into one binary reduced the macOS first-run check (0.33 seconds per binary, serial) (100 programs 188 seconds→34 seconds). 600 take about 2.5 minutes.
3. **Defects found and fixes.**
   - fz01: `a[i] = v` is evaluated value-first in proof mode and index-first in safe mode (output differs by mode). Of the same kind, `alloc(n, v)` was also v-first in proof mode (fz04). Decision: code uses source order in all modes (SPEC §5, M1_LLVM_SPEC §5). Changed the expected values of r24, which fixed the old order, and added r34_eval_order, which fixes 9 order configurations.
   - fz02·fz03: rustc's default deny lints blocked unreachable `x % 0`·constant overflow in a function that passes verification because its requires is contradictory. Added `unconditional_panic`, `arithmetic_overflow` to the Rust backend allow list.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Regression `run_all --backend both` | 363/363, about 50 seconds |
| bench vow_agg | both backends 9/10 normal through performance, p04 exceeds 120 seconds in the performance stage (same as before), correctness on both backends 307/307 (noperf) |
| Mutants (LLVM) | verdicts·error codes for 81 all the same as `mutants8_m0_llvm.json` |
| Fuzzing re-run (seed 41) | candidates 400 → passed 183, inputs 1,698·executions 6,312, 68 seconds, failures 0 |
| Audit (Claude opus) | Batch 1: must-fix 1 (default budget pinning weakened)·recommendations 5 → pinning·Ctrl-C cancellation·range assert reflected, confirmation audit must-fix 0. Batch 2: must-fix 0, of 4 recommendations only the order-test strengthening reflected. The fuzzer had 2 audits in a sub-conversation |

- Left: the fuzzer calls vowc separately per backend, so the LLVM proof build runs verification once more (a fuzzer speed issue, deferred because it needs a compiler option). The generator does not yet produce multiple arrays·array returns·structs·enums·Option/Result. The next fuzzing is toward widening the generator's scope.

## 11. Widening the fuzzing generator's scope (2026-11-10)
1. **Oracle** (`bench/fuzz/oracle.py`, still not using vowc): struct·enum declarations·literals·field reads/assignments (nested), `match` (Option·Result·enum), `Some/None/Ok/Err`, array returns·array local reassignment, contract-only `is_some/some_val/is_ok/ok_val/err_val/is_variant/variant_field` and struct·enum·Option `==`. Out-of-domain cases (`some_val(None)`, `variant_field` of a different variant, etc.) take the same path as existing domain violations (requires gives `requires undefined`). Values have copy semantics. `format_value` follows the Rust `{:?}` shape (struct `Point { x: 1, y: -2 }`, enum `Hit(Point { .. }, true)`).
2. **Harness** (`run.py`): the driver builds Option·Result·struct·enum arguments as Rust expressions and prints all returns with `{:?}`. It counts input candidates that raised an exception in the requires check (`input_errors`), and if all raise or there are 0 inputs it is `harness_error`.
3. **Generator** (`generate.py`) new templates: `twoarr` (2 arrays), `arrret` (`-> [i64]`, reassigned from `copy`·`alloc`·helper function results), `option` (Option/Result return, `match` on helper function results), `optparam` (Option/Result parameter, `some_val` with/without a guard), `struct` (scenario at 70% that receives and returns structs·enums at the f boundary, struct literals inside requires·enum·Option comparison, `tap(v) ! io` to observe field·argument evaluation order·single evaluation of the match target·two-field overflow order, empty struct·Result field·enum field).
4. **Result**: no compiler defects were found. Deliberately corrupting the oracle's struct output gets 84 `oracle_mismatch` cases caught, so the comparison is alive.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Fuzzing seed 51 (new templates, 1st round) | passed 109, inputs 1,028, 18 seconds, failures 0 |
| Fuzzing seed 52 | passed 809, inputs 6,586, 132 seconds, failures 0 |
| Fuzzing seed 54 (128 f-boundary struct candidates) | passed 808, inputs 6,610, 145 seconds, failures 0 |
| Fuzzing seed 56 (after audit fixes) | passed 805, inputs 6,550, 154 seconds, failures 0, `input_errors` 0 |
| Regression `run_all --backend both` | 363/363, 108 seconds (load from other work) |
| Audit (Claude opus) | No false-negative paths in the oracle·harness. Must-fix 2 (no user-type comparison in requires, no io in the new templates — generator scope holes)·5 of 6 recommendations reflected (the oracle argument-leaf check is left since it surfaces as a rustc build error). Confirmation after the fix (Claude opus): must-fix 0, of 7 recommendations only the two variants that were always rejected (option_tap arg, tmatch Hit only) fixed → sample 14/14 passed verification, seed 57 passed 212 failures 0 (38 seconds) |

- The compiler was not changed, so bench performance·the full mutation run·g1 were not run.
- Left: ~~the fuzzer calls vowc separately per backend, so the LLVM proof build runs verification once more~~ (removed in §12). Inputs exceeding i128 in requires comparisons are rare because of boundary requires. For some comparison requires (e2·qval·always-true struct `==`·`Some(x)`), the input hints do not produce violating values well (confirmation audit recommendation, left).

## 12. Removing the LLVM proof build re-verification in the fuzzer (2026-11-10)
1. **Problem**: because the fuzzer (`bench/fuzz/run.py`) called vowc separately per backend, the LLVM build (`--backend llvm --rust-bindings` in `prep_llvm`) of a program that passed verification ran once more the z3 verification already done in phase_a.
2. **Fix**: a hidden test option `--also-llvm LL RS` in vowc (`vowc/vowc.py`, outside SPEC and not in help, like `--contract-budget`). It is accepted only on the Rust backend, and with the same parsing·checking·verification done once, it writes the Rust output together with the LLVM IR·bindings. An option that "trusts that it is already verified" would be a path to emitting an unverified program in proof mode, so it was not chosen. If verification fails, no file is written; files are written only after all output text has been built (so if the generator has an internal error, no output from this invocation remains). If OUT·LL·RS overlap or it is given together with `--backend llvm`, it is a usage error (exit 2). The harness attaches this option to the two phase_a invocations (`--no-verify`, default), deletes the output paths before invoking, and `prep_llvm` does not call vowc but only passes those files to clang. The `llvm-proof` file is written only when status is `verified` (otherwise `harness_error`).
3. **The verifier·code generation were not changed** (only the option and output paths). The audit confirmed in code that the two generators do not modify `prog`, and a new test confirms that the output is byte-identical to separate invocations. So bench performance·the full mutation run·g1 were not run.
4. **New test** `tests/cli_also_llvm.py` (run separately, outside run_all): examples × 3 modes byte-identical, must-reject exit 1·no files·same diagnostics, 5 kinds of usage errors (exit 2·no files), 40 fuzzer-generated programs × 2 modes. Diagnostics are compared only by (stage, code, line, col). This is because repeating the same vowc invocation sometimes changes the z3 counterexample values (1 in 4 times, existing behavior).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `tests/cli_also_llvm.py` | 185/185, 3 times in a row, about 7 seconds |
| Regression `run_all --backend both` | 363/363, 106 seconds |
| Fuzzing seed 56 before·after (1st pair, the audit was running alongside during the after measurement) | failures 0, passed 808·inputs 6,550·executions 25,720 the same. Wall 232→200 seconds, CPU (user+sys) 805→645 seconds |
| Fuzzing seed 56 before·after (2nd pair, back to back, alone) | same result. Wall 291→264 seconds (generation+verification 65→75, build 98→50), CPU 876→671 seconds |
| Audit (Claude opus) | must-fix 0. Verification failure·timeout·stale file·replay·reject-sample paths are all safe. 4 of 6 recommendations reflected (`rust+llvm`·end of output in LLVM generator error details, reject llvm backend + also_llvm in library calls, documentation wording, test strengthening). Comparing paths that differ only in case has the same limitation as the existing `--rust-bindings` so it is left, and the cost of producing LLVM even for candidates that will not be built is smaller than the gain from removing one invocation so it is left. Confirmation after the fix (Claude opus): must-fix 0. The diagnostic comparison is an ordered list of (stage, code, line, col), so it is stricter than a set of keys, and the omitted message·fix are determined by code except for `verify_unknown`, so little information is lost. The recommendations (also compare message·fix, compare stderr on reject paths) are left and only an unused variable was removed |

- Time: CPU time dropped by over 20% in both pairs. Wall clock fluctuated from other workload on the same Mac (execution stage of the same code was 81~138 seconds) so the reduction appears to be only 9~14%. LLVM generation moved into the generation+verification stage, so that column grows and the build column shrinks.
- The candidate count difference of 1,519/1,520 is the tail (1 rejection) at the point where streaming submission stops, and the build targets are the same.
- Changed failure classification: an internal error of the LLVM generator is now caught in phase_a as `internal_error` and that whole program drops out (previously only the llvm mode gave `build_error`). What is stored as a failure is the same.

## 13. M3 = C4 first batch: design finalized (2026-11-10)
1. **Deliverable**: `docs/M3_C4_SPEC.md` (396 lines, finalized, design debate incorporated). No code was changed (full regression suite skipped).
2. **Survey** (web·community, code): language Rust (Go uses cgo for both z3 and LLVM, so static linking·cross-building is worse). z3 crate `=0.22.0` `vendored` + `z3-src =503.3.0` (z3 `gh-release` missing `libz3.a` after 5.3.0, report Z3Prover/z3#11266). LLVM is `llvm-sys 221` (LLVM 22) `force-static` (`prefer-static` silently falls through to dynamic); the first port moves the text IR generator as is, reads it with the LLVM library, and produces O2·object files. The system linker is borrowed (Windows needs MSVC Build Tools). The Python implementation already has 2 nondeterministic traversals (`check.py` call graph, `verify.py` havoc set) → sort them in 0A.
3. **Prototype** (`Lair Outputs/.../spike`, this Mac arm64): z3 query + text IR → `default<O2>` → object → `cc` link → ran successfully.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Static z3 runtime version | `5.3.3.0` = Python implementation z3-solver 5.3.3.0 |
| Build | cold 393 seconds (mostly z3 C++, LLVM is the Homebrew installation), rebuild 6 seconds |
| Size | stripped 105MB (initializes all LLVM targets) |
| Dynamic dependencies | macOS default libraries + Homebrew `libzstd` (not distributable) → the distributed LLVM is built directly with zstd·libxml2·zlib turned off |

4. **Design debate** (Codex): all 13 points adopted or reflected with modification. Major change: put risks first in the first implementation batch (0A determinism + 0B z3 real-obligation prototype·SMT-LIB cross-check + 0C turn the Python .ll into objects with the LLVM library to cut the test harness's clang dependency first + 0D Windows ABI samples), keep the CLI default in M3 (add `--emit exe`), the generator emits Windows sret directly per target, cost caps (150MB·CI cold 120 minutes), release order macOS arm64 → Linux → Windows → macOS x64. Among the opposing opinions, "abandon porting the Rust source backend" was not accepted and was deferred to the very end of M3 (because of the Stage 6 multi-language output plan and the aggregation of existing Rust-output tests).
- Verification time: prototype build 393 seconds once. Regression suite·bench were not run because there was no code change.

## 14. Rename Vow → Pruv (2026-11-10)
Because a language of a similar nature (vow-lang) exists, the name was changed to Pruv (rationale in the BLUEPRINT header·§6). Domain `pruvlang.org` secured.

| Target | Handling |
|---|---|
| Compiler | `vowc/` → `pruvc/`, `vowc.py` → `pruvc.py` (git mv, history kept), usage text `pruvc.py` |
| Source files | 205 `*.vow` → `*.pruv`, solution folders `pruv_try1`·`pruv_agg`·`ref_pruv`·`solve/pruv` |
| Generated code·runtime support | runtime error prefix `pruv:`, symbols `__pruv_body`·`pruv_abi_*`·`Pruv__Tagged`·`pruv_runtime_rust_allocator_v1`, macros `PRUV_*`, `PruvError` |
| Language rules | reserved-name prefix `vow_` → `pruv_` (changed together because it is a rule for not colliding with generated symbols; the must_reject tests were also changed) |
| Test·bench harness | path constants·commands·temporary folder prefixes·result keys (`pruv_error`·`pruv_errors`, mutate verdicts `pruv`·`pruv_stage`·`pruv_code`) |
| Documents | README·SPEC·BLUEPRINT·docs/M0~M3·bench/fuzz/README·PROBLEMS body text |
| Left as is | RESULTS §1~§13 (mapping table in the header), `docs/research-2026-11.md` (survey record from the time, memo in the header), bench result JSON (`mutants*.json`·`results_*vow*.json`·`g1/results_*.json`, old keys such as `vow_stage`), `solve/` input copies SPEC·PROBLEMS (text the worker saw at the time, memo in the header), the mention of the competing language Vow (vow-lang) in BLUEPRINT |

- Method: after git mv, a case-preserving simple substitution (`Vow`/`VOW`/`vow` → `Pruv`/`PRUV`/`pruv`) on 50 live files (including BLUEPRINT). There was no place where 'vow' was used as an English word or in another sense (all tokens checked).
- Verification: `run_all --backend both` 363/363 (82 seconds), `cli_also_llvm` 185/185 (8 seconds), fuzzer seed 1110 30 programs failures 0 (8 seconds). In the generated code only symbol names and error wording changed and the meaning is the same, so bench performance·the full mutation run·g1 were not run.
- Audit (Claude opus): must-fix 1 — `bench/mutate.py` stripped the extension by taking the first 4 characters, so with `.pruv` the problem number became `p01.` and mutation testing stopped (a path the tests do not cover) → fixed with `os.path.splitext` and confirmed by running p02 alone (7 mutants normal, 122 seconds). Recommendations reflected: reverted the body of the `solve/` input copies, "name at the time" note on BLUEPRINT line 43, code block alignment, supplemented this section's key·preserved-items list.
- Note: the keys in the new result JSON are `pruv_*`, so a script that compares them directly with the old result JSON (`vow`·`vow_*`) needs to align the keys.

## 15. M3 = C4 first implementation batch 0A~0D+T (2026-11-11)
The first batch of design `docs/M3_C4_SPEC.md` §4·5·6·7. The Python reference changed only two lines in 0A. Verified only on this Mac (arm64 10 cores, Homebrew llvm@22 22.1.8, rustc 1.99.1).

| Batch | Deliverable | Verification result |
|---|---|---|
| 0A Determinism·baseline | only `check.py:134` call graph·`verify.py:686` havoc traversal sorted by name (declaration order unchanged), `tests/diff_py_adapter.py` (parse AST v1·check·trace·snapshot·seeds), baseline snapshot `tests/baseline/py_0a/` (220 inputs, manifest `cfbeae2f…`, z3 5.3.3.0) | hash seed 0·1·2·random: before the fix 24 of 220 differed (all SMT order), after the fix 0. Before vs after the fix at seed 0: all 220 diagnostics·verdicts the same, only SMT query order changed in 16. No change in proof time·counterexamples·first path of recursion |
| 0B Locking·z3 prototype | `pruvc-rs/` workspace (pruv-core·pruv-proof·pruvc), `Cargo.lock`, `rust-toolchain.toml` 1.99.1, `ci/m3/` (README·check_deps.sh·compare_spike.py), 6 samples | For 58 obligations (aggregate lemmas·retry, Opt/Res, user struct/enum, havoc, arrays, nested quantifiers+patterns), the first-attempt·retry SMT-LIB text and verdicts all match Python character for character. `cargo build --release --locked` succeeded, `cargo tree -d` no duplicates |
| 0C Object·link tools | `pruvc-ir-tool` (obj·runtime·link·undef), runtime.c 4 variants embedded in build.rs | `run_all --backend llvm --ir-tool` 222/222 (objects made without clang). i128 sample output matches, unresolved helper symbols `__divti3`·`__modti3` are supplied by macOS libSystem. Linking works with Korean·space paths. Object minimum OS fixed at 11.0 |
| 0D Windows sret samples | `tests/abi/` (5 per-target wrapper .ll files, C reference ref.c, Rust extern driver, check_abi.py) | 5/5: COFF x86-64 object (both llc·ir-tool paths), verify, clang C reference signature (sret first argument·align 8·void) matches. **Windows runtime ABI unconfirmed (Windows CI's job)** |
| T Differential harness | `tests/compiler_driver.py`, `tests/diff_compilers.py` (`--replay`), `--compiler py\|rs`·`--compiler-path` on the 5 existing harnesses, run_all `--ir-tool` | py vs py 468 same. Unimplemented responses are not counted as passes and give exit code 3 (0 with `--allow-unimplemented`). Failure case saving·`--replay` reproduction confirmed |

| Dependency (exact lock) | Value |
|---|---|
| z3 / z3-src / z3-sys | `=0.22.0` vendored / `=503.3.0` / `=0.14.1` (runtime version 5.3.3.0) |
| llvm-sys / cc / tempfile / serde_json | `=221.1.2` force-static / `=1.11.1` / `=3.27.0` / `=1.0.154` |
| Build | z3 cold about 280 seconds (measured at the point where the first build ended in a compile error; a cold re-measurement without errors was not done), code-only rebuild a few seconds |
| Size (strip) | pruv-proof-spike 21MB, pruvc-ir-tool 37MB |
| Dynamic dependencies | spike: libc++·libSystem only (check_deps passes). ir-tool: Homebrew `libzstd` → check_deps fails (due to the development LLVM, to be removed in a direct build of the distributed LLVM) |

- Porting pitfalls found in 0B (to observe in the real verifier port): (1) because of Python's reflected comparison rule, z3py emits `x >= IntVal(0)` as `(<= 0 x)` (the reflected method when the right side is a number literal). Comparisons with Python integers (`in_range`, `nv.n >= 0`, etc.) are not flipped → Rust `Ast::ge` follows the reflected rule, `*_i` stays as is. (2) The output mode is `Z3_PRINT_SMTLIB2_COMPLIANT`=2 (the 3 the worker put in was an out-of-range enum transmute and thus UB, and expression output became an empty string so all lemma dedup keys became identical). (3) z3py passes a null symbol for the quantifier qid/skid, but z3-sys 0.14.1 binds this argument as NonNull, so the same C function is declared separately with a null-permitting signature. (4) The z3 default error handler writes to stdout and calls exit(1) → indistinguishable from a language rejection, so it was replaced with an abort handler.
- Fixed in 0C: a typo in an LLVM function name, link failure of `-lSystem` on macOS because the SDK was not passed (`PRUVC_SYSROOT` or `xcrun --show-sdk-path`).
- Verification (last run after the fixes): `run_all --backend both` 363/363, `cli_also_llvm` 186/186 (1 case omitting `--backend` added), fuzzer seed 1111 30 programs failures 0 (13 seconds), mutation testing LLVM 81 (list has 83, 2 duplicates on the same line) verdicts·stages·codes·test results all the same as `mutants8_m0_llvm.json` (287 seconds, result `bench/mutants9_0a_llvm.json`). The fuzzer·mutation runs were before the audit fixes, and the fixes were only reverting mutate's time limit and comments, so they were not re-run.
- Audit (Claude): must-fix 5 — a 300-second limit that appeared in mutate (reverted), mutate's expanded crash definition (kept·recorded), diff_compilers exit code 0 when only unimplemented results exist (→3), the ir-tool host triple is the LLVM default so the minimum OS follows the build machine (→ triple from the target table), check_deps missed the Intel Homebrew path (→ added). Recommendations reflected: z3 error handler, Datatype output array null check, Rust havoc internal sorting, counterexample value differences not counted as diffs, Linux `--sysroot=`, xcrun result cache, temporary folder cleanup.
- Confirmation after the fix (Claude): all 5 must-fix resolved, no new must-fix. Of 4 recommendations, aligned the mutate crash definition wording with the actual definition, and removed `todo` (which also matches paths) from the diff_compilers unimplemented detection (possible false pass under `--allow-unimplemented`). The rest (executable name substitution applied on only one side so a false failure is possible, check_deps corner cases) are left.
- Remaining work: build·i128 helper symbols·static dependency checks on Linux·Windows·macOS x64, Windows runtime ABI (CI), static direct build of the distributed LLVM, connect ir-tool to the bench harness·fuzzer object generation as well, re-measure the z3 cold build time without errors. The next stage is Stage 1 parser port.

## 16. M3 = C4 Stage 1: parser port (2026-11-11)
Design `docs/M3_C4_SPEC.md` §4 Stage 1. Moved `vast.py`·`vparser.py` to `pruvc-rs/crates/pruv-core/src/{ast,diagnostic,lexer,parser}.rs`, and added the test-schema output `dump.rs`, the Python-compatible JSON `pyjson.rs`, the Python `repr()` imitation `pyrepr.rs` (+ generated table `pyrepr_table.rs`, generator `pruvc-rs/tools/gen_pyrepr_table.py`) and the test binary `pruv-parse-dump`. No external crates. The Python reference was not changed. Verified only on this Mac.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `tests/diff_parse.py` (new tool) default inputs | 2261 of 2263 byte-identical, differences 0. must_reject 81·examples 7·runtime 72·bench 10·boundary `tests/parse_edge/` 50·byte boundaries 123·fuzzer seeds 41·56·57×200·variants 1320 |
| Widened inputs (seeds 1~8×500, variants 30) | 10941 of 10943 identical, differences 0 |
| 2 excluded cases | 2000 nested parentheses·3000 unary `-`: Python dies with RecursionError so there is nothing to compare (Rust handles them with a 1GB-stack thread). Known permitted difference |
| Coverage | nodes 33/33, parse error codes 5/5 (bad_char·bad_int·reserved_name·syntax·unsupported_type), 28 error sentence forms. `9223372036854775807`·`-9223372036854775808`·numbers over 20 digits·large negative numbers·`--5` folding are all in the boundary batch |
| Regression suite | `run_all --backend both` 363/363, `cli_also_llvm` 186/186, `cargo test -p pruv-core` unit 13 |

- Python behaviors matched in the port: universal newline of `open().read()` (`\r\n`·lone `\r` → `\n`) applied before the lexer, line/col in code-point units, BOM is `bad_char '﻿'`, integers are sign + normalized decimal string (no digit limit, negative folding at the position of the `-` token), `%r` in bad_char sentences chooses `\x`/`\u`/`\U` using the Python 3.9.6 (Unicode 13.0) `str.isprintable` table, line/col in the error JSON are numbers, integers in the AST are numbers and positions are strings.
- 1 thing found in the differential: the adapter `ser()` has Quant's `kind` field ("forall"·"sum", etc.) overwrite the node name "Quant" (Python dict overwrite). This is behavior already in the baseline snapshot pruv-ast-v1, so the Rust `dump.rs` followed it identically. Fixing the schema requires bumping to v2.
- Invalid UTF-8·read failure: the Python adapter dies with an exception and Rust emits an `{"adapter_error": "decode"|"io"}` line. The comparison tool defines this correspondence. The Python reference reads with the system default encoding (UTF-8 on this Mac), so Windows reference output was not checked.
- Audit (Claude Opus): no byte-difference defects (the worker cross-checked 241 boundary·3000 variant·1500 generated·18 large CRLF/CR·all code points of the repr table). Must-fix 1 — `peek`/`next` cloned the token every time (linear but a constant-factor waste; audit measured 0.92 seconds for 2 million statements, 27.6 seconds in Python for the same input) → `peek` returns a reference, `expect` only consumes (clone only at the 4 places that need the token), and discarding consumption uses `bump`. Recommendations reflected: removed temporary strings in operator matching, cleaned up unused functions·stale comments, `pruv-parse-dump` writes immediately line by line so a stack overflow in one file does not lose earlier lines. Recommendations left: no depth limit in the public `parse` (the caller must reserve a large stack, to be decided in the Stage I CLI), newline normalization·`Vec<char>` memory about 6x, error type size (no `Box`), JSON tree construction in the dump.
- Confirmation after the fix (Claude Opus): passed. The 4 `expect_tok` places match the 4 places in Python that use the return value of `expect`, and consumption order·error positions·operator precedence·no new panics were cross-checked line by line against the original. After the fix `cargo test` 13 passed, `diff_parse` 2263·10943 cases differences 0 (main run).

## 17. M3 = C4 Stage 2: checker port (2026-11-11)
Design §4 Stage 2. Moved `typeinfo.py`·`check.py` to `pruvc-rs/crates/pruv-core/src/{typeinfo,check}.rs` (no external crates), and added the test binary `pruv-check-dump` (the same typed AST + type_table JSON as the adapter `check`). Added `--mode check` to `tests/diff_parse.py` and boundary inputs `tests/check_edge/` (77 errors·12 successes). The Python reference was not changed. Verified only on this Mac.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `diff_parse.py --mode check` default inputs | 2834 of 2836 byte-identical, differences 0 (2 excluded are the same deep-nesting Python RecursionError as Stage 1). type_table the same for all 964 successes (68 with 2 or more types) |
| Widened inputs (seeds 1~8×500, variants 30) | 13650 of 13652 identical, differences 0, 5168 successes |
| Coverage | check error codes 45/45 (excluding unreachable `internal`), both stages type·effect, ty 6 built-ins + 24 user types |
| Regression suite | `run_all --backend both` 363/363, `cli_also_llvm` 186/186, `diff_parse` parse mode 2263 differences 0, `cargo test -p pruv-core` 20 |

- Audit (Claude Opus): no byte-difference defects (about 110 inputs including all previously uncross-checked code paths were fed to both sides and compared). Must-fix 2 — the enum variant·field name duplicate check was `Vec::contains` so O(n²) (100,000 fields: Python 0.92 seconds, Rust 9.2 seconds) → changed to `HashSet`, with 100,000 fields + 100,000 variants input in 0.16 seconds. Recommendations left: scope cloning per block (same order as Python `dict(scope)` but a String allocation per item), even the 1GB stack overflows on an addition chain of 1 million terms (Python gives RecursionError at 400 terms, so outside the comparison range), type-string allocation per expression, double copy of TypeDecl.
- Confirmation after the fix (Claude Opus): the 2 fixes passed with the same behavior as Python (name check → duplicate check → insertion, same error position·sentence). The same shape of list-containment check remains in match arms (`need`·`seen`), struct literals (`declared`·`given`), and type dependencies (`deps`), but Python also uses a list at the same places so the order is the same, and it is left as a recommendation (the duplicate name in a struct literal is "the first among names that appear two or more times", so a count dictionary must be used when fixing it to keep the same result).

## 18. M3 = C4 Stage 3: verifier port (2026-11-11)
Design §4 Stage 3. All of `verify.py` (Model, state·arrays, fresh·wf·rebuild, prove·retry·seen, cex·fmt_val, ev·dom·aggregate lemmas·call·ensures, exec·havoc·loop·run, verify_program) was moved onto the 0B prototype as `pruvc-rs/crates/pruv-proof/src/{lib,model,obligation,symbolic,sys}.rs`. `pruv-proof` uses `pruv-core` as a path dependency (no external dependencies added). Test binary `pruv-trace-dump` (the same JSON as the adapter `trace`), differential tool `tests/diff_trace.py` (the two implementations as fresh processes per input). There is no public CLI yet (Stage I). The Python reference was not changed. Verified only on this Mac.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `diff_trace.py` (including seeds 41·56·57·1·2 × 150) | 932 cases: SMT-LIB text·verdicts·errors (code·line·col·message·fix) all the same. 849 cases byte-identical, 83 cases differ only in counterexample values |
| Obligation scale (reference basis) | obligations 9649 (skipped 371), attempts 9578, lemma retries 300, sat 1466·unsat 8112·unknown 0 |
| Counterexamples | all 148 differing counterexamples (within the 83 cases) have the same keys·shape (scalar/array/struct names). Only values differ |
| Prototype cross-check `ci/m3/compare_spike.py` | 58/58 obligations same SMT·verdicts, z3 5.3.3.0 same in both implementations |
| Regression suite | `run_all --backend both` 363/363, `cli_also_llvm` 186/186, `cargo build --release --locked` (workspace), `cargo tree -d` no duplicates, `cargo test -p pruv-proof` passed |

- Found and fixed in the differential: the z3py reflected comparison rule, which had looked only at number literals (ArithRef⊃IntNumRef), was widened to the class hierarchy. `BoolVal(False) == ForAll(..)` has the right side as QuantifierRef, a subclass of BoolRef, so the reflected method is called first and gives `(= forall false)` (bench p03). Also fixed 1 compile error (a `Sat` enum reference) in the worker's code.
- Counterexample value differences: the SMT text is the same but the model z3 picks differs. Switching the whole program to a single context made no difference so it was reverted (a non-rc context per function, discarded wholesale when the function ends). The cause is presumed to be the difference from z3py's rc context (AST number reuse) but was not confirmed. Per design §5, counterexamples are not required to be byte-identical. Since they are models of the same query, I consider them valid per query, but there is not yet a mechanism that re-evaluates the violation with an independent oracle (the milestone gate's job).
- Audit (Claude Opus): counterexamples (Opt/Res/user types/first 8 of arrays/havoc locals)·`verify_unknown`·relaxed `_note`·reflected comparison (ne·Datatype·Option·Result·quantified expressions)·13 pairs of the FIX table were cross-checked with 9 batches of small inputs for which the model is uniquely determined, byte-identical down to counterexample strings. Must-fix 1 — `pruv-trace-dump` gave `adapter_error` on read·UTF-8 decode failure, which differed from the Python adapter (internal_error) → fixed to give the same internal_error with `pruv-core/src/pyio.rs` (reproducing Python `UnicodeDecodeError`·`OSError` sentences, 7 samples cross-checked). Recommendations reflected: panics in the dump binary give exit 3, a Python recursion limit category (py_recursion) in `diff_trace`. Recommendations left: z3 error handler abort (no path reaches it from inputs that pass the checker), unreachable internal failure wording, `Env` linear search and env cloning per statement (constant factor in path-explosion functions), `MODEL_SEQ` thread_local, non-rc context memory.

## 19. M3 = C4 Stage 4: LLVM output·common function port (2026-11-11)
Design §4 Stage 4. Moved `llvmgen.py` (Mod·FnGen·entry_of·cdivrem·gen_llvm) to `pruv-core/src/llvmgen.rs`, the C-ABI wrapper (Words·abi_of) to `abi.rs`, the Rust bindings (gen_bindings) to `bindings.rs`, and the common functions of `codegen.py` (rty·cty·conv·emit_public_types·show·match_sorted_requires·rstr) to `emit_common.rs` (2875 lines total, no external crates). Test binary `pruv-llvm-dump`, differential tool `tests/diff_llvm.py`. Direct emission of per-target Windows wrappers is Stage 6's job so it does not exist yet (for now the same existing IR as Python). Verified only on this Mac.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `diff_llvm.py` default (inputs 1636 × modes 4 + budget 4) | all 6548 cases IR·bindings byte-identical (successes 3320, language errors 3228, internal 0) |
| Widened inputs (seeds 1~8×500, variants 10) | 6849 inputs, all 27400 cases identical (successes 17532) |
| Real CLI cross-check | the .ll·.rs written by `pruvc.py --backend llvm` are byte-identical to bench p03 |
| Coverage | 28 LLVM instruction kinds, wrapper 4244, nsw (proof mode) 1190 cases, with.overflow (safe mode) 1658, user types 488, i128 cdiv 1248, requires checks 3320 |
| Regression suite | `run_all --backend both` 363/363, `cli_also_llvm` 186/186, `cargo test -p pruv-core` 32, workspace `--locked` build |

- The worker code was written concurrently in three parts (body / bindings·common functions / differential tool), and from the first build there were no compile errors and everything matched on the first cross-check.
- Audit (Claude Opus): no output differing from Python on inputs that pass the checker (directly cross-checked empty struct, enum with only fieldless variants, Option/Result inside nested user types, sorted-pattern variants, i64·i128 boundaries × 7 budgets, and the input decoding path). Must-fix 2 — the struct-literal value collection (`mk_struct`) and the user enum match binder (`user_match`'s retain) were quadratic compared with Python's linear (32000 fields 2.14 seconds) → fixed with a hash map·retain removal, the same input in 0.72 seconds (mostly checker time), after the fix `diff_llvm` 6548 cases differences 0. Recommendations left: internal-error wording for paths the checker blocks so they are not reached (KeyError·`%r` imitation), `cty`/`conv` unwrap (callers are tests only), duplicate `words` implementation, cloning of variable lookups·string constants, string copying of deep chains in contract wording `show` (same order as Python), the test binary `--budget` has no range check (the CLI does).
- Confirmation after the fix (Claude Opus): passed. Struct literals are evaluated in source order with later values winning for the same name, output is in declared field order (hash map iteration does not enter the output), and match binders are inserted in sequence by `run_block` with later values winning, the same as Python `dict.update`.

## 20. M3 = C4 Stage 5: Rust implementation `pruvc` command and standalone executable (2026-11-11)
Attached the CLI part of Stage I (`pruvc/src/{main,cli,driver}.rs`) ahead of schedule to design §4 Stage 5 (object·link integration), because the command must exist first to test the executable. It adds `--emit exe|obj|ll|rs`·`--target`·`-mcpu=` from design §3 to the same options·output·exit codes as Python `pruvc.py`. `--emit exe` checks the entry point (`fn main() -> ()`, no parameters), attaches a C `main` shim to the same module, builds an object with the LLVM library, and then links it with the built-in malloc runtime and the system linker (no clang for IR compilation). `--backend rust` (Rust source output) is Stage 7 so it is still exit 2 (unimplemented). The 4-platform CI·distribution LLVM static build are not yet (Stage I). Verified only on this Mac.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `diff_compilers.py --backend llvm` (both CLIs as fresh processes per input) | all 234 cases same (exit code·stdout JSON·.ll·bindings bytes) |
| `run_all.py --compiler rs --backend llvm` | 222/222 (the .ll written by the Rust implementation turned into objects and run with the Rust driver) |
| `tests/standalone/run.py` (new: 29 tests, 50 cases) | all same. Compares exit code·stdout·`pruv:` lines of the Rust implementation standalone executable against the reference (Python implementation → Rust source → rustc). Proof·safe·trust·budget modes, overflow·division by 0·out of range·negative alloc·requires violation (exit 101), 300,000-iteration allocation loop, space·Korean paths, entry-point error 2, rejection 2 |
| Compile time (bench 10 + examples 7, `--backend llvm`, including z3) | Python 1.63 seconds, Rust 0.88 seconds |
| Executable | `pruvc` 58MB (strip), Homebrew `libzstd` in dynamic dependencies (due to the development LLVM, to be removed in a direct build of the distributed LLVM — same as the 0B record) |

- Of the 29 test programs, 3 (s03·s09·s11) used computations the worker could not prove, so verification failed in the Python implementation too. I fixed the range conditions·output expressions to make them as intended.
- `pruv-core/src/pyio.rs` is also used for CLI input reading so read·decode failure sentences are the same as in the Python implementation.
- Audit (Claude Opus): must-fix 3 — (1) the internal sentence for output file write·copy failures differed from Python → `[Errno 2] No such file or directory: '…'` via `pyio::io_error_message` (same in both implementations confirmed). (2) On deep inputs such as 300,000 nested parentheses the 1GB stack overflowed and it aborted (134) without JSON → put an AST depth limit of 50,000 in the parser (counting recursion together with left-accumulating binary·postfix chains), and when exceeded it gives internal_error `maximum recursion depth exceeded` like Python RecursionError, exit 3. 40,000 nested parentheses·45,000 addition terms pass, 300,000·3,000,000 nesting and 1 million terms confirmed exit 3. Python stops at about 1000 levels so this is outside the comparison range, and `diff_parse` 2263·2836 cases remain with differences 0. (3) The stdout usage line of usage errors differed from Python because of the new options → stdout is the same line as Python (only the executable name differs), the new option line goes to stderr. Recommendation reflected: `link_failed` diagnostics summarize the linker's `error`/`ld:` lines. Confirmation after the fix (Claude Opus): results below the limit·error positions unchanged, 49,990 nested parentheses pass the LLVM path including verification. 1 problem — the `else if` chain recursion was outside the depth count, so 1 million overflowed the stack (exit 134) → fixed to count that recursion too, exit 3 confirmed. Recommendations left: argparse tokens containing spaces·Unicode digits·`--t` abbreviation ambiguity (because of the new `--target`), no per-target validation of `-mcpu` (continues after an LLVM warning), atomic writing of obj/exe output, println panic on closed stdout, macOS minimum-OS warning for the runtime object, rejecting non-UTF-8 argv, the `-o`-omitted sentence for inputs without an extension.

## 21. M3 = C4 Stage 6: Windows x64 per-target wrapper direct output (2026-11-11)
Design §7 "Windows x64 per-target wrapper". Added `AbiTarget{SysV, WinX64}` to `pruv-core/src/abi.rs` and `gen_llvm_for` to `llvmgen.rs`. For the Windows x64 target, only the external `pruv_abi_*` that return Option/Result are emitted from the start as `void` + a first hidden `ptr sret({ i64, i64 }) align 8` (zext the tag of the entry result and store it in two slots), and the datalayout·triple are attached at the very top of the IR. Array returns·user-type out pointers, Option/Result argument expansion, and internal body/entry are unchanged. The Rust bindings are target-independent (an extern returning the value `-> Pruv__Tagged`; the MSVC C ABI creates the hidden argument). The default (macOS·Linux) output is unchanged. `pruvc --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` uses this path.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `tests/abi/check_generated.py` (new, 8 sources) | all passed: two header lines, `opt -passes=verify`, wrapper shape (only Option/Result have one sret, the rest have no sret, matches the binding `Pruv__Tagged` verdict), `--emit obj` COFF x86-64·`pruv_abi_*` defined symbols, host output of the same source has no triple·sret, same as the C 2×int64 return signature clang makes from `ref.c` (0·1·5 arguments) |
| 0D sample `check_abi.py` | 5/5 passed |
| Default output unchanged | `diff_llvm` 6548, `diff_compilers --backend llvm` 234 all byte-identical to Python, `cargo test -p pruv-core` 36 |

- Audit (Claude Opus): all function boundaries in the design table are correct (even the mixed-argument `g(c: C, a: [i64], o: Option<i64>, b: bool) -> Option<i64>` has a single sret first), and the datalayout is the same as clang 22's value for x86_64-pc-windows-msvc. Must-fix 1 — a code path where, on a Windows host, `--emit ll` without `--target` would produce the SysV shape (target None → SysV) → fixed so that without `--target` the ABI is chosen from the host target (Python byte cross-check on non-Windows hosts unchanged). Recommendations left: the development tool `abi_lower`'s `--input-abi target` does not check signatures (to be removed after the Python cross-check), a test comparing the datalayout constant with the TargetMachine value, widening the samples (enum return·2-argument C reference), measuring rustc MSVC extern IR (this Mac has no Windows rust-std — judged from the Win64 C ABI rules).
- **Whether it actually meshes when run on Windows cannot be confirmed on this Mac.** Linking with a Rust extern call·C caller and running Some/None·MIN/MAX·0·1·5 arguments·repeated calls·Option inside a struct·array return is the job of Windows CI (Stage I).

## 25. Finishing Stage 0: large-scale random-program fuzzing (2026-11-11)
Question: **does a program that passes the proof break its promise at execution?** We ran `bench/fuzz/run.py` (independent oracle = arbitrary-precision interpreter, Rust·LLVM × proof·4 safe modes) with the canonical Rust `pruvc`, seeds 301~320, 600 proof-passing programs per seed and 12 inputs per program (`--programs 600 --max-candidates 3000 --inputs 12`).

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Candidate programs generated | 22,283 (generator errors 0, oracle-unsupported 0) |
| Verifier verdicts | passed 12,185 (of which 12,000 built·executed) / rejected 10,098 |
| Inputs executed / process executions | 150,030 / 585,720 |
| **Proof passed + inputs satisfying requires** | **all 114,204 cases gave the same normal result as the oracle (promise violations 0)** |
| Proof passed + inputs violating requires·undefined | 28,626 cases (caught by the public boundary check, exit 101, same as the oracle) |
| Rejected programs (sample 600, safe mode) + inputs satisfying requires | inputs that actually blow up: division by zero 1,003·out of range 389·overflow 105·internal requires 94 / normal 4,039 |
| Failures (unsound·mode_mismatch·oracle_mismatch·build·timeout, etc.) | **0 in all 20 seeds** |
| Not comparable because of oracle limit | 0 |
| Programs with no input satisfying requires | 68 (mostly strong requires combinations, tested only with violating inputs) |
| Elapsed time | 2,037 s (92~113 s per seed) |

- Combining the earlier runs of the same day, seeds 201~205×400 (§24, proof passed 2,051·failures 0), with the seed 1·10·41·51~57·1110·1111 records during M0~M3 (§10~12·15), this fuzzer has found no promise violation in more than 16 thousand proof-passing programs (14,051 from just today's two batches).
- The results on the rejected-program side show the verifier is not rejecting arbitrarily: runtime errors matching the rejection reason occurred on real inputs 1,591 times. The 4,039 cases that ended normally are the verifier's conservatism (correct but proof failed) and are the target of Stage 1 (refinement types·invariant inference).
- Limits: syntax outside the generator's ingredients (the generator section of `bench/fuzz/README.md`) — nested loops deeper than 2, recursion, large arrays — is not covered by this fuzzing. The inputs are 12 near-boundary ones. The runtime-check i128 overflow remains as it was, left as a resource limit (M0_CONTRACTS).
- Compiler code was not changed, so no code audit was done for this batch (measurement only).
- Conclusion: **Stage 0 (verifier trust) is closed.** Independent audit·follow-up audit·rejection regression (must_reject)·safe mode·contract domain·execution budget·large-scale fuzzing are all done.

## 26. Stage 1 A: refinement types (2026-11-11)
Design `docs/STAGE1_SPEC.md` (design delegation → 15 design debate items applied → §0 finalized). Stage 1 is split into A refinement types → B template Houdini invariant inference (`--infer`) → C interval analysis (decided after measuring B), and each batch finishes Python implementation → Rust implementation → cross-check in one go.

1. **Syntax** (SPEC 7a): only in parameter·return·`let` type positions, `x: i64 | 0..=100`, `a: [i64 | -1_000_000_000..=1_000_000_000; <=1_000_000]` (value range uses closed `..=`, length uses `<=N` — distinguished in notation from the half-open `forall k in 0..n`). Bounds are decimal integer literals only (`1e9`·`0x10`·expressions give `parse/refinement_syntax`), inside fields·payloads·Option gives `parse/refinement_position`, `bool` etc. give `type/refinement_base`, reversed·outside i64·`N>2^40` give `type/refinement_bounds`.
2. **Semantics = unfold into existing contracts** (`pruvc/refine.py`, `pruv-core/src/refine.rs`): parameter → synthesized `requires` (length → element `forall` → user requires, in that order; runtime checks and sharing of the 2^30 budget go through the existing path as is), return → synthesized `ensures`, local → synthesized `assert` after declaration·reassignment (`verify/refinement_init`·`refinement_assign`), `while` that changes a refined local → mandatory invariant (both entry and preservation are proved). An element store has only the stored value as its range obligation. Synthesized bound variable names do not collide with identifiers inside the function (`k`, `k_1`, …). Types·ABI·generators are unchanged. Safe mode does not runtime-check local·return refinements (stated in the documentation).
3. **Porting to the Rust implementation**: no compile errors on the first build. One dump field name collision (`kind` overwrote the node name) was changed to `shape` to align the two implementations.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `run_all --backend both` Rust implementation / Python implementation | **444/444 / 444/444** (existing 363 + 27 refinement rejection tests·12 execution tests × backends·modes) |
| `cli_also_llvm` | 213/213 |
| `tests/m3_all.py` | all 8 stages same (parse 2445 inputs·check·trace·llvm·rust·compilers 572·standalone 50) |
| Fuzzer seed 41 × 200 (Rust implementation) | proof passed 209, failures 0 |
| bench usability: `bench/solutions/pruv_refine`, with `pruv_agg`'s input conditions moved into refinement types | 5 of 10 converted, signature+requires characters 1,043 → 714 (−32%), 10/10 proof·correctness tests pass, the `--trust-callers` LLVM IR is byte-identical to the original (with checks on, the check content is the same and only the order·message characters differ) |

- Audit (Claude Opus): in 20-odd cases entered directly into both implementations (reassignment·element store·`copy`/`alloc` reassignment·if·match·return after assignment in a loop·nested while·sibling blocks·old length after havoc·name capture·integer bounds·execution modes) **soundness holes 0**, both implementations gave the same verdict. Required 1 — every element store accumulated a whole-array `forall` synthesized assert on the path, so 200 stores took 18.1 s (0.13 s without refinement) → changed to attach the element range to the store node and prove only the already-evaluated stored value once: **0.18 s** (Python implementation 0.30 s). Also fixed: no bogus cascading errors after an out-of-range store (exactly 1 error), the Rust double-unfolding marker, diagnostics for expression after bound·`[i64; 3]`·`0x10`, and the audit's manual cases turned into 11 regression tests + a performance sample (`refine_many_stores`, 62 straight-line stores). Remaining recommendations: synthesized requires/ensures/invariant messages have no provenance phrase (in B), the runtime check reads the element twice, an always-true full-i64-range check is also generated, refinement error positions are the annotation position (only element stores use the store statement position).
- Follow-up audit (Claude Opus): required 0. Confirmed in both implementations with loop·branch·call·nonlinear-store cases (trace sha1 same) that it is sound even though the stored value is only proved and not assumed on the path, because the same path condition implies the range; the new rejection tests are rejected with the intended code·line. 2 recommendations applied: added the execution test `refine_loop_store`, in which the refined invariant·sum after a store inside a loop·branch is proved, and added the `bench/solutions/pruv_refine` group to the `diff_trace` cross-check (trace·llvm·rust·compilers all same again, compilers 576).

## 27. Stage 1 B1: solver query-recording boundary·cross-checker fixes (2026-11-11)
First work of batch B (template Houdini invariant inference) (STAGE1_SPEC §0 items 11·13). The inference itself does not exist yet.

1. **Query-recording boundary**: every z3 query of both verifiers passes through one place (`run` inside `prove` in `verify.py`, `run_attempt` in `obligation.rs`), and its settings are received as `QueryConfig` (stage·round·timeout·rlimit·seed). For each attempt it records the verdict and status (`proved / not_proved / resource_unknown / timeout / error`), the actual timeout·rlimit·seed, and `reason_unknown`. The test adapters now record through this official boundary (`verify.TraceRecorder`) instead of monkey-patching `z3.Solver`. Trace schema `pruv-trace-v2` (obligations get `phase/round/cand`, attempts get the values above, top-level `options`).
2. **Cross-checker** (`tests/diff_trace.py`): "the two implementations are the same" and "verification completed" are counted separately (the `verified` column). Even when bytes are identical, if unknown·`verify_unknown`·`inference_timeout` is involved it is `same_unknown` (not counted as success; `inference_timeout` is a failure). Differences resolved by retry are reported with the first result kept. It checks that an option passed like `--flags=--infer` is actually reflected in the `options` of both implementations' output (`option_mismatch` is a failure). The adapter·`pruv-trace-dump` exit with code 2 on an unknown option (previously the adapter ignored it).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| SMT unchanged on the path without `--infer`: per-obligation SMT text·verdicts·errors of the 231 corpus files compared with HEAD (d2c649e) | **byte-identical** |
| `tests/m3_all.py` | all 8 stages ok, trace 351 inputs same 333·cex_only 18·same_unknown 0, verified 164 |
| `run_all --backend both` Rust implementation / Python implementation | 444/444 / 444/444 |
| `cli_also_llvm` | 213/213 |
| `--flags=--infer` (not yet implemented) | both implementations reject it as an unknown option → caught as a cross-check failure (as intended) |

- The audit is done once for the whole B batch (up to B2 inference) + one follow-up.

## 28. Stage 1 B2: Python implementation template Houdini invariant inference `--infer` (2026-11-11)
Implementation spec `docs/STAGE1_B_INFER.md` (finalized by the main session, fixed so that the two implementations produce the same candidates·order·SMT). New `pruvc/infer.py`, hooked into `verify.py` (`extra`·`probe`), `--infer`·`--show-inferred` in `pruvc.py`, test adapter `--infer` (records the search queries and `inference` in the trace). Porting to the Rust implementation is B3.

- Candidates: for each loop, 11 templates (counter lower·upper bound, array length preservation, element range, prefix sum/count/max/min, forall/exists of max/min, accumulator range) are built from the code shape. They do not rely on variable names.
- Houdini: in each round, rerun the whole function with a fresh verification state and ask only the candidate obligations (500ms·rlimit 100,000·seed 0), and remove the failed candidates all at once at the end of the round. The remaining candidates are attached and the whole is finally verified in a fresh state (8,000ms). The inference result is not trusted; the final verification proves it.

| Measurement (Python implementation) | Result |
|---|---|

The runner also checks the round in which candidates are dropped.
- Time: the samples above are under 1 second per function (precise measurement in B3, with both implementations together).
- The audit is done once at the end of the B batch (after B3) + one follow-up.

- **Goal reached**: p01·p07·p09, with all 9 invariant clauses deleted, are proved with `--infer` by both implementations (§0 item 14 minimum goal). p02 as a bonus. No inference is 0/10, templates alone 4/10. Budget discards (truncation) 0, budget exhaustion 0 (on these inputs), timeouts 0.
- Not measured: generation·symbolic execution·final verification times could not be separated (only the off/on difference of whole compile time). Times on a quiet machine.
- **Failure cause diagnosis**: p10 lacks a simple value range like `x >= 0 && y >= 0` (the kind interval analysis could provide); p03 (sorted-prefix `forall`), p04 (lo/hi interval relation), p05·p08 (output-array prefix relation), p06 (partial-sum forall/exists) need relational·quantified invariants.
- **Audit (Claude Opus)**: required 2·recommendations 7. Required 1 is a **soundness hole** — if a user function call is in an accumulator term or max comparison, that call enters a candidate, and since a call in contract context assumes the callee's `ensures` without checking `requires`, a false postcondition passed with `--infer` (2 reproductions, both implementations). → candidates containing calls other than `len` are discarded before sorting (same rule in both implementations, recorded in the spec), and the reproductions became the rejection samples `inf_rej_call_in_term`·`inf_rej_call_in_max`. Required 2 — the cross-checker counted unknowns·inference timeouts in inputs differing only in counterexamples as success → made `same_unknown`. Recommendations applied: the Rust CLI does not print the record on an internal error, `pruv-trace-dump` has the same option set as the adapter (`options.no_verify`), removed copying of the loop candidate list·immediate return after abort, deleted an unused function, wording of §0 item 7. Remaining recommendation: the design where a 500ms timeout of a single candidate query makes the whole function `inference_timeout` (a measurement target, 0 occurrences in this measurement).
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus)**: required 0. Confirmed in code that the call filtering applies to every candidate path (a single point) and that in contract context the only node adding an assumption without `requires` is the call; on a call-in-condition case and a budget-exhaustion case (exactly 512 queries), the two implementations' outputs are identical. 3 recommendations applied: turned off adapter option abbreviations (same in both implementations), budget-exhaustion test `inf_budget`, table header spacing.

**C (interval analysis) decision: not starting now (deferred).** Rationale: of the 6 remaining failures with templates alone, the only one that non-relational interval analysis can directly solve appears to be p10 (value range), and the other 5 need relational·quantified invariants (this is the measurement result of the STAGE1_SPEC §6 open problem "after measuring whether the limits of non-relational interval analysis are the main cause of actual failures"). The next candidates are, rather than interval analysis, (1) adding value-range templates (`x >= 0` kinds from initial values·updates), (2) relational templates such as sorted·output-array prefix. Recorded in STAGE1_SPEC §0.


## 30. Stage 1 B+: extending inference templates (value range·output-array prefix·early-return prefix) (2026-11-11)
Added three kinds to the `--infer` candidate templates (spec `docs/STAGE1_B_INFER.md` 3.7, inference configuration version 2). All are built from code structure only, do not rely on names, and pass through the same `len`-only call filtering (single point). Added in the order Python implementation (`pruvc/infer.py`) → Rust implementation (`pruv-proof/src/infer.rs`).

| Kind | Candidate | Target input |
|---|---|---|
| 12 value range | start-value range of a non-counter scalar v that only receives assignments in the loop: literal, parameter lower·upper bound in `requires` (`>`·`<` give ±1), array element with an element range → `v >= L`, `v <= U` | p10 gcd |
| 13 output-array prefix | `b[v] = E` before a counter with step 1 (b starts with `alloc`/`copy` and is written only by this store) → `forall k in lo..v: b[k] == E[v:=k]` | p08 reverse |
| 14 early-return prefix | `if C { return R; }` (no else) before a counter with step 1 → `forall k in lo..v: !C[v:=k]` | p03 sortedness check |
