- **Test tool**: added the expectation keys `absent` (candidates that must not appear in the report) and `fallback` to `tests/inference/run.py`.
- **Audit (Claude Opus)**: required 1·recommendations 4. Required — a chain in which kind-12 candidates support each other (`a0 = a1; …; a19 = i + 1`) makes Houdini drop only one per round and exhaust the query budget of 512, and on exhaustion all candidates are discarded, so even `0 <= i`·`i <= n`, which remained in version 1, are lost and the ensures is rejected (reproduced in both implementations). → **version-1 fallback** (spec 4.1): if the budget is exhausted and the initial list had kinds 12 or higher, search again with only kinds 1~11, with a new budget·the same deadline·continuing round numbers, and the report carries `"fallback": "v1"`. Reproduction is the sample `inf_budget_fallback` (both implementations queries 512+6, verified). Recommendations applied: spec cost explanation (the query count grows as rounds × remaining candidates), restricted `strip_reason` to the two rlimit wordings, test reinforcement (strict inequalities·`-literal`·nested `&&`, `copy` start, not-generated form). Remaining recommendation: the 3.1 counter determination exists twice per implementation (the existing one for 1~11·the new one for 12~14, currently the same) — not merged so as not to touch the existing generation path.
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus)**: required 1·recommendations 2. The fallback is equivalent across the two implementations·matches the spec·first-search bytes unchanged·deadline maintained (code cross-check and direct execution). Required — the `absent` check did not compare with the `invariant ` prefix of the report so it could not catch surviving candidates → fixed, and confirmed that the check fails when a candidate that deliberately survives is inserted. Recommendations applied: the meaning of absent in `inf_budget_fallback` (based on the fallback result)·the limits of `absent` in the README, and the spec states that the fallback's "does not get worse" holds only for the query budget and not for time.

**Turning `--infer` on by default: keep it off** (STAGE1_SPEC §0 item 17). Turning it on makes compile time 1.3~8×, trace 1.5~19×, and 3/10 still fail even with it on. Timeouts and fallback time limits on realistically sized inputs have not been measured yet.

## 31. Finishing Stage 1 (soundness regression measurement) and G2 design (2026-11-11)
### 31.1 `--infer` soundness regression: new passes of wrong mutants 0
We measured the last indicator of STAGE1_SPEC 5.5, "soundness regression (number of new passes of wrong mutants)". The new tool `bench/inference/mutate_infer.py` takes the mutation rules of `bench/mutate.py` (OPS·SKIP·`mutants`) directly from the source to make the same mutants, runs compilation (verification + code generation) four times (Python implementation·Rust implementation × `--infer` off·on), and compares the verdicts. Verdicts: pass = exit 0, rejected = exit 1 + error JSON (stage≠internal), **undecided** = exit 1 + `inference_timeout`·`verify_unknown`, crash = anything else (exit 2·3, exit 1 without JSON, timeout). New pass = not a pass with off but a pass with on. Undecided ones are rerun with `--retry` with parallelism 1. The backend is a stage after verification and does not affect verdicts, so only the rust backend is used (we first ran with llvm too and saw the same verdicts, but that is not independent evidence).

Command: `mutate_infer.py OUT bench/solutions/pruv_try1 bench/solutions/pruv_agg bench/inference/agg_noinv bench/g1/solutions/ref_pruv --jobs 8 --retry` (HEAD 8af89bf, 2 min 6 s). Summary in `bench/inference/mutate_infer_31.json`.

| Implementation | Off: pass/rejected/undecided/crash | On: pass/rejected/undecided/crash | New passes | Lost passes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Python | 20 / 278 / 0 / 0 | 21 / 269 / **8** / 0 | 1 | 0 |
| Rust | 20 / 278 / 0 / 0 | 21 / 272 / **5** / 0 | 1 | 0 |

- **The 1 new pass is a mutant that is originally true.** `if a[i] > m` → `if a[i] >= m` in `agg_noinv` p02 (max). It merely picks the same value again and the result is the same. The same mutant of `pruv_agg` p02, which has human-written invariants, passes even without inference, and in the earlier record `bench/mutants9_0a_llvm.json` it also passes·tests pass. Therefore **new passes of wrong mutants are 0** (both implementations). The true/false mutant judgments were made by a human.
- **The 13 undecided (Python 8·Rust 5) are not passes** (exit 1, compile failure). They stayed the same in the parallelism-1 retry. So "new passes 0" is the result of counting these 13 as non-passes.
- **Newly found difference between the two implementations (to fix):** all the undecided are `inference_timeout` occurring in about 0.1 s and reproduce deterministically. The mutants that get caught differ between the two implementations, so the `--infer` verdict diverges in 7 cases (one side undecided·the other rejected, neither a pass). In one case (`pruv_try1` p09 line 8), one search query in the Python trace was `unknown` (reason `canceled`, much shorter than 500ms → appears to be rlimit), and `query_status` classified `canceled` as `timeout`, which stopped the whole Houdini. Rust gave an answer at the same spot. This is behavior per the spec (`docs/STAGE1_B_INFER.md`: query timeout → whole-function `inference_timeout`), and the difference between the two implementations comes from the same rlimit exhaustion splitting, depending on the z3 wording, into `canceled` (→timeout, inference aborted) versus `push canceled`·`max. resource limit exceeded` (→resource_unknown, candidate dropped) (the same root as the 2 same_unknown cases in §30). A single query's `unknown` should only discard that candidate (sound), and the problem is that it stops the inference of the whole function. Supporting material: the per-item record (seconds·code·first attempt) of the undecided·verdict-differing mutants is in `bench/inference/mutate_infer_31.json`. Trace confirmation was done only for the one case of p09 line 8. **To be fixed as the first task of the next batch** (→ fixed in §32: rlimit exhaustion drops the candidate, only wall-clock `timeout` aborts the function) (an unknown from a search query drops the candidate, and aborting the function happens only at the 5 s wall-clock deadline — spec STAGE1_B_INFER update, both implementations, `--infer` regression·cross-check again).
- Baseline check: for the 83 mutants of `pruv_agg`, the no-inference pass/rejected results are all the same as the earlier record `mutants9_0a_llvm.json` (83).
- Limits: the mutants are only the 15 operator forms of `mutate.py` and do not change invariant·contract lines. Execution tests of the mutated programs were not done by this tool.
- Follow-up audit (Claude Opus): of the earlier 7 required items, 6 resolved, and 1 required item, the G2 sample count error (24 → 28, runtime 10 → 11), was fixed. Recommendations applied: undecided verdict only when all errors are undecided codes, record the z3 versions (Python·`z3-src` crate)·whether Cargo.lock was modified, save material for undecided items, refine the cause explanation, G2 decision 4 enumeration·decision 16 diagnostic names·A6 end-criteria supplement.
- Fixed 2 required items from the audit (Claude Opus): exit 2·exit 1 without JSON had been counted as rejected (→ crash; the tool fails if there is a crash), `inference_timeout` had been counted as rejected (→ undecided, retried·aggregated separately). Recommendations applied: record the compiler implementation (git HEAD·whether the source was modified·Rust binary sha256), clean up temporary folders, record task exceptions as crash, reject input-folder name collisions, correct the wording "verification only"·"both backends", store the command·summary in the repository.

**Stage 1 complete.** Refinement types (A), template Houdini `--infer` (B·B+, pruv_agg with invariants deleted 7/10) are done and all the 5.5 indicators were measured. Interval analysis (C) is deferred, and turning `--infer` on by default stays off.

### 31.2 G2 design finalized (`docs/G2_SPEC.md`)
Made in the order survey → draft → one design debate → main finalization → audit (implementation starts from the next batch, no measured values).

- **Survey (Codex):** currently the integer is the single `i64`, arrays are only `[i64]` (the parser accepts only `i64` after `[`), there are no string·character tokens (a quotation mark gives `bad_char`). The proof uses `Array(Int,Int)`+length, Rust output `&[i64]`/`Vec<i64>`, LLVM `{ptr,i64}`·C runtime element fixed at 8 bytes, and `--rust-bindings` is also `*const i64`.
- **Draft (Codex):** do not add scalar integer types, only open the array element storage type. Reading a narrow element gives `i64`, and writing proves `elem_range`. `str` = a read-only byte sequence guaranteed to be valid UTF-8, with a bounds-proved `substr` (copy). The `[u8]→str` conversion is postponed because there is no `Option<str>`.
- **Design debate (Codex, required 9·recommendations 6) → 14 decisions finalized in §0.0:** first version only `[u8]`·`[bool]` (i32 later), range assumptions only at points where a value comes from outside (entry·call result·havoc), the `elem_range` that the `--infer` search skips is a mandatory gate in final verification, bool array `Array(Int,Bool)` and counterexample JSON, the `alloc` element type only immediately to the right of an untyped `let`·reassignment·`return`, reject use of internal type names in source, audit per batch, 444 fixed by the list of execution rows, cross-check of new samples accepts only `same`. The first implementation batch is **A0 foundation** (no new syntax, preserve the element type in the existing `[i64]` symbolic array).
- **Audit (Claude Opus, document-side required 5·recommendations 6):** A0 boundary was missing `samples.rs` (the prototype path builds `Arr {..}` directly)·`run_all.py` (row-list export), decision 4 misidentified the candidate kind (the element range is **kind 5**, not kind 12; as it stood, a bool array would get `lo <= b[k]` and a z3 sort error), the `in_range(Select)` assumption point at code-read time was unspecified (narrowing it gives a vacuous proof on a read after a wrong store), postponing i32 contradicted the end criteria (26 samples·examples·mutation·independent use) → all reflected in §0.0 (decisions 2·4 reinforced, 15~17 added, batch table boundary reinforced). Recommendations applied: explicit rejection of G2 types while the next layer is unimplemented, G2-specific mutation OPS, fixing the SMT baseline input list, correcting line number citations.

## 32. B++: rlimit exhaustion of an inference search query drops only the candidate (2026-11-11)
Fixed the difference between the two implementations found in 31.1. In the `--infer` search, when a candidate query is `unknown`, only if the z3 reason (`reason_unknown`) is **exactly `timeout`** (the query's 500ms wall-clock deadline) is it, as now, a whole-function `inference_timeout`; for any other reason (`canceled`·`push canceled`·`max. resource limit exceeded` etc. from rlimit exhaustion) **only that candidate is dropped in this round** (discarding a candidate is always sound — a surviving candidate is proved again by the final verification with default settings). Spec `docs/STAGE1_B_INFER.md` 4.1 "B++ revision"·§5 list of `dropped[].status` values. Code: `_probe_attempts` in `pruvc/verify.py`, `probe_attempts` in `pruv-proof/src/obligation.rs` (same flow in both implementations). `query_status` and the path without `--infer` are unchanged. The inference configuration values are unchanged, so the configuration version stays at 2.

- **Empirical basis:** on Python z3 5.3.3, solving the same hard formula with rlimit 100000 gives `unknown`·reason `canceled` (statistic `rlimit count` 100039 ≥ limit), and solving with a large rlimit and timeout 500ms·50ms gives reason `timeout`. That is, `canceled` is rlimit exhaustion (a deterministic result identical on every run) and `timeout` is wall-clock.
- **The first fix (drop regardless of reason) was narrowed by audit required item 1:** if wall-clock `timeout` is also changed to a drop, the set of surviving candidates can vary with load, which conflicts with STAGE1_SPEC §0 item 6 ("timeouts are not turned into candidate removal") and line 283 ("do not make different candidate sets succeed depending on time"). So wall-clock `timeout` was left as the old behavior. The reason wording of a wall-clock overrun could not be reproduced directly on the Rust z3 implementation (Rust has always classified rlimit exhaustion as `resource_unknown`).
- **298 mutants (same command as 31.1, `bench/inference/mutate_infer_32.json`):**

| Implementation | Off: pass/rejected/undecided/crash | On: pass/rejected/undecided/crash | New passes | Lost passes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Python | 20 / 278 / 0 / 0 | 21 / 277 / **0** / 0 | 1 | 0 |
| Rust | 20 / 278 / 0 / 0 | 21 / 277 / **0** / 0 | 1 | 0 |

  Undecided 13 → **0**, `--infer` verdict differences between the two implementations 7 → **0**. The 1 new pass is the same originally-true mutant as 31.1 (`agg_noinv` p02 `>`→`>=`), so new passes of wrong mutants stay 0. Since undecided is 0 even under the narrowed rule, all 13 cases were rlimit exhaustion (`canceled`).
- **Regression:** `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 210/210, SMT snapshot without `--infer` 231 files byte-identical, `diff_trace --flags=--infer` 392 succeeded (same 363·cex_only 27·same_unknown 2, §30 had 364·26·2), run_all 444/444 (both implementations), cli_also_llvm 213, m3_all 9 stages ok, fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 41, pruv_agg with invariants deleted `--infer` 7/10 (both implementations, unchanged).
- **Timing fluctuation of the test tools:** `tests/inference/inf_budget.pruv` (the budget-exhaustion sample) takes about 2 s on the Python implementation when run alone, but under the parallel load of the full `diff_trace` (`--jobs 5`)·`m3_all`, only the Python side hit the 5 s per-function wall clock and became `inference_timeout`, while Rust ended with `budget_exhausted`, producing a diff. The same failure reproduced with the pre-fix code (once out of two runs), and this sample's trace was byte-identical before and after the fix. So `tests/diff_trace.py` now classifies a difference where one side has `inference_timeout` in errors as `flaky_unknown` and re-judges only that input serially (if still different after the retry it is a diff, and it is not added to the success column). In the 392-success run above, retries were 0.
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** no soundness defects, the flow of the two implementations is the same, required 1 (the STAGE1_SPEC violation above) → fixed. Recommendations: worst-case performance — a query started just before the deadline uses 500ms more, up to about 5.5 s per function (not reproduced, not measured); no test directly sees the function-level `timeout` status (hard to make deterministic, so left); marked the "remaining fix" wording in 31.1·BLUEPRINT as fixed (applied).
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus):** required 0. Required 1 resolved (the same verdict order in both implementations, consistent with STAGE1_SPEC §0 item 6·lines 282·283 — the old behavior actually violated line 282, "unknown from a deterministic resource limit removes the candidate"); the new `diff_trace` classification remains a diff if different after retry so it does not hide real differences. Recommendation applied: wording distinguishing traces before and after the revision (spec). Remaining recommendations: no regression sample where only a candidate is dropped by rlimit exhaustion (a deterministically reproducing input could not yet be made), the vocabulary issue that `timeout` in `dropped[].status` also means rlimit exhaustion (consider consolidating to `resource_unknown` at the next vocabulary revision), counting separately in the summary line the number of cells changed by re-judging. Could not confirm from the z3 source that a wall-clock overrun cannot come out as `canceled` (guess: the timer overwrites the reason with `timeout`).
- Not measured: search time on realistically sized inputs, the maximum search time per function.

## 33. G2 A0: implementation foundation for element-typed arrays (2026-11-11)
This is not a G2 feature but the **G2 implementation foundation**. There is no new syntax, and the only array you can write in source is still `[i64]` (`docs/G2_SPEC.md` 0.0.1 A0 row).

- **Element type in symbolic arrays:** in Python `pruvc/verify.py`, `Arr` gets `elem`, and construction goes through the single `mk_arr`. In Rust `pruv-proof/src/symbolic.rs`, `Arr` gets `elem`, and construction goes through the single `Arr::new` (including the `samples.rs` prototype). The element type is passed on at fresh (entry parameters·call results)·alloc (currently fixed to i64)·copy·rebinding·element store·loop havoc. The audit cross-checked the correspondence of the two implementations path by path. The fresh numbering order is unchanged. The branch that builds SMT `Array(Int, Bool)` for bool elements is already included but cannot be reached yet.
- **Array query API and internal names:** Python `typeinfo.py` `is_array`·`array_elem`·`array_of` (None if absent), internal names `array`↔i64·`array_u8`↔u8·`array_bool`↔bool. In Rust, the name functions in `pruv-core/src/typeinfo.rs` are the single correspondence table, and `Elem`·`Ty::Array(Elem)` in `model.rs` use that table (1 round-trip unit test). Internal names are still rejected in source: `[u8]` is parse `syntax`, `array_u8`·`array_bool` are type `unknown_type` (same in both implementations). User types must start with an uppercase letter so they cannot collide with the lowercase internal names.

## 34. G2 A1: `[u8]` frontend (syntax·type checking) (2026-11-11)
`[u8]` can now be written in source, but **only the frontend (parsing·type checking·refinement verification)**. The proof·code generation layers do not know `[u8]` yet, so a `[u8]` program that passes the checks is rejected with an explicit diagnostic (`docs/G2_SPEC.md` 0.0 decision 16). It is not yet counted as a usable G2a feature.

- **Syntax:** `[u8]`, refinement `[u8 | L..=U; <=N]` (the refinement `base` is the written element name `u8`). Scalar `u8`·`i32` give parse `element_storage_type`, `Option<u8>`·`Result<i32>` give parse `unsupported_type`, `[bool]`·`[i32]` still give parse `syntax` ("only [i64] and [u8] arrays exist"). The internal names `array_u8`·`array_bool`·`array_i32` give parse `syntax` in source (decision 7). It used to be type `unknown_type`. `u8` can still be used as a variable name. Added `u8` to the lexer's judgment of the refinement bar `|`.
- **Type checking:** the array rules (no aliasing·element assignment·no `==`·no fields·`len`·indexing·`copy`) were widened from `[i64]` comparison to all array types. The read result is i64, and the stored value is also an i64 expression. `copy` preserves the input type. `alloc`, per decision 6, receives the expected array type only for the right-hand expression itself of a typed let·the right-hand expression itself of an array-variable reassignment·immediately after return. Anywhere else (`copy(alloc(..))`·function argument·`len(alloc(..))`) it is `[i64]`, so using it in a `[u8]` position gives type `type_mismatch`. `array_u8` (`[u8]`)·`array_bool` (`[bool]`) were put into the builtin type table and the output table so that internal names do not leak into diagnostics and `is_user_type` (A0 audit recommendation). Diagnostics·wording·order for `[i64]` inputs are unchanged.
- **Refinement:** for `[u8 | L..=U]` the whole range must be inside 0..=255, and if outside it is type `refinement_bounds` (not interpreted as an intersection). The unfolding is unchanged. The implicit u8 range 0..255 is put in only at domain-introduction points in A2.
- **Unimplemented-layer guard (decision 16):** `first_g2_unimplemented` in both implementations searches, in function order, parameters → return type → body Let (block depth-first) for the first array type that is not `[i64]`. With verification on, it is rejected at the entry of verify (Python `verify_program`, Rust `verify_program_opts`) as verify `unsupported_type`, and with `--no-verify` in the driver as codegen `unsupported_type` (exit 1, message includes the name of the unimplemented layer). The generator functions (`gen_rust`·`gen_llvm`) also produce an internal-error string with the same wording at the start. This is for cross-check tools that do not go through the driver, and both implementations classify it the same as `internal`. The audit judged that there is currently no way to create a `[u8]` value without a declaration, so there is no bypass. If the expected-type passing is widened in a later batch, the guard must also be widened.
- **Samples:** `tests/must_reject/g2a_*` 21 pairs. 13 of the frontend-applicable ones in G2_SPEC 6.2 (scalar_u8·scalar_i32·nested_array·user_element·option_u8·array_field·mixed_argument (`[u8]`→`[i64]`)·param_store·alias·array_eq·refine_wide·return_mismatch·missing_alloc), 2 internal names (internal_name·internal_name_bool), 2 alloc positions (alloc_copy_nested·alloc_argument), 4 guards (a1_verify_layer·a1_codegen_layer·a1_guard_nested·a1_guard_nested_nv). A normally placed alloc·`len(alloc(..))`·a local variable named `u8`·a refined parameter are checked by whether a1_verify_layer passes the checks. The bool samples (bool_fill etc.) are for A5, the verify samples (u8_negative etc.) are for A2. An optional key `flags` was added to the must_reject json to run `--no-verify` samples (`run_all`·`tests/inference/run.py`).
- **Test tools (A0 follow-up audit recommendations):** the `--compare-rows` comparison scope is built from the `--only`·`--backend` arguments rather than from executed rows. If there are old-format rows without `group`, it exits 2. The compiler verdict uses rows within the scope. The Rust round-trip test walks all of `Elem` and `unwrap` was changed to `expect`. The row list `tests/run_all_rows.txt` was saved again with 465 rows (444 + must_reject 21).
- **Regression (last run after the fixes):**
  - run_all `--backend both` both implementations 465/465, row comparison: missing rows·new rows·failures all 0
  - m3_all 9 stages ok (parse/check dumps same in both implementations, compilers 618)
  - SMT for 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - `diff_trace --flags=--infer` 413 succeeded (same 384·cex_only 27·same_unknown 2), without flags 413 succeeded (same 384·cex_only 29). The 21 new ones are all same.
  - `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 231/231, cli_also_llvm 234
  - fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 41·`model::tests` 1
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 0·recommendations 6.
  - Applied (2): samples where the guard descends into a Let inside a block (two guard_nested), a pointless notice line when the number of in-scope stored rows is 0.
  - Recorded only (4):
    - Input with a wrongly written single `|` such as `u8 | ..` now gives parse `syntax` instead of the old lexer `bad_char`. Confirmed by execution that the two implementations are the same.
    - The guard sees only declared types (widened in a later batch).
    - When it fires on a parameter·return, the diagnostic position is the function position.
    - The `len`·`copy`·indexing error messages say "expected [i64]" even though they accept all arrays. Left as is to preserve existing bytes.
- **Fixed directly by the main session:** the stage of the Rust guard diagnostic, which was fixed to "type", was changed to the layer name. Aligned the Python generator guard with the same internal error as Rust `Err(String)` (resolved 8 diff_rust·diff_llvm mismatches). Made the inference regression tool read the sample `flags`.

## 35. G2 A2: `[u8]` proof (2026-11-11)
`[u8]` programs are now proved. Since the code generation layer does not exist yet, a `[u8]` program that passes verification is rejected in the driver as codegen `unsupported_type` (decision 16, lifted in A3·A4). The implementation spec was fixed in `docs/G2_A2_IMPL.md` so that the two implementations emit the same SMT·obligation order·wording.

- **Domain (decision 2):** for a u8 symbolic array, `forall k: 0 <= k < n ==> 0 <= A[k] <= 255` (bound variable with fixed name `elem!k`, no sequence counter used) is assumed **in three places only**: function entry parameters, user-function call results, loop havoc (all immediately after the length assumption). It is not put in after alloc·copy·rebinding·store, and element reads in code assume only the i64 range as before.
- **`elem_range` obligation (verify, new code):** `alloc(n, v)` goes in the order `alloc_negative` → `elem_range` (v within 0..=255), and an element store in the order `index_oob` → `elem_range` → the refinement's `refinement_assign`. The position is the alloc call expression·the store statement. Since it is not a candidate obligation, it is excluded from the `--infer` search and judged only in the final full verification (decision 4). The element type comes from the type the checker attached to the alloc call node.
- **`--infer` filter (decision 4):** builds a table of the function's array name → element type (parameters·Let), kind 5 uses only ranges coming from arrays with the same element type, and among candidates of kind 5 and above, those reading a bool-array element are discarded (currently `[bool]` is blocked in the parser so this does not actually trigger). The implicit u8 range was not added as a new candidate.
- **Guard:** deleted the verify-stage guard. The driver's codegen guard also runs when verify is on (both implementations). Changed the expectation of the A1 samples `g2a_a1_verify_layer`·`g2a_a1_guard_nested` to verify → codegen `unsupported_type` (names kept from A1).
- **23 sample pairs (must_reject 152):**
  - verify rejections 10: u8_negative·u8_high·alloc_value (+ alloc_value_reassign·alloc_value_return, audit applied) give `elem_range`, store_index `index_oob`, refined_store `refinement_assign`, contract_oob `contract_domain`, alloc_length `alloc_negative`, store_then_read (read after storing 300, `10 / (x - 300)`) `div` — this catches the case where assuming 0..255 at the read makes the path contradictory and the div is proved vacuously.
  - Loop exit·return·call after a wrong store, 3 kinds × `--infer` off/on = 6, and a wrong store inside a loop body without an invariant + `--infer` 1 (bad_store_in_loop_inf, follow-up audit applied), all `elem_range`.
  - Proof-passing (expected codegen `unsupported_type`) 6: ok_param·ok_call_result·ok_loop_havoc·ok_branch·ok_copy_store·ok_loop_havoc_inf. param·call_result·copy_store·loop_havoc (both) each fail without the domain (ok_loop_havoc_inf was confirmed to fail the assert when changed to `[i64]`).
  - The u8 rejection samples of G2_SPEC 6.2 are now all filled. bool (bool_fill etc.)·infer_bool·sum_bool are A5, i32 is A6.
- **Regression (last run):**
  - run_all `--backend both` both implementations 488/488 (row list 488 = 465 + 23, missing rows·failures 0)
  - SMT for 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - diff_trace without flags 435 succeeded (same 406·cex_only 29), `--infer` 435 succeeded (same 407·cex_only 26·same_unknown 2). The 22 new ones are same in both modes (decision 11). The 1 added after the follow-up audit is also same in both modes in an individual cross-check (the full cross-check was run with the 435 from before). The remaining classification is at the same level as in A1, and only counterexample values fluctuate.
  - `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 251/251 (on the Python implementation inf_budget timed out once under parallel load, passed on rerun)
  - m3_all 9 stages ok (compilers 664)·cli_also_llvm 257 (after adding all samples), fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0 (before adding the audit-applied sample), cargo test pruv-core 41·`model::tests` 1
- **Fixed in the first cross-check:** the 2 `_inf` samples without invariants were cex_only (only the i value of the counterexample differed between the two implementations) in the cross-check without flags. Changed them to have the same content as the off samples (including the invariant) with only the `--infer` flag, and the inference path without invariants is covered by bad_store_return_inf·ok_loop_havoc_inf.
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 0·recommendations 5.
  - Applied (2): added rejection samples for the alloc reassignment·return points, changed ok_loop_havoc_inf, which had passed without the domain thanks to the inferred prefix invariant (`forall k in 0..i: b[k] == k * 30`), to an element check outside the prefix.
  - Recorded only (3): since the inference's element-type table is name-based, declaring the same name with different array types in sibling blocks can mix them (the candidates go through the final verification so there is no effect on soundness; to be handled in A5 when bool opens). The name `g2a_a1_verify_layer` differs from its current content (codegen-layer rejection). A universal assumption enters the pc of u8 functions, which may slow down functions with many obligations (not measured).
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus):** required 0·recommendation 1 (the sample of a wrong store inside a loop without an invariant + `--infer` was missing) → applied. Also confirmed that the 3 deferred recommendations have no effect on soundness (candidates go through the final verification, and if it becomes slow it is merely rejected as `verify_unknown`).
- Not measured: proof time of u8 functions (R5), Windows·Linux execution.

## 36. G2 A3: `[u8]` Rust source execution (2026-11-11)
`[u8]` programs are now turned into executable code by the Rust source backend (`--backend rust`). The LLVM backend is rejected until A4 as codegen `unsupported_type` ("the llvm codegen layer is not implemented") (decision 16). The generated characters were fixed in `docs/G2_A3_IMPL.md` so that the two implementations emit the same bytes.

- **Generation:** parameter `&[u8]`, local·return `Vec<u8>`, reads widened to i64. Proof mode uses `get_unchecked` + `as u8` only on proved values (two places: store·alloc). Safe mode (`--no-verify`) has `pruv_get_u8`·`pruv_set_u8`·`pruv_alloc_u8` check and then narrow: a store is index → value, alloc is length → value (value check even for length 0). New failure message `pruv: element out of range: <v> (u8 0..=255)` (exit 101). The element read in the requires runtime check is `pruv_cidx_u8` (the O(n) rewrite of the sorted requires also inherits the element type).
- **Existing output unchanged:** the u8 helpers are appended only when the program uses `[u8]`. The generated source of programs using only `[i64]` is byte-for-byte the same.
- **Guard:** the driver lets `[u8]` through only in the pure Rust backend, and rejects every path that uses llvmgen (`--backend llvm`, `--also-llvm`, the Rust implementation's `--emit ll/obj/exe`). The generator's own guard uses the same criterion (the llvmgen guard is unchanged).
- **Scope adjustment:** generalizing `runtime.c` size·alignment was moved to A4. The runtime is used only by the LLVM backend, and putting the v2 host helper extern into the Rust allocator variant would break existing bindings that do not yet have that helper at link time (the v2 runtime and v2 bindings must go in together).
- **Samples:** deleted 10 proof-passing must_reject pairs (they now pass in the rust backend) and moved them to positive samples. 11 examples `g2a_u8_*` (the 8 moved + ASCII refinement uppercasing·loop element change·typed alloc 3), 15 runtime `g2a_rt_*` (both end values, read widening, 256/-1 store rejection, length-0 alloc value check, alloc length priority, store evaluation·check order, copy independence, return, reassignment·early return, refinement entry check, trust, sorted requires, `--infer` havoc, match arm·call result indexing). Their llvm rows expect "llvm layer rejection" via `tests/llvm_pending.txt` (removed in A4). Covers the u8 items of G2_SPEC 8.2; bool layout·raw bool are A5·A4, i32 is A6.
- **Regression (last run):**
  - run_all `--backend both` both implementations 590/590 (row list 590 = 488 − 10 + 112, missing rows·failures 0)
  - SMT for 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - diff_rust·diff_llvm all same, cli_also_llvm both implementations 280/280
  - diff_trace 451 (before adding the 1 audit-applied sample): without flags same 422·cex_only 29, `--infer` same 423·cex_only 26·same_unknown 2. The new samples are same in both modes (decision 11, the 1 added is also same in an individual cross-check). The remaining classification counts are the same as A2.
  - m3_all 9 stages ok (compilers 752), `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 252/252, fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 42·`model::tests` 1
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 0·recommendations 5, all applied: aligned the Index ty fixing of the Rust sorted rewrite with the spec wording, the Python prelude's u8-use determination only once, the run_all pending check also verifies the "llvm codegen" layer wording and the existence of the list file, the cli_also_llvm pending also checks the output·files of the rust execution, added samples for direct indexing of match arms·call results.
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus):** required 0, all 5 applied items correct. Recommendation 1 (the cli_also_llvm pending also checks the layer wording) → applied.
- Not measured: proof time of u8 functions, generated-code performance, Windows·Linux execution.

## 37. G2 A4: `[u8]` LLVM backend·`--rust-bindings`·runtime v2 (2026-11-11)
`[u8]` programs are now also produced by the LLVM backend (`--backend llvm`, `--also-llvm`, the Rust implementation's `--emit ll/obj/exe`) and `--rust-bindings`. With this, `[u8]` is open across the frontend·proof·both execution backends·ABI, all layers (`[bool]` stays `unsupported_type` until A5). The IR·bindings·runtime characters were first fixed in `docs/G2_A4_IMPL.md` so that the two implementations emit the same bytes, and the two implementations had identical IR·binding bytes right at the first comparison.

- **IR:** the aggregate stays `{ ptr, i64 }` (length = number of elements), u8 elements are `i8` (size·alignment 1·1), reads are `zext` → i64, stores are `trunc` → `i8`. Array values·slots carry the element type (filled from parameters·slot load·alloc·copy·call results), and GEP·load·store·copy·free follow it. Safe mode checks in the same order·wording as the Rust backend: store index → value (`icmp ule 255`), alloc length → value (even for length 0), `pruv: element out of range: <v> (u8 0..=255)` (exit 101). Proof mode narrows without checks (`elem_range` proves it). The element reads of requires and the sorted-requires O(n) check also read as `i8`. The 4 u8 declaration lines are attached only to u8 programs, so **the IR of programs using only `[i64]` is byte-for-byte the same**.
- **Runtime v2 (`pruvc/runtime.c`):** existing `pruv_alloc/copy/free`·v1 host helpers·v1 marker are left as is, and `pruv_alloc_u8`·`pruv_copy_v2`·`pruv_free_v2` (element count·size·alignment)·`pruv_panic_elem` were added. The size computation checks, before multiplying, the layout (1·1/4·4/8·8)·`len <= 2^40`·`len*size <= SIZE_MAX`. The Rust allocator variant calls the v2 host helpers (`pruv_host_alloc_v2`·`_zeroed_v2`·`pruv_host_free_v2`, `Layout(len*size, align)`) and defines the marker `pruv_runtime_rust_allocator_v2`.
- **Bindings:** a `[u8]` argument is `*const u8, len`, a return is a `Pruv__ArrayU8 { data: *mut u8, len }` out pointer (the existing `Pruv__Array` is kept), and the public functions are `&[u8]`/`Vec<u8>`. The three v2 host helpers are **always included in the bindings of every program**, and a function returning an array references the v2 marker. So the binding characters changed for every program (G2_SPEC 5.4 compatibility items 3·4: regeneration·relinking required). Regenerated the Windows CI fixed artifacts `tests/abi/winci/out` and added the u8 sample call list (48 files, `--check` identical).
- **Link tests (`tests/abi/check_runtime_v2.py`, m3_all `abi_v2` stage):** all 6 cases as expected — new bindings + new runtime succeed (u8 has ≥1 frees inside the runtime·allocations = frees + 1), new bindings + old runtime (`tests/abi/runtime_v1.c` = copy of the A3 runtime) fail (both u8·i64, no v2 marker), a mock of old bindings without the v2 helpers + new runtime fail, new bindings + malloc runtime fail.
- **Guard:** merged the driver·two generators into a single guard that skips only `array_u8` on every path. By deleting `tests/llvm_pending.txt`, the llvm rows of the 11 examples `g2a_u8_*`·15 runtime `g2a_rt_*` changed from "rejection expected" to actual link·execution checks (the row list is unchanged).
- **Allocation/free balance:** attached `alloc_balance` to the 6 u8 runtime samples that end with exit 0 (excluding `g2a_rt_return`, where Rust releases the returned array, `g2a_rt_refine_trust` with 0 allocations, and the 7 ending in panic). New sample `g2a_rt_free_paths`: loop reassignment, branch early return (with a live copy), call-result temporaries, a return that moves a local array to a pruv caller, and `[u8]` argument passing, run in both modes, checking balance + the function-body IR characters (`call void @pruv_free_v2(`·`call ptr @pruv_copy_v2(` present, no i64 helper).
- **Regression (last run):**
  - run_all `--backend both` both implementations 594/594 (row list 594 = 590 + 4 new samples, missing rows·failures 0). After audit changes, `--only runtime` both implementations 348 rows failures 0
  - SMT for 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - diff_llvm all same (u8 IR·binding bytes identical across both implementations), m3_all 10 stages ok (compilers 760, including rust·llvm·trace·trace_infer·standalone·abi·abi_v2), cli_also_llvm both implementations 280/280, check_generated passes (u8 return = out pointer + void)
  - new-sample diff_trace same in both modes (decision 11), `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 254/254, fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 42
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 0·recommendations 6, all applied: free-helper IR character check on u8 samples (the count alone cannot catch a regression to i64-size freeing), added passing cases for requires u8 reads (sorted·refinement entry), tightened the link-test verdict (≥1 frees inside the runtime, the u8 old runtime must have the marker), narrowed the spec's link-failure wording to the actual guaranteed scope, recorded the `alloc_balance` exceptions, samples for the moving-return·argument-passing paths.
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus):** required 0, 5 of the 6 applied items correct. 3 recommendations: the whole-.ll check of free·copy helpers was always true because of the u8 declaration lines → changed to the function-body `llvm_fn_contains`, updated the old balance criterion·expected symbols in the spec's link-test table, recorded the new path in the spec's sample description → all applied.
- **Windows CI (after push, run 42986134593):** passed — the new bindings of 12 samples (including the u8 return `g2a_u8_ret`) were linked and run on Windows with `runtime.c` (Rust allocator variant, including the v2 helpers) compiled to an object by clang and with rustc (MSVC), and gave the same output as the Mac expectation.

## 38. G2 A5: `[bool]` arrays in all layers (2026-11-11)
`[bool]` (including `[bool; <=N]`) was opened in one batch across the frontend·proof·Rust source backend·LLVM backend·`--rust-bindings`·runtime. With this, the G2a first-version element types `[u8]`·`[bool]` work in all layers (`[i32]` postponed to A6, G2_SPEC decision 1). The diagnostics·generated code·IR·bindings·runtime characters were first fixed in `docs/G2_A5_IMPL.md` so that the two implementations emit the same characters, and the Rust source·IR·bindings of bool programs were byte-identical across the two implementations at the first comparison.

- **Frontend:** the element name `bool` is allowed (the error wording for other names is `only [i64], [u8] and [bool] arrays exist`), `[bool | L..=U]` is `type/refinement_base`. `a[i]` is bool, the stored value is bool, and `alloc(n, v)` takes `v: bool` only when the decision-6 hint is `[bool]` (`len(alloc(4, false))` without a hint is `type_mismatch`). `sum` with a bool body is rejected, `count(k in ..: a[k])` is allowed.
- **Proof:** SMT `Array(Int, Bool)`. Reading a bool element gives a Bool value without the i64 range assumption (changed the Python range assumption·the place where Rust fixed `Val::Int`), alloc is `K(Int, v)`, a store is `Store`, no `elem_range` obligation·domain assumption. **Elements of a bool array in the counterexample JSON now come out as `true/false`** (previously this variable was dropped entirely from the counterexample). The sample `g2a_bool_cex` confirms `"first": [true]` in both implementations.
- **`--infer`:** changed the element-type table from a name-based flat table to a **loop-position-based table** (the recommendation deferred from the A2 audit). When sibling blocks use the same name as `[i64]`·`[bool]`, the old table produced an integer-range candidate (`0 <= a[k] && a[k] <= 5`) for the bool array (reproduced with a copy of the old infer.py). The new tests `tests/inference/inf_g2a_bool_sibling`·`inf_g2a_infer_bool` check that there is no such candidate.
- **Execution:** memory is 1 byte per element, 0/1 (no bit packing). Rust source backend `&[bool]`/`Vec<bool>` (safe-mode helpers `pruv_get_bool`·`pruv_set_bool`·`pruv_alloc_bool`, contract read `pruv_cidx_bool`). LLVM: store `zext i1 → i8`, read `load i8` then `icmp ne i8 0`, alloc reuses `pruv_alloc_u8(n, zext v)`, copy·free are v2 (1,1). The bool declarations·prelude·struct are attached only to bool programs, so **the output of `[i64]`·`[u8]` programs is byte-for-byte the same**.
- **Raw ABI representation check:** added `pruv_check_bool`·`pruv_panic_repr` to the runtime. `pruv_abi_<f>`, called directly by C, checks every byte of a `[bool]` argument before requires, and if it is not 0/1 gives `pruv: invalid representation: 2 (bool 0..=1)` (exit 101). It also runs under `--trust-callers`. A `[bool]` return of the bindings is taken over as `Vec<bool>` after the check (`Pruv__ArrayBool`). The `&[bool]` of the typed Rust bindings calls `pruv_abi_<f>__typed`, with only the check lines removed, since the type guarantees 0/1 (audit required 2: removed the redundant O(n) check on every call). New test `tests/abi/check_bool_repr.py` (m3_all `abi_bool`): two compilers × 8 cases = 16/16 (normal, bytes 2·255, trust build, representation error before requires).
- **Samples:** must_reject 9 (`g2a_bool_fill`·`_store`·`_read_int`·`_refine`·`_alloc_nohint`·`_cex`, `g2a_sum_bool`·`g2a_mixed_bool`·`g2a_infer_bool`), examples 5, runtime 7 (return value, copy independence, safe-mode index panic, requires element read and `--trust-callers`, free paths·balance, `--infer` sibling blocks), ABI gen `g2a_bool_ret` + winci call list (52 files regenerated). Row list 594 → 663.
- **Regression (last run):**
  - run_all `--backend both` both implementations 663/663 (row list 663, missing rows·failures 0)
  - SMT for 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - m3_all 11 stages ok (parse·check·trace·trace_infer·llvm·rust·compilers 838·standalone·abi·abi_v2·abi_bool), cli_also_llvm both implementations 304/304, check_generated passes, winci `--check` 52 files identical
  - new-sample diff_trace `--infer` on/off succeeded (decision 11), `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 271/271 (including new examples·tests), fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 42·model::tests 1
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 2·recommendations 5, all applied. Required: the sibling-block sample passed even with the old table so it could not catch the regression → inference `absent` test, the redundant O(n) check of the bindings' `&[bool]` argument → `__typed` entry. Recommendations: unify the two implementations' fallback value when no loop is found, stale comments, tighten the counterexample check characters, reinforce the infer tests, spec numbers.

## 39. G2 B0: `str` frontend (2026-11-11)
As the first batch of G2b (strings), the `str` type and string literals were opened through the lexer·parser·type/effect checking·refinement·dump. Proof (B1)·execution·ABI (B2) do not exist yet, so a program that uses `str` or string literals is rejected as `verify/unsupported_type` at the proof layer, and with `--no-verify` as `codegen/unsupported_type` at the generation layer (decision 16). The diagnostic wording·dump characters were first fixed in `docs/G2_B0_IMPL.md`, and the two implementations were byte-identical at the first comparison.

- **Literals:** `"..."` is a UTF-8 byte sequence (Py `bytes`, Rs `Vec<u8>`). Escapes are only `\" \\ \n \r \t \0`. 3 new parse errors: `string_escape` (any other escape, including an actual line break after `\`, position at the backslash), `string_unterminated` (end of input, position at the opening quote), `string_character` (actual line break·ASCII control characters·0x7F). Column numbers remain per character. `//`·parentheses·`|` inside a string are ordinary characters. `StrLit.value` in the dump is lowercase hex.
- **Types:** the internal name is `"str"`, the same as the source spelling (no leak of decision 7). `str` is not an array. `len`·indexing (result i64)·`copy` accept both arrays and `str`. New builtins `is_char_boundary(s, i) -> bool` (code·contract), `substr(s, lo, hi) -> str` (`alloc`, `effect_in_contract` inside a contract). Element assignment is `type/immutable_string`, ownership is the same as arrays except that a literal is also a source (`copy` on a parameter return), whole-string `==`/`!=` is `eq_type` in both code and contract, struct·enum fields `field_type`, `Option<str>`·`Result<str>` are `parse/unsupported_type`. A literal in code has the `alloc` effect, a literal in a contract has no effect. `substr`·`is_char_boundary` became reserved function names.
- **Guard:** `first_g2_unimplemented` now looks at the `str` type and **literals inside expressions** (previously only declared types). For each function, the earliest position among parameter → return → candidates inside the function (str Let·all literals in requires/ensures/body). A guard was newly placed at the very front of the proof-layer entry (Py `verify_program`, Rs `verify_program_opts`) (previously there was only the codegen guard after verify, so this was a place where an internal error would arise if str entered the proof). `--infer` also ends before this. The guard result for arrays is unchanged.
- **Changed existing behavior:** `"` is no longer `bad_char` (`tests/parse_edge/badchar_dquote.pruv` is parse success·check `print_type`). The Py implementation's source reading is now `open(path, encoding="utf-8")` (the same strict UTF-8 as the Rs implementation).
- **Samples:** must_reject (`g2b_*`: lexer 6, `Option<str>`, type 15, effect 1, layer guard 8 — verify·literal only·`--infer`·`--no-verify`·contract literal·position order 3), 31 pairs in all. parse_edge 10, check_edge 6. 4 new required codes in `diff_parse`. Row list 663 → 694. The 5 verify samples of G2_SPEC 6.3 (`g2b_slice_range`·`_slice_start`·`_slice_end`·`_index`·`_contract_index`) are B1.
- **Regression (last run):**
  - run_all `--backend both` both implementations passed (row list 694, missing rows·failures 0)
  - diff_parse parse·check both implementations 3981 all same, diff_compilers `--only must_reject --backend both` 362 same
  - SMT for 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - m3_all 11 stages ok (compilers 894), cli_also_llvm both implementations 332/332, `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 299/299, fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 42·model::tests 1
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 0·recommendations 4. Applied: two-implementation cross-check of guard positions (2 position samples + diff_compilers), fixed wording (`cex_contains`) in samples that share a code (`g2b_eq`·`g2b_alias`·`g2b_param_return`), fixed samples without a trailing newline with `.gitattributes -text`. The Py guard traversal cost is 0.125ms once on the largest sample, so left as is.
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus):** required 0, confirmed the 4 first-round applied items. 3 recommendations applied: a position sample where the literal comes before the Let (`g2b_b0_guard_lit_first`), added the new samples·wording fixing to the table in spec 9.1, corrected the `.gitattributes` comment.
- **Windows CI:** this change does not touch the CI paths (`tests/abi/winci/**`·`pruvc/runtime.c`·workflow), so it did not run after the push. Instead, `winci/prepare.py --check` confirmed that the 52 files are the same as the generated result (the last Windows pass is run 42992603822).
- Not measured: running the Python implementation on Windows (effect of locale encoding changes), Linux execution.


## 40. G2 B1: `str` proof (2026-11-11)

The `str` opened through the frontend in B0 was made to be handled at the proof layer. Since execution·ABI (B2) do not exist yet, a program that uses `str` or literals is rejected at the generation layer as `codegen/unsupported_type` after passing the proof (decision 16). The SMT characters·obligation order·wording were first fixed in `docs/G2_B1_IMPL.md`, and the two implementations were the same at the first comparison.

- **Representation:** the symbolic `str` is, without a new shape, a symbolic array with element U8 (`Array(Int, Int)` + length). So the byte 0..255 assumption (`u8_dom`) attaches, with the existing code as is, to the three places of decision 2 (function entry·call results·loop havoc), and length·code read (index_oob, the read range assumption stays i64)·contract read (contract_domain)·`copy`·counterexample `{"len","first"}` all go the array way. Rs adds a new `Ty::Str` variant and only fresh creates a U8 array.
- **Literals:** a constant array made by `Store`-ing the bytes in order into `K(Int, 0)`, length is the number of bytes. No counters·obligations·assumptions (the same term in code·contract).
- **Boundary:** `is_char_boundary(s, i)` = `Or(i == 0, i == n, And(i > 0, i < n, Or(s[i] < 128, s[i] >= 192)))` (G2_SPEC 4.4). Negative·past-the-end is false, no domain obligation.
- **substr:** argument evaluation → `string_slice_range` (`0 <= lo <= hi <= len(s)`) → `utf8_boundary` (both ends as one obligation, also for `lo == hi`) → new result (`len = hi - lo`, `forall k: result[k] == s[lo + k]`, bound variable `str!k`). Both obligations are proved without cand, so they are excluded from the `--infer` search and judged only in the final verification (decision 4). No range assumption is separately put on literals·substr results (they are constants or equal to the original bytes, so it already holds).
- **UTF-8 over-approximation (decision 13):** a `str` in SMT may be any byte sequence. "a counterexample str may not be valid UTF-8" was written in both FIX wordings.
- **Guard:** added `str` to the skip of the proof-layer entry guard (it now skips all G2 types but is kept for A6 i32). The 6 generation-layer guard places are unchanged.
- **Samples:** the 5 verify samples of G2_SPEC 6.3 (`g2b_slice_range`·`_slice_start`·`_slice_end`·`_index`·`_contract_index`), 8 B1 positive proofs (`g2b_b1_ok_*`: byte length ASCII·2/3/4 bytes·empty string·NUL, boundaries 0·end·interior·continuation·negative·past-end·leading byte 194/195/236·ASCII 126, literal→copy→substr→return→caller, parameter·call-result byte range, havoc, contract, `--infer`), 12 B1 rejections (non-vacuity assert 4, boundary·range obligations 6, rejected also under `--infer` 1, ensures 1). Moved the expected stage of the 5 B0 layer-guard samples from `verify`→`codegen` (in `g2b_b0_guard_lit_first`, since the proof runs and overflow comes first, `+ n` was removed, position 4:20 unchanged). Row list 694 → 719.
- **Regression (last run):**
  - run_all `--backend both` both implementations 719/719 (row list 719, missing rows·failures 0)
  - SMT for 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - trace cross-check of new·changed g2b samples: both without flags and with `--infer` same (51 samples×2 = 102 cases, the 5 samples×2 added after the audit also same)
  - diff_compilers `--only must_reject --backend both` 414 same, diff_parse parse 3537 (same 3535, excluding 2 Py recursion-limit cases)·check 4142 (same 4140, same 2 excluded) verdict "all same"
  - m3_all 11 stages ok, cli_also_llvm both implementations 355/355, `tests/inference --regress` both implementations 322/322 (in the first run, which was run concurrently with other tests, Py had 1 failure from `inf_budget` timing out, passed on standalone rerun), fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 42·model::tests 1
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 0·recommendations 5. No false proofs·no SMT differences between the two implementations, baseline SMT 231 same (HEAD baseline reproduced separately). Applied: stale comments, the reason for the B0 sample body change in the spec, 5 missing boundary sample pairs (continuation 191·leading 194/195/236·`lo == hi` in the middle·negative lo·hi exceeded·3/4-byte truncation), and in spec 5.6 the completeness limit that `--infer` has no candidates under a literal loop condition and that str counterexample values can differ between the two implementations (cex_only).
- **Follow-up audit (Claude Opus):** required 0, confirmed the 5 first-round applied items. 2 recommendations applied: since run_all only checks that a diagnostic is "contained", corrected the "first diagnostic" expression in comments·spec (obligation order confirmed by direct execution), added an assert of `"a~"` (126) for mutants that lower the 128 threshold (127 cannot be used in a literal so it remains).
- **Windows CI:** did not run because CI paths were not touched. `winci/prepare.py --check` confirmed the 52 files are identical.
- Not measured: str proof time (other than the audit's diff_trace 0.2~0.3 s), Windows·Linux execution, why the diff_parse count is counted differently from the B0 record (3981), agreement between the execution boundary judgment and the SMT boundary (in B2).

## 41. G2 B2: `str` execution and raw ABI (2026-11-11)

`str`, which until B1 was only proved, is opened up in the Rust-source backend, the LLVM backend, the raw ABI, `--rust-bindings`, and the C runtime. Programs that use `str` and literals are now generated and run end to end. The generated text (literal escapes, boundary and substr Rust/IR, runtime functions, panic messages, bindings) was fixed first in `docs/G2_B2_IMPL.md`, and the two implementations, built in five parallel tracks, were byte-identical on the first cross-check.

- **Representation:** the runtime str is `{ ptr, i64 }` (length = byte count, element `i8`, v2 layout (1,1)). In Rust source, parameters are `&str`, locals and returns are `String`. A literal is Rust `String::from("…")` (of 0x20..0x7E, only characters other than `"` and `\` are kept as-is, everything else is `\u{X}`); in LLVM, an `@.str.N` global is copied with `pruv_copy_v2` (no copy for literals inside contracts). A read `s[i]` is the same zext as for u8.
- **Boundary:** the runtime `pruv_str_boundary`, the Rust `pruv_str_boundary` (i64), `pruv_cboundary` (i128), and the LLVM contract path (`icmp ule i128` → `select -1`) all mean the same as the B1 SMT formula (0 and len are true, negative and past-the-end are false, anything else is not a continuation byte). Contract values 2^64 and -2^64 (lower 64 bits zero) were also tested and are false.
- **substr:** after evaluating the arguments s → lo → hi, proof mode copies into a new buffer without checks (Rust `get_unchecked`, LLVM `getelementptr inbounds` + `pruv_copy_v2`); safe mode checks range → boundary in that order (`pruv: string slice out of bounds: LO..HI (len N)` / `pruv: UTF-8 boundary error: LO..HI (len N)`, exit 101, same text in both backends) and then copies. The result is a buffer independent of the original.
- **Raw ABI (decision 13 a):** a str parameter of `pruv_abi_<f>` is checked by `pruv_check_utf8` before requires and the body (Unicode Table 3-7: isolated continuation, truncated multi-byte sequence, overlong, surrogate, beyond U+10FFFF; after the audit also negative length and NULL with positive length), and the check is kept under `--trust-callers` too. The generated Rust bindings call the `__typed` entry with no check line (`&str` is guaranteed by the language), and a returned str is taken over with `String::from_utf8_unchecked` after `pruv_check_utf8`.
- **Guard:** `str` is added to the 6 generation-layer guard skips (all G2 types are open; the function is kept for A6 i32). The sorted-requires fast path is not used for str.
- **Samples:** deleted the 16 old pairs of str samples that expected generation-layer rejection (`g2b_b0_*` 8, `g2b_b1_ok_*` 8). The 7 proof positives were moved to `examples/g2b_*`, and 2 more were added: ASCII substring and boundary branch (examples 9). 16 runtime `g2b_rt_*` samples (escapes, NUL, empty string, multi-byte, large boundary, both ends, bad range/boundary/negative ×3, evaluation order, failure after evaluation order, reassignment, nested temporaries, return ownership, contract read, `--infer`; 3 allocation-balance samples). 2 must_reject pairs (`g2b_b2_bad_slice_range`, `_cut`). Raw UTF-8 test `tests/abi/check_utf8_repr.py` (10 valid, 12 rejected, representation first, negative length, NULL; default and trust builds × two compilers = 104 cases, m3_all stage `abi_utf8`). ABI artifacts `g2b_str_ret` and `g2a_bool_opt` (A5 confirmation-audit recommendation: [bool] argument + Option return sret), plus `__typed` signature and definition checks in `check_generated.py` (A5 recommendation). Row list 719 → 833.
- **Regression (last run):**
  - run_all `--backend both`, both implementations 833/833 (row list 833, missing rows and failures 0)
  - SMT 231 files without `--infer`: byte-identical to the baseline
  - trace cross-check of the 27 new or changed str samples: same (`g2b_rt_infer` uses `--infer`)
  - diff_compilers `--only must_reject --backend both` 386 same (down from 414 because the old samples were deleted), diff_parse parse 3649 (same 3647 + 2 Py recursion limits) and check 4254 (same 4252 + 2) succeeded
  - m3_all 12 stages ok (llvm and rust artifacts same in both implementations, compilers 1050, standalone 50 same, abi_bool 16/16, abi_utf8 104/104), cli_also_llvm 373/373 in both implementations, `tests/inference --regress` 325/325 in both implementations, fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0, cargo test pruv-core 42 and model::tests 1, winci `prepare.py --check` 60 files identical
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 0, recommended 6. (a) raw pre-check, (b) SMT/runtime boundary agreement, (c) substr byte relation and deallocation: no defects in any. Applied: `pruv_check_utf8` rejects negative length and NULL, UTF-8 sample 11 for the boundary end values, a 2^64 contract-boundary sample, `willreturn memory(argmem: read)` on the `pruv_str_boundary` declaration, Py `uses_str` computed only once. The public `__typed` symbol (same design as A5) and the re-check of binding returns (a defense under `--trust-callers`) are kept, with the reasons written in spec 5.5.
- **Confirmation audit (Claude Opus):** required 0, 6 fixes confirmed (`memory(argmem: read)` is accepted by clang 22 and Apple clang 17, and the runtime function really reads only its argument memory). 2 recommendations applied: added negative-length and NULL tests, updated the runtime and test header comments.
- **Windows CI:** ran because runtime.c and winci were changed. After the push, run 43012869415 (commit 3260277) succeeded — the windows-latest clang accepted the `memory(argmem: read)` declaration and the str artifacts (including `g2b_str_ret` and `g2a_bool_opt`) and ran them.
- Left over: rejection of negative length and NULL at the `[i64]`, `[u8]`, and `[bool]` raw entries (a gap that existed before B2; only str was fixed).
- Not measured: str proof and execution time, Linux execution.

## 42. G2 Q1: G2 fuzzing, G2 mutation, 6 G2 problems (2026-11-11)

This closes the G2_SPEC 8.3 (fuzzer and mutation) gate. The compilers (`pruvc/`, `pruvc-rs/`) were not changed; only test tooling and measurements were added. The contract is in `docs/G2_Q1_IMPL.md`.

- **6 G2 problems and the grader (`bench/g2/`):** byte-range conversion (`clamp_bytes`), ASCII filter (`ascii_mask`), u8-array saturating update (`add_sat`, used instead of an i32 update per decision 15), longest bool run (`longest_true_run`), UTF-8 character count (`count_chars`), first k characters (`prefix_chars`). `PROBLEMS.md`, `reference.rs` (trusted Rust implementation), `run.py` (g1 grader shape + `--compiler py|rs`, random UTF-8 input: 1/2 ASCII and 1/6 each of 2/3/4-byte, EDGE + 300 inputs per problem), and the reference Pruv solutions `solutions/ref_pruv/`. The reference solutions pass verification in all four combinations (two compilers × rust and llvm backends) and are correct on all 308–314 inputs. Q2's independent-author solutions and measurements build on this.
- **Reference-solution contracts made to carry the full meaning:** when G2 mutation was measured on the first version (ensures only about length), 39 of 124 false mutants passed verification. This was not a compiler defect: the contract did not carry the meaning, so a "wrong value" was not a promise violation. The ensures were changed to per-element values, `count`, and boundary counts. For q04, the invariant "there is a true run whose length is the result" (exists) timed out, so the start position `start` of that run was made a code variable and used as the witness.
- **G2 mutation (`bench/g2/mutate_ops.py`, decision 17):** the existing `bench/mutate.py` OPS are left as they are (read from the source by ast and run together as `base`), plus 7 G2-specific operations — store upper bound / byte constant ±1 (`byte_const_pm1`), range guard deletion (`guard_true`/`guard_false`), index end value (`len_pm1`), substring boundary ±1 (`substr_pm1`), boundary-condition deletion (`boundary_drop`, `conj_drop`). Each mutant gets 4 verifications (py and rs × `--infer` off and on), and true/false is judged automatically with an rs `--no-verify` build + the grader. i32 sign-extension mutants go to A6 (decision 15).

| Result (`bench/g2/results/mutants_q1.json`) | Value |
|---|---|
| Mutants (identical text merged) | 129 |
| False mutants / true mutants | 113 / 16 |
| **False mutants that passed in at least one of the 4 runs** | **0** |
| True mutants passed | 10 |
| Compiler crashes | 0 |
| Undecided (`verify_unknown`, not counted as passing) | 4 cells (2 q04 26-line mutants) |

  The 4 undecided cells appeared in the first run, which ran concurrently with fuzzing, and re-running q04 alone gave the same 4 undecided cells (`mutants_q1_q04_alone.json`) — so it is not load: the proofs of those two mutants (`if cur > best` → `>=`, → `true`) do not finish within the default time. Because of this there are 2 verdict differences between the two implementations (one undecided, the other decided). Neither is a pass, and the `>=` one is a true mutant.
- **Fuzzer G2 mode (`bench/fuzz/*.py --g2`):** the oracle interprets G2 semantics independently without importing the compiler (string literals and escapes, `[u8]`/`[bool]`/`str` values, the three places where the alloc narrow type is determined, alloc length → value and store index → value order, `is_char_boundary` = the G2_SPEC 4.4 formula, `substr` range → boundary, safe-mode messages `element out of range`, `string slice out of bounds`, `UTF-8 boundary error`). The generator uses 11 G2 templates (truncating store, u8 scan, copy update, alloc u8, bool aggregate and construction, str scan, boundary substring, literal, str→u8, requires-boundary call) with a separate rng, and makes G2-specific faulty variants at a rate of about 0.3. The path without `--g2` is unchanged (`generate.py --seed 41 --count 300` output is byte-identical to before the fix; existing fuzzer seed 41×200 failures 0).
  - Only strong programs count toward the gate (at least 8 inputs, results in all modes, and for verified-pass programs at least one input that satisfies requires and is compared to the end). If short, exit 1 with `g2_shortfall` (decision 11).

| seed | compiler | strong verified passes (target 200) | strong rejected safe runs (target 30) | executed inputs | failures | oracle limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 41 | rs / py | 217 / 217 | 35 / 35 | 2036 | 0 / 0 | 12 |
| 56 | rs / py | 220 / 220 | 34 / 34 | 2039 | 0 / 0 | 8 |
| 57 | rs / py | 219 / 219 | 35 / 35 | 2037 | 0 / 0 | 0 |

  The 4 modes (rust and llvm × proof and safe) were compared against the oracle, and in the safe mode of rejected programs, 32–41 `elem_range`, 2–10 `slice_cut`, and 0–6 `slice_range` inputs matched down to the actual runtime message. Summaries are in `bench/g2/results/fuzz_{rs,py}_{41,56,57}.json`. Programs with fewer than 8 inputs (the kind that take only one short literal) were excluded from the gate count, 1–3 per seed.
- **No degradation on the existing mutation corpus:** the same 298 `mutate_infer.py` mutants as in 31.1 — both implementations: without `--infer` 20/278, with it 21/277, undecided 0, crash 0, new passes 1 (the same true mutant as in §32), same as §32.
- **Audit (Claude Opus):** required 2, recommended 5. Required: (1) the fuzzer `FAULT_KINDS` lacked the three G2 failure kinds, so G2 safe-mode failures and proof-mode soundness were not compared — the "failures 0" of the first fuzzing is a number with that comparison missing, so it was discarded and only the table above, re-run after the fix, is recorded. (2) The count of rejected safe runs was tallied with an always-true condition → the "strong program" rule. Recommendations applied: the oracle also narrows a parenthesized `(alloc(..))` like the compiler (confirmed by `elem_range` rejection in the Rust implementation), a rustc failure in the mutant truth judgment is treated as a crash, empty-substitution deletion in the generator. Kept: the reimplementation of line substitution for mutant `base` (behaves the same as the current `mutate.py`).
- **Confirmation audit (Claude Opus):** required 0, the 7 earlier findings confirmed resolved (the path without `--g2` was compared directly against HEAD's generate and oracle: 300 cases and inputs, 0 differences in 1440 oracle runs). 3 recommendations applied: comments in the mutation tool, the README's strong-condition and 110% collection explanation, exclusion of timeouts and harness-exception runs from the strong condition (re-ran seed 57 rs after the fix: 219, 35, failures 0, same).
- **Windows CI:** not run, because the CI paths (winci, runtime.c, workflow) were not touched. `winci/prepare.py --check` 60 files identical.
- Left over: the proof-undecided state of the two q04 26-line mutants (not a pass), rejection of negative length and NULL at the `[i64]`, `[u8]`, and `[bool]` raw entries (a gap that existed before B2). Not measured: G2 proof time, execution time, and memory (Q2 measurement), Linux execution.

## 43. G2 Q2: measurements, 6 independent-use problems, SPEC summary — G2 complete (2026-11-11)

The last G2 gate (G2_SPEC 7.7 and 8.4). Spec `docs/G2_Q2_IMPL.md`. **The compilers (`pruvc/`, `pruvc-rs/`) were not changed** — only measurement tools, solutions, and documents were added. The comparison baseline is commit `5c65fe3` just before G2 (worktree built separately), and now the Rust implementation `pruvc` at HEAD (rebuilding it had nothing to compile, confirming it matches the source). Screen recording (ffmpeg) ran throughout the measurements, so the machine was under load (loadavg 5–13, 10 cores).

- **SPEC.md §6b (one page of G2 user rules):** `[u8]` and `[bool]` (store range `elem_range`, the three places where the alloc narrow type is determined, exact type match, `count` for bool), `str` (literals and escapes, byte `len`/`s[i]`, ownership same as arrays, the `is_char_boundary` defining formula, `substr` obligations `string_slice_range`→`utf8_boundary`, what is absent, the prover does not know UTF-8 rules), safe-mode messages and raw-entry checks. 2 rows added to the table. Several rules were verified against actual behavior with small programs (rejection of a bool body in sum, an alloc in argument position is `[i64]`, the first-byte boundary is not proved, str variable assignment is `array_alias`).
- **SPEC.md §8 `verify_unknown` guide (new):** it is not a counterexample but a hit of the 8-second wall-clock limit per attempt; how to make proofs easier (state a single-position fact without quantifiers, nested quantifiers are expensive, small facts as invariants). It was added because it showed up as a spec gap during independent use (below).

### Independent use (8.4)

Each time the author was a fresh Claude sonnet write delegation (no knowledge of earlier attempts or conversation), allowed to read only `SPEC.md` and `bench/g2/PROBLEMS.md`, writing without running anything. The main session graded with `bench/g2/run.py` in four combinations (Rust implementation and Python implementation × rust and llvm backends), 308–314 inputs per problem. Results in `bench/g2/results/indep*_*.json`.

| Author | SPEC | Attempt | Rust implementation (both backends) | Python implementation (both backends) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 6b only | try1 (first attempt) | **6/6** | **5/6** — q04 `verify_unknown` (double-forall invariant preserved, timeout) |
| 1 | same | try2 (fixed after receiving only diagnostics) | 5/6 (q04 timeout) | 5/6 |
| 1 | same | try3 (last fix) | 5/6 (q04 timeout) | 5/6 |
| 2 | + §8 guide | try1 | 6/6 | 6/6 — **counted as invalid**: the guide's example contained the q04 correct invariant (`cur <= best`) (audit required 1) |
| 3 | §8 example rewritten as general wording | **try1 (first attempt)** | **6/6** | **6/6** |

- The gate verdict uses author 3's first attempt (0 fixes): together with the reference solution (Q1, 6/6 in all four combinations) it is **6/6**. Caveat: author 3 did not know the answer, but the §8 guide it read was refined after seeing author 1's q04 failure (the bad and good examples are author 1's invariant shape transplanted to a different problem). The q04 ensures of authors 2 and 3 are identical down to the characters. It is **not** the case that author 1 fixed things to reach 6/6 — author 1 fixed twice from diagnostics alone but could not turn q04 into a shape whose proof finishes quickly.
- All failures are the single problem q04, all `verify_unknown` (proof undecided), and no wrong program ever passed. q01–q03, q05, and q06 passed for every author and attempt (including the string problems q05 and q06).
- The Python implementation's q04 timeout is not deterministic: the number of error lines on the same file varied from 1 to 3 between runs (wall-clock limit under load). Authors 2 and 3 both wrote "every interval of length result+1 contains a false" as `forall … exists`, avoiding the double forall — the general §8 advice "nested quantifiers are expensive" may have influenced this choice.

It was re-measured by making the function return a str and calling the raw C entry directly.

The earlier 3 required items are resolved (1 and 3), and 2 is resolved by this fix. 6 recommendations applied: the caveat on author 3's verdict, added author 3 solver statistics, corrected the bench input count, the p04 range, and the llvm RSS figures, first-run time, the i32 array measurement deferred to A6, completion verdict after the audit results were recorded. The additional SPEC 6b/8 rules match the implementation (cross-checked by file:line).

- **Windows CI:** not run, because the CI paths (winci, runtime.c, workflow) were not touched. After commit 73204d7, `winci/prepare.py --check` 60 files identical.

### G2 completion verdict

**G2 complete (G2a = `[u8]` and `[bool]`, G2b = `str`).** End criteria: two-implementation cross-check and regression (A0–B2), fuzzing and mutation (Q1), bench, measurements, independent use, and SPEC summary (Q2), one audit + one confirmation per batch. **A6 `[i32]` was deferred** (decisions 1 and 15: 2 i32 reject samples, 2 examples, 1 runtime, sign-extension mutants, an independent-use i32 problem, and the 8.4 i32 array execution time and memory measurement belong to the A6 end criteria). Unresolved audit-required items 0.

Left over: no check of negative length and NULL at the raw `[i64]`, `[u8]`, and `[bool]` entries (the caller's responsibility per G2_SPEC 5.4, stated in SPEC 6b), the proof-undecided state of the two Q1 q04 26-line mutants, and the non-determinism of the Python implementation's q04 timeout. Not measured: Linux execution, time on an unloaded machine, inspection of the generated code (IR) of the array perf programs. Next candidate (proposal only): replace Lair's `src/backend/engine-import/tail.ts` `clipBytes` (cutting UTF-8 text at a character boundary under a byte limit) with a pruv `str` function.

## 44. Performance check: why p03, p04 and p08 were 1.3–1.5× slower than Rust (2026-11-12)

Measured with `bench/harness/run.py bench/solutions/pruv_agg --pruv --backend {rust,llvm} --trust-callers`, n=10⁶ (p04/p10: 10⁶ queries), median of 21 runs per execution, p03/p04/p08 repeated 3 times (ratio = median of the three). The machine was loaded (`uptime` load average 3–5), so single values move by up to ±30%; Apple arm64 only.

| Problem | Rust backend sol/ref | LLVM backend sol/ref | Hand-written Rust (`rust_try1`) sol/ref |
|---|---|---|---|
| p03 `is_sorted` | 1.47 | 1.16 | ~1.0 (uses `for i in 1..len`) |
| p04 `binary_search` | 1.44 | 1.50 | ~1.6 (same `lo < hi` algorithm) |
| p08 `reverse` | 1.34 | 1.33 | 1.3–1.4 (`vec![0; n]` + index loop) |
| p01, p10 (controls) | 1.00, 1.02 | 1.0, 1.00 | |

Causes (evidence: generated `.rs`/`.ll`, `--emit asm`, micro-benchmarks):
- **p04 is the algorithm, not the translator.** The generated loop has no bounds or overflow checks and compiles to branch-free `csel`. The reference (`partition_point`) halves a `size`, so the loop-carried dependency does not go through the loaded value; the Pruv solution moves both `lo` and `hi`. The same Pruv source rewritten in the halving form ran 23.8 ms vs reference 26.6 ms (n=10⁶). Hand-written Rust with `lo < hi` is equally slow. Option is returned in registers (x0/x1) on the LLVM backend, so the FFI boundary is not the cause (this disproves the earlier "FFI cannot be inlined" guess).
- **p08 is the zero-initialized allocation.** `alloc(n, 0)` lowers to `vec![0; n]` (`__rust_alloc_zeroed`) on the Rust backend and to `pruv_alloc(len, 0)` → calloc in `pruvc/runtime.c` on the LLVM backend; the copy loop itself is NEON-vectorized like the reference. sol − ref ≈ 0.036 ms ≈ the measured calloc cost 0.033 ms. The reference uses an uninitialized `with_capacity` + `collect`.
- **p03 is the only translator-shaped cause, and only on the Rust backend.** `while i < len { if a[i-1] > a[i] { return false; } i = i + 1; }` (emitted as is by `pruvc/codegen.py` `While` and `pruv-core/src/codegen.rs` `Stmt::While`) is compiled by rustc into a loop that materializes the return value inside the loop with two branches; a hand-written `usize` while loop of the same shape is equally slow, while `for i in 1..len` matches the reference. The LLVM backend output already has the reference shape (`ldp` + one compare branch); the remaining 1.16× is within the noise of this loaded machine (unconfirmed).

Decisions:
- No compiler change in this batch. p04 needs no compiler change (the faster form is a different algorithm a user can write). p08 would need a language-level "allocate without initialization" or a proof that the whole array is overwritten; the gain is ~0.035 ms per 10⁶ elements, so it is deferred to G8/G5 (owned containers). p03 would need a `while` → `for` rewrite in the Rust-source backend only; the LLVM backend (the long-term path) does not need it, so it is deferred.
- Still open from the roadmap line: passing more proof facts (value ranges, non-overlap) to LLVM — not measured here because none of the three gaps came from missing facts.
- Not measured: a quiet machine, x86-64/Linux/Windows, default mode (without `--trust-callers`).

## 46. G3 Q: fuzzing, mutation, independent use (2026-11-12)

**Fuzzing (`bench/fuzz/run.py --g3`, commit below).** Template-based generator of generic programs (11 scenarios:
generic functions over `T`/`T: Element`, `Pair`/`Choice`/`Tagged`, `Option<X>`/`Result<X>` with X in i64, bool, Pair and
nested Option, nested explicit calls, `[T]` read/copy/store/alloc, abstract equality, guarded payloads, bounded forall,
`count`, loop invariants), about 30% deliberately defective but well-typed programs, an independent Python oracle,
at least 8 inputs per program, both backends and verified/safe modes.

| Seed | Compiler | Generated | Verified | Safe-only | Executions compared | Failures |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 41 | rs / py | 172 / 176 | 110 / 110 | 25 / 25 | 3920 / 3920 | 0 / 0 |
| 56 | rs / py | 180 / 181 | 110 / 110 | 25 / 25 | 3920 / 3920 | 0 / 0 |
| 57 | rs / py | 169 / 169 | 110 / 110 | 25 / 25 | 3920 / 3920 | 0 / 0 |

The result digests of the two compilers are identical per seed. "Meant to be unproved but verified": 1 each in seeds
56 and 57; the seed 56 case (`c56_40`, defect `drop_invariant`) dropped `invariant n == len(a)` for an immutable
`let n`, which the loop does not havoc, so the program is still correct (a harmless defect, not a soundness gap).
First run had 8 `oracle_mismatch` failures caused by the harness expecting the source name in `requires violated:`
while a specialized callee reports its instance name (`pruv_g3_f_9_set_first_1_i`); the G3 mode now compares only the
message kind.

**Mutation (`bench/g3/mutate_ops.py`, RESULTS commit d1862d1).** 581 mutants of the reference solutions, the 24 G3
proofs and the 22 G3 examples: 196 independently classified incorrect (11 by execution, 185 by rule), **0 incorrectly
verified**, 0 crashes, 0 Python/Rust verdict differences. 9 mutants change behavior in functions without `ensures`
(`main` prints, uncontracted helpers) and are reported as `out_of_contract`; a field swap that turns an ensures into a
tautology `x == x` is not counted as incorrect.

**Independent use (`bench/g3/PROBLEMS.md`, d4e7da9).** An author given only SPEC.md and the problem text in an isolated
folder (no compiler) solved all four problems on the first attempt: 16/16 problem runs (two compilers × two backends).
Limitation: it was not enforced that the author could read nothing outside that folder. SPEC §6c now states the two
points the author found unclear.

**G3a is complete** (A0–A5 in RESULTS §45, Q here). Remaining advisory items: boundary tests at the 4096/65536 limits,
repeated encoding during specialization.

## 47. G6 A0: foreign declarations, collection types and ownership checking — frontend (2026-11-12)

First G6 batch (`docs/G6_SPEC.md` §7.2, decisions in §10). Python reference first, Rust port in the same batch.

- **Syntax:** `trusted rust fn NAME(params) -> T [! effects] contracts = "::path";`, `take` collection parameters, `move x`. `trusted`, `rust`, `take`, `move` are contextual; the existing test function named `take` keeps working. `Vec`/`HashMap`/`String` become the catalog types only when no user type of that name is declared.
