- **Checker:** fixed catalog of 10 executable operations (`vec_new/push/get`, `map_new/get/insert`, `string_new/push_str/byte/to_str`) and 2 contract-only observers (`map_lookup`, `map_updated`); `len` on collections; contract-only `v[i]`/`s[i]`; foreign signature, path, owned-type, contract and export rules; an ownership pass (owned-live/dead/maybe, borrowed parameters, left-to-right arguments with a whole-call overlap scan first, joins, loop backedge restoration). New codes: `parse/foreign_syntax`, `type/foreign_{path,signature,owned_type,move,borrow,loop_move,contract,export}`.
- **CLI:** `--rust-extern NAME=PATH`, `--rust-search PATH`, `--foreign-checks requires|all|off` (`--no-verify` with `off` is a usage error). No later layer lowers G6 yet: after a successful check every G6 program stops with `codegen/unsupported_type`, also under `--no-verify`.
- **Tests:** 24 accepted frontend samples (`tests/g6/frontend/`, new run_all group `g6_frontend`, 2 modes = 48 rows) and 40 rejection files (`tests/must_reject/g6_*`, distribution exactly as §6.1). All passed on the first run of the Python implementation; one fixture was rewritten from `Vec<i64> | 0..=5` to `String | 0..=5` because the existing lexer rejects `> |` earlier.
- **Cross-check:** `tests/diff_parse.py --mode parse` and `--mode check` (new group `g6`) — 5622 inputs, all identical after one Rust fix (the checker's function signature table was built before collection-name normalization; "expected Vec<i64>, found Vec<i64>"). A lexer quirk found during the port (the `move` look-ahead could match characters inside a following `//` comment) was fixed in both compilers.
- **Regression:** run_all 1280/1280 (both compilers, both backends; rows regenerated: 1192 + 40 + 48), m3_all 12/12 stages, cargo test pass, inference 41/41.
- **Deviation from §7.2:** cleanup plans are not dumped in A0; they are produced where they are consumed (A2 Rust backend, A3 LLVM drop flags). The Rust `pruv-trace-dump` has no G6 guard yet (no G6 input reaches it in the current trace corpus); A1 replaces the guard with real verification.

## 48. G6 A1: verification with trusted contracts and collection models (2026-11-12)

Second G6 batch (`docs/G6_SPEC.md` §7.3, decisions in §11). Python reference first, Rust port in the same batch.

- **Pipeline:** verification now runs on G6 programs; the `codegen/unsupported_type` guard moved after verification (still right after the check under `--no-verify`) and into both code generators' entry points (the codegen comparison tools call the generators directly). Trace tools produce real traces for G6 programs.
- **Models:** `Vec<T>` is an array value exactly like G3 `[T]`; `String` is a u8 array like `str`; `HashMap<K,V>` is `(d: K→Bool, a: K→V, n)`. The ten catalog operations use exact terms (`Store`/`Select`/`If`) except `string_push_str` (fresh array with two quantified facts, like `substr`); `map_lookup`/`map_updated` are contract-only predicates with the frame relation. Reading an i64/u8 element assumes the machine-integer range, as array reads do.
- **Trusted declarations:** bodyless; requires and ensures are domain-checked only (no ensures proof); call sites prove requires (`call_requires`) and assume ensures, also for unit results. `--infer` never builds candidates mentioning collections.
- **Tests:** 24 positive proof programs (`tests/g6/proof/`, new run_all group `g6_proof`; all verify) covering every catalog transition, abstract `Vec<T>` helpers, i64/bool keys, entry snapshots of consumed parameters, map frames and loops; 20 reused-code rejections (`tests/must_reject/g6v_*`, distribution as §6.2). All passed on the first Python run after fixing two inverted signs in the worker's map-size encoding (`If(d[k], 0, 1)`; caught by review before running, confirmed by p06/p07).
- **Cross-check:** traces of 763 inputs: 733 identical, 30 `cex_only` (29 legacy as before; 1 new: `g6v_req_string_growth`, identical queries, different array contents in the counterexample model). Parse/check/Rust/LLVM dumps identical.
- **A0 audit (one review):** 4 required findings. Fixed in c539f96: nested calls did not share the outer call's move/borrow track (`read(v, eat(move v))` was accepted by both compilers), quadratic state lookups in the Rust ownership pass, `move move` reported `reserved_name`. The fourth (Rust trace tool without the A0 guard) became moot: A1 gives G6 programs real traces in both tools. Two new rejection files.
- **Regression:** run_all 1302/1302 (both compilers, both backends; rows regenerated), m3_all 12/12, cargo test 90 passed, inference 41/41. Legacy SMT traces are byte-identical to the Rust implementation, so existing proof results are unchanged.

## 49. G6 A2: Rust source backend and runtime boundary checks (2026-11-12)

Third G6 batch (`docs/G6_SPEC.md` §7.4, decisions in §12). Python reference first, Rust port in the same batch.

- **Lowering:** `Vec`/`HashMap`/`String` become `::std` collections; borrowed collection parameters are references, `take` parameters and results are owned, `move x` is a Rust move, and Rust's RAII performs the drops (the Pruv ownership checker only accepts programs whose moves Rust also accepts, so drop counts balance by construction on branches, loops, reassignment and early returns). Collection-bearing functions are private. Each catalog instance and each `trusted rust fn` becomes one private boundary helper (`pruv_g6_<op>__<enc>` / the declaration's name), emitted once in first-use order; trusted calls run inside `catch_unwind` (`pruv: foreign panic: NAME`).
- **Check modes:** `--foreign-checks requires` (default) checks foreign/catalog preconditions at run time also in verified builds; `all` adds postconditions (catalog updates compare a `.clone()` snapshot with the actual result; work is charged to the contract budget); `off` checks nothing but keeps checked indexing. Runtime `requires` of Pruv functions gained collection observers (`len`, `v[i]`, `s[i]`, `map_lookup`, `map_updated`).
- **Manifest:** programs using G6 also get `<out>.foreign.json` (`pruv-foreign-v1`: check mode, trusted declarations with contracts and reachability, catalog instances, extern crates).
- **Tests:** 24 runtime fixtures (`tests/runtime/g6_*`: 4 adapter, 6 Vec, 6 map, 4 String, 4 ownership/order) × 6 flag rows, plus 4 injected-wrong-Rust fixtures using an adapter crate (`tests/runtime/g6_liar.rs`): a false ensures passes silently under `requires`/`off` and is caught only under `all`; an adapter panic is reported as `pruv: foreign panic:`; a bad input under `--no-verify --trust-callers` hits `pruv: foreign requires violated:`. The runtime harness gained `rust_externs` (builds an rlib per fixture and passes `--rust-extern`/`--extern`). The LLVM rows of these fixtures are pending (A3). All fixtures passed on the first Python run except one that used the contract-only `is_some` in code (fixture fixed).
- **Cross-check:** `tests/diff_rust.py` all same (default mode), and every G6 sample × 5 check/verify modes compared directly (generated Rust, `.foreign.json`, diagnostics): identical except counterexample values on two frontend samples that are not meant to verify.
- **Regression:** run_all 1618/1618 (both compilers, both backends; rows regenerated), m3_all 12/12, cargo test 91 passed, inference 41/41.
- **A1 audit closure:** the one required finding was the new `cex_only` trace of `g6v_req_string_growth` (identical queries, different array contents in the counterexample). It is the same class as the 29 legacy `cex_only` inputs and is accepted; no A1 code change.
- **Known cost:** `all` mode re-checks the whole prefix on every `vec_push`/`string_push_str`, so repeated appends are quadratic under `all` (by design, §8 item 5; measured in Q).

## 50. G6 A3: LLVM backend through a Rust shim (2026-11-12)

Fourth G6 batch (`docs/G6_SPEC.md` §7.5, decisions in §13). Python reference first, Rust port in the same batch.

- **Bridge:** for G6 programs the generated Rust bindings file gains `mod pruv_g6_shim`: the same A2 boundary helpers (same check mode and messages) plus one `#[no_mangle] extern "C"` adapter per helper, typed `len`/`drop` per collection type, `string_bytes`, and the `pruv_g6_bridge_v1` marker that every G6 LLVM function references (linking without the shim fails). Collections cross as pointers to boxed Rust collections; consuming updates replace the boxed value in place and return the same pointer (no allocation per update); every adapter runs under `catch_unwind` and exits with 101 after flushing C stdio, so no Rust unwinding crosses LLVM frames.
- **LLVM code:** each owned collection binding is a null-initialized slot; `move` nulls the slot; assignment drops the old value after evaluating the right side; scope exits, loop iterations and every `ret` drop the non-null slots innermost first; fresh collections used only in borrow positions are statement temporaries. Collection-bearing functions are internal.
- **Subset:** i64/bool elements, keys and values, `String`, trusted signatures over i64/bool/arrays/str with i64/bool/unit results. Other G6 on LLVM stays `codegen/unsupported_type` (Option/Result in trusted signatures, non-scalar elements: 3 runtime fixtures remain pending). The canonical driver's `--emit exe|obj` reports `codegen/foreign_target` for G6 programs; the supported build is the Rust host build (bindings + LLVM object linked by rustc).
- **Tests:** the A2 runtime fixtures now run on LLVM too (all modes; 457 LLVM runtime rows pass on the Python compiler), including the injected-wrong-Rust fixtures (false ensures caught only under `all`, panic, requires violation) and the budget fixture. A live-box counter in the shim (`PRUV_G6_COUNT=1`, new harness field `g6_drop_balance`) shows 0 live collections at exit for every collection fixture on every mode.
- **Cross-check:** `tests/diff_llvm.py` all same; every G6 runtime/proof sample × 5 modes compared directly (IR, bindings with shim, diagnostics): identical.
- **A2 audit (one review + one confirmation):** R1 — a `String` parameter indexed in a runtime `requires` referenced `pruv_cidx_str` without emitting it; R2 — `all` mode did not charge single-element checks to the budget. Both fixed in b1072ad. The confirmation found the R1 fixture did not reproduce the original defect (a string literal already pulled in the helper); it was rewritten without any `str` and now fails on the old code and passes on the fixed code.
- **Deferred (13.6):** bundle digest symbol, aggregate payloads across the bridge, native exe/obj linking through rustc, `--rust-search`, the 16-probe ABI suite and non-host targets.
- **Regression:** run_all 1624/1624 on both compilers and both backends (rows regenerated), m3_all 12/12, cargo test 91 passed, inference 41/41.

## 51. G6 A4: release integration (2026-11-12)

- **User documentation:** SPEC.md §6d (one page): trust, collections and their operations, ownership (`take`/`move`), run-time check modes, the two build paths and their limits, new error codes. Its code sample verifies on both compilers.
- **Examples:** `examples/g6_min_clamp`, `g6_vec_running_sum`, `g6_histogram`, `g6_string_build` verify and build on both compilers and both backends in all three example modes (examples group 344/344).
- **A3 audit (one review):** one required finding — a `--no-verify` LLVM build of a collection-bearing function whose `requires` uses `v[i]`, `s[i]`, `map_lookup` or `map_updated` is rejected with `codegen/unsupported_type` (the LLVM run-time requires evaluator has no collection observers). Resolved by scoping: it is now listed outside the LLVM subset (`docs/G6_SPEC.md` §13.1, SPEC.md §6d); the Rust backend supports it, and the rejection is an explicit diagnostic. Advisory recorded in §13.6: under `all`, LLVM does not re-compare the bytes of `string_to_str` (the copy is made from the borrowed bytes by the existing `pruv_copy`). No leaks or double drops were found on the reviewed paths.
- **Rejection inventory:** 42 frontend + 20 reused-code G6 `must_reject` files (≥ 60 required) plus the A2 runtime trust-violation fixtures.
- **Regression:** run_all 1648/1648 on both compilers and both backends (rows regenerated), m3_all 12/12 (all twelve stages run over the G6 inputs; no separate foreign stage was added because parse/check/trace/rust/llvm already cover them).

## 52. G6 Q: fuzzing, mutation, independent use — G6 complete (2026-11-12)

- **Fuzzing** (`bench/fuzz/run.py --g6`, own generator stream `g6:<seed>:<index>`, oracle extended with Vec/HashMap/String, take/move, the catalog and trusted `::std::cmp::min/max`): seeds 41/56/57 × both compilers, 110 verified + 25 safe-only programs per seed, all four modes (rust/llvm × proof/safe). Executions compared: 4,032 / 4,016 / 4,080 per compiler and seed; **0 failures**. Per seed the two compilers produce the same result digest. 6–10 generated programs per seed that were meant to verify were rejected (`ensures` or solver unknown), and 4–8 deliberately weakened programs verified anyway; the latter all matched the oracle on every input (the weakening was harmless), so none is a wrong proof.
- **Mutation** (`bench/g6/mutate_ops.py`, 75 mutants of the four reference solutions and the q04 adapter): 41 incorrect Pruv computations → 40 rejected by the prover, 1 equivalent (m18: reading the queried key's count while updating another key never changes the queried entry; the expectation was wrong, not the prover); 21 incorrect foreign results/contracts → 7 rejected by the prover (contradictory or inconsistent contracts), 10 caught at run time under `--foreign-checks all`, 2 equivalent, 2 **undetectable trust violations** (an adapter with a hidden global counter, an adapter that writes to stderr — correct results, so no contract or check can see the effect lie); 13 ownership faults → all rejected by the checker. **0 wrong but verified, 0 crashes, 0 Python/Rust differences.**
- **Reference solutions** (`bench/g6/solutions/ref`): q01–q04 verify and pass every test on py/rs × rust/llvm (16/16).
- **Independent use** (a worker given only SPEC.md and `bench/g6/PROBLEMS.md`): first attempt **3/4** (12/16 runs; `solutions/indep1`). q01 (Vec filter/accumulate), q03 (UTF-8 String assembly) and q04 (external Rust parser adapter, scalar result, built with `--rust-extern`) passed on all four configurations. q02 (HashMap histogram) failed: an invariant over every i64 key made the solver time out. One repair attempt with the error message and a hint (talk only about the queried key) also failed (`solutions/indep1_repair/q02.pruv`): the invariant then cannot bound the counts of the other keys, so `count + 1` is not provably free of overflow. The reference avoids this with a saturating update. **Lesson:** a map whose values grow needs either a bound on all values (expensive to state) or a saturating update; this belongs in the user documentation of collections (follow-up).
- **G6 status:** A0–A4 and Q done. Deferred items are listed in `docs/G6_SPEC.md` §13.6 (LLVM: aggregate payloads, Option/Result in trusted signatures, collection observers in `--no-verify` requires, native exe/obj link through rustc, bundle digest; manifest on the LLVM path).
- **A4 audit:** confirmed the A3 scoping; four statements of SPEC.md §6d did not match the compilers (type arguments of `string_*`/`len`, manifest only on the Rust backend, `string_to_str` under `all` on LLVM, `foreign_target` only in the Rust compiler); all four corrected.

## 53. G6 Q audit fixes (2026-11-12)

- **Audit of the G6 Q tooling** (fuzz `--g6`, `bench/g6`): three required findings, all fixed.
  - The mutation classifier ran only one compiler on the Rust backend. It now runs every accepting compiler on both backends (py/rs × rust/llvm), and the worst configuration decides the class (`runs`, `config_diff` in `bench/g6/results/mutants.json`).
  - A mutant counted as caught at run time whenever the `--foreign-checks all` run failed. It now needs a `pruv: foreign` diagnostic. A failure without one counts as wrong but verified, and a timeout counts as a crash.
  - The fuzz oracle deep-copied a vector twice on every append. Constructor results are now copied once (repeated appends are still quadratic in total, with half the copying).
- **Harness bug found by the rerun:** on LLVM, a foreign panic ends the process (exit 101), and its message comes from the panic hook. The test template had silenced that hook, so the diagnostic was lost and 7 caught mutants looked wrong or undetectable on LLVM only. The template now prints the panic message to stderr. `mutate_ops.py` also rejects unknown options; it used to take them as the output path.
- **Rerun with the stricter classification** (75 mutants × up to 4 configurations): the same result as §52. 47 rejected by the prover, 13 by the checker, 10 caught at run time, 2 undetectable trust violations, 3 equivalent. **0 wrong but verified, 0 crashes, 0 differences between configurations.**
- **Confirmation audit:** R1, R3 and the harness fix confirmed. R2 still had one path: tests failing without any `pruv: foreign` diagnostic were classified as an undetectable trust violation. They are now wrong but verified, the self-test checks six `exec_class` cases, and option checking also covers `--list`/`--self-test`. No saved run took that path, so the results above are unchanged.
- **q02 lesson** added to SPEC.md §6d: a map whose values grow needs a saturating update or an invariant that bounds every value.
- **G9 design** written: `docs/G9_SPEC.md` covers recursion with `decreases`, recursive types with automatic boxing and `size`, and buffer fields. It plans batches A0–A4 and Q.

## 54. G9 A0: frontend for recursion, recursive types and buffer fields (2026-11-12)

- **Parser:** a function may carry a `decreases` clause after its contracts (`Function.decreases`).
- **Checker** (both compilers, byte-identical dumps):
  - Function call graph SCCs (Tarjan). A recursive function needs `decreases` (`recursive_decreases`); recursion that changes generic arguments is rejected (`recursive_polymorphism`).
  - Type graph SCCs with inhabitance. Recursive types are allowed when they are inhabited and not generic (`recursive_generic_type`); the boxed edges and the extended set (types that hold a recursive type or a buffer field) are recorded in `Program.g9`.
  - The `size` builtin, the `alloc` effect for building recursive values, buffer field ownership (a buffer field takes only a fresh value), recursive borrows in `match`, and equality on extended types.
  - The old `type_recursion` and `no_recursion` rejections are gone.
- **Guard:** later layers are not written yet. A program that uses a G9 construct stops after type checking with `unsupported_type` ("... not implemented by the verifier / rust codegen layer / llvm codegen layer yet"). `run_all` expects that message for the `g9_frontend` group (`G9_GUARD = True`); A2 flips it. The diff tools apply the same guard.
- **Fixtures:** 37 frontend samples (`tests/g9/frontend`, 31 new programs and 6 migrated ones), 42 new must-reject pairs, and `tests/g9/legacy_migrations.json` (10 old rejections that are now accepted or reclassified). Four old must-reject pairs (`g1_array_field`, `g1_recursive_indirect`, `g2a_array_field`, `g2b_field`) moved to the frontend group; three old recursion fixtures now expect the new codes.
- **Results:** must_reject 360/360, g9_frontend 37/37 on both compilers; diff_parse (parse, check) all same with coverage pass; diff_trace, diff_rust, diff_llvm all same; m3_all 12/12; cargo test 91; inference 41/41; run_all rows 1727 with Rust both backends 1098/1098.

## 55. G9 A1: verification of recursion, recursive types and buffer fields (2026-11-12)

- **Model** (both compilers, same Z3 symbols and assertion order): each nominal type `D` gets `size!D` and, for recursive types, `wf!D` as plain uninterpreted functions. Their structural definition is asserted only as ground one-step equations on observed terms (size >= 1, the one-step size and wf equations, child size >= 1). Z3 recursive-function definitions are not used: their unfolding timed out on the tree and JSON fixtures. Fewer facts can only lose proofs, never soundness. Array and string fields are buffer sorts (`buf!...`) with field get/put.
- **Termination:** a recursive call must make the `decreases` measure strictly smaller and keep it non-negative (`recursive_decrease`, `recursive_nonneg`). The measure may use `size(x)`.
- **Counterexamples** print recursive values with a `<truncated>` marker past 64 nodes or depth 16.
- **Guard:** the G9 guard now runs after verification, so G9 programs are proved first and then stop at the Rust codegen layer. `run_all` expects that for the new `g9_proof` group.
- **Fixtures:** 32 proof samples (`tests/g9/proof`, trees, JSON numbers, buffers, mutual and generic recursion) and 32 new must-reject pairs (`g9p_*`, all rejected by the verifier).
- **Results:** g9_proof 32/32, g9_frontend 37/37, must_reject 392/392 on both compilers; Python full suite 1791/1791 (both backends); diff_trace all same on every group (4 + 14 counterexample-value-only differences); diff_compilers 2066 same, 0 diff; diff_llvm (g9, must_reject, examples) and diff_parse all same; Rust full suite 1791/1791 (both backends, rows compare clean); m3_all 12/12; cargo test 91; inference 41/41; run_all rows 1791.

## 56. G9 A2: Rust source backend (2026-11-12)

- **A1 audit fixes** (verifier, both compilers): observed terms are cached by id and a field access observes its parent first. A generic application's weight is a constant when its size does not depend on the value (`fixed_size`); otherwise it is a per-instance uninterpreted size with positivity only. This removed a Z3 crash on deeply nested generic sorts (p34). New proof fixtures p33–p35.
- **Code generation:** extended types derive `Clone` and `Debug`, and recursive edges become `Box<...>`. Values are read through `&T` views and owning positions clone explicitly. Runtime `requires` that use `size` or `==` call generated helpers that charge a contract budget per visited node. Types are emitted in SCC-condensed order. The Rust guard is lifted; LLVM stays guarded until A3.
- **A2 audit fixes:** `fixed_size` adds sizes as unbounded decimal numbers (no i64 wrap). Constructors with payload are allowed as aggregate operands in runtime contracts (real temporaries, i64 fields narrowed with a check). Subfields of a non-extended payload inside an extended value are read from the original reference, and a nominal value converted whole is charged to the contract budget first. The size helpers sum field weights with a linear accumulator. New proof fixtures p36, p37.
- **Fixtures:** runtime tests g9_r01–g9_r25.
- **Results:** both compilers emit the same Rust source; `run_all --backend rust` 1267/1267 with each compiler; m3_all 12/12; cargo test 91; inference 41/41. A 64-level nested generic example hits a Z3 limit and is covered by a unit test instead.

## 57. G9 A3: LLVM backend (2026-11-12)

- **Code generation** (both compilers, byte-identical IR): recursive types become heap nodes, with generated drop, clone, size and equality helpers. Reads borrow, and contract walks are metered by the same budget as on the Rust backend. The G9 LLVM guards are removed.
- **A2 confirmation audit fixes:** `variant_field` and field reads on constructor values no longer copy the whole value, and constructor copies inside contracts are charged to the contract budget (runtime tests g9_r26, g9_r27).
- **Fixtures:** LLVM runtime tests g9_l01–g9_l10, and an allocation balance check (every allocation freed) on the existing G9 runtime tests.
- **Results:** LLVM output the same from both compilers; `run_all --backend both` 2051/2051 with each compiler; m3_all 12/12; cargo test passes. Constructors with payload as aggregate operands of a runtime `requires` were still Rust-only (listed in `tests/llvm_pending.txt`); see §59.

## 58. G9 A4: user documentation and examples (2026-11-12)

- **SPEC.md §6e** explains `decreases`, recursive functions and types, `size`, and buffer fields for users.
- **Examples:** eight `examples/g9_*` programs that run on both backends, and a proof test for the SPEC tree example. `run_all` rows regenerated.
- **Results:** examples 48/48; CI (including windows-abi) passes.

## 59. G9 A3 audit fixes and G9 Q: mutants, fuzzing and independent use (2026-11-12)

- **A3 audit fixes** (both compilers, byte-identical output): on the LLVM backend, constructors with payload are real temporaries as aggregate operands of a runtime `requires` (i64 fields narrowed with a checked conversion, buffers charged their length then copied, temporaries dropped per clause, per short-circuit right side and per quantifier body). Field paths below `variant_field` read only the selected leaf, and whole non-extended payloads are charged to the contract budget before conversion. The payload index is memoized (no quadratic helper generation). On the Rust backend a buffer field operand is evaluated once. Runtime tests g9_l11, g9_l12, g9_r28; the r26/r27 entries are removed from `tests/llvm_pending.txt`.
- **Problems and solutions:** four problems in `bench/g9/PROBLEMS.md` (array recursive search, tree traversal with size termination, bounded JSON-like descent with a cursor, nested buffer copying with independence assertions) with graders in `bench/g9/run.py`. Reference solutions and an independent author's solutions (SPEC and problem statements only) both pass every grader test on all four configurations (py/rs compiler x rust/llvm backend). The independent author solved 4/4 on the first attempt with no diagnostic-driven revision.
- **Source mutants** (`bench/g9/mutate_ops.py`): 227 mutants, 151 incorrect, 69 correct, 7 contract-weak (fail the full tests but keep every stated contract, helpers included; §15.1). All 151 incorrect mutants are rejected by the verifier on all four configurations; wrong-but-proved 0. Compiler fault injections are covered by the existing differential, runtime and model tests and are not rerun here.
- **Fuzzing** (`bench/fuzz/run.py --g9`): seeds 41, 56 and 57, 100 programs each, on both compilers: 0 backend/mode mismatches, 0 oracle mismatches, 0 unsound results; the result digests are identical between the compilers. A few programs meant to verify were rejected (2, 2, 3; `verify:ensures`, solver incompleteness) and one program meant to be unproved verified (seed 57); neither is a soundness issue.
- **Measurements** (reference solutions, this Mac): compile with proofs 0.02-0.08 s (Rust compiler) and 0.07-0.16 s (Python compiler), without proofs 0.01 s and 0.04 s; generated Rust source 4.1-6.6 KB and LLVM IR 7.8-14.0 KB, the same from both compilers. Clone allocations are checked for balance (every allocation freed) by the G9 runtime tests; per-call clone counts, traversal time and a legacy latency comparison were not measured in this batch.
- **Q audit fixes:** a runtime `requires` operand that copies a named value whose type can hold an array or str field is rejected (`codegen/unsupported_type`, fixture `g9_contract_buffer_copy`) instead of being charged one budget unit per node while its buffers are copied unmetered. The fuzz oracle's `size` walks `Some`/`Ok` payloads. The q01-q04 graders compare each fixed wrapper signature and contract exactly (only a trailing `decreases` may follow). A mutant is contract-weak only when it edits no signature or contract line and the reference interpreter (`bench/fuzz/oracle.py`) finds every function contract, helpers included, satisfied on all test inputs; m087, m090 and m181 break the `items_end` ensures and are now incorrect (all rejected). A confirmation review found that the interpreter did not evaluate function-level `decreases`; it now checks, opt-in for this grading, that each call inside a recursive SCC has a defined, nonnegative callee measure below the caller's entry measure, and the oracle step limit for this check was raised so q01's 1000-element inputs finish. The counts are unchanged. `holds_buffer` is memoized in both compilers (a synthetic 800-field case: Python LLVM codegen 0.48 s before, 0.13 s after).
- **Results:** `run_all --backend both` 2063/2063 with each compiler (2064/2064 after the Q audit fixes); diff_compilers 1166 same, 0 diff; diff_llvm, diff_rust and diff_trace all same; m3_all ok; cargo test passes.

## 60. G4 A0-A2: modules, visibility, host entries and the Rust backend (2026-11-12)

- **Design:** `docs/G4_SPEC.md` (batches A0-A3 and Q). A root that contains `mod` or a top-level `pub` switches the whole program to explicit visibility; any other program keeps its exact legacy output (checked byte for byte against the pre-G4 compiler).
- **A0 frontend** (`b5418be`): declared files load root-first depth-first, absolute `crate::` paths resolve, private items and private dependencies of public interfaces are rejected, and diagnostics carry a `file` field. 24 new G4 fixtures.
- **A1 verification** (`b5418be`): every definition is verified; callers see only contracts, contracts resolve in their defining module, and specializations keep full declaration identity. In explicit mode, unit calls also assume `ensures`. Legacy SMT input is unchanged.
- **A2 Rust backend** (`5eacae3`): one flat Rust file with length-encoded stems (`pruv_m_f_`/`pruv_m_t_`), source spelling for root entries and root public types, checked wrappers only on host entries (verified mode) or on every call with `requires` (`--no-verify`). Ten runtime projects g4_r01-g4_r10.
- **Audit fixes:** A1 audit (foreign-declaration diagnostics without `file`, a `pub` hidden before an invalid character, canonical file identity, repeated public-interface walks) and A2 audit (G6 helper names built from unencoded `crate::` spellings, per-call argument copies in the Rust mono pass on legacy programs, a `pub` before an invalid string escape missed by the explicit-mode sniff; must-reject fixture g4_r25).
- **Results:** two-compiler output 2480/2480 the same at A2; `run_all` 2164/2164 with each compiler; CI passes for `b5418be` and `5eacae3`.

## 61. G4 A3: LLVM backend for module programs (2026-11-12)

- **Code generation** (both compilers, byte-identical IR and bindings): module programs use the same stems, specialization identities, order and entry set as the Rust backend. Bodies and safe-mode checked wrappers are internal; `pruv_abi_<entry>` is emitted only for root host entries, and `--rust-bindings` exposes only entries and the nominal types their signatures reach. Same-spelled types in different modules keep distinct LLVM types and helpers. The native executable shim calls the root `main` entry. Every G4 guard and every G4 row in `tests/llvm_pending.txt` is removed.
- **Fixtures:** runtime projects g4_r01-g4_r10 now also run on LLVM with an expected export list; new projects g4_r11 (entry type closure), g4_r12 (raw bool/str validation under verified and `--trust-callers` modes), g4_r13 (same-spelled layouts), g4_r14 (root versus child `main`); nested example `examples/g4_backend/`; must-reject g4_r25. SPEC.md §6f documents the user rules.
- **Native executable:** on this Mac (arm64), the Rust compiler's `--emit exe` for g4_r14 runs and prints 7 and 8; the binary exports only `pruv_abi_answer` and `pruv_abi_main`. No cross-target emission was checked in this batch.
- **Results:** diff_compilers 2510 same, 0 diff; diff_llvm same; cargo test passes; `run_all --backend both` 2193/2193 with each compiler (row list regenerated, +29 rows).

## 62. G4 A3 audit fixes and G4 Q: fuzzing, mutants, fault injection and independent use (2026-11-12)

- **A3 audit fixes** (both compilers, byte-identical output): the explicit-module sniff lexes the source once and keeps the tokens read before the first lex error (`lex(src, toks)` / `lex_into`); it used to retry and was quadratic (2000 repeated `pub `: Python 2.35 s before, 0.0024 s after; the Python and Rust errors match for 2000 and 20000 repetitions). Accepted change: tokens before a lex error count, so `mod "unterminated` is explicit mode. Foreign helper naming in the Rust compiler borrows the safe type when nothing changes (`safe_type_cow`). A confirmation review found nothing (20037 sniff inputs equal before and after in Python).
- **Problems and solutions:** two problems in `bench/g4/PROBLEMS.md` (q01 bounded array service split over `store` and `store::check`; q02 recursive tree service in `tree` and `tree::shape` with scalar host wrappers), grader `bench/g4/run.py`. The reference solutions pass 8/8 problem runs (q01 1854/1854 tests, q02 93/93, py/rs compiler x rust/llvm backend).
- **Independent use** (a worker given only SPEC.md and `bench/g4/PROBLEMS.md`, no compiler): first attempt **2/2**, 8/8 problem runs, no revision (`bench/g4/solutions/indep1`, notes in its NOTES.md). The author had to assume that `size` counts `Empty` as 1 and that `pub enum` is written `pub enum T {...}`; both assumptions were right.
- **Source mutants** (`bench/g4/mutate_ops.py`): 124 mutants, 67 incorrect, 51 correct, 6 contract-weak. All 67 incorrect mutants are rejected by the verifier on all four configurations; wrong-but-proved 0. The 25 contract mutants are all rejected. One mutant first labelled a contract change (m019, `range_sum` drops `len(a) <= 1000`) is correct: array parameters already have `len(a) <= 2^40` (SPEC §6) and `2^40 * 10^6` fits in i64, so it was relabelled. One correct mutant is rejected (m030, the explicit store of the value `alloc` already filled is removed; the loop-entry invariant is not proved): a completeness gap, not a soundness issue.
- **Compiler fault injections** (Python compiler, `Lair Outputs` scratch scripts, not committed): disabling the private-item check, naming symbols by the last path component only, dropping runtime `requires` checks of host entries, and skipping the decreases check for calls across modules were each caught by existing tests (must_reject 416/418, runtime 1167/1196, runtime 1192/1196, must_reject 417/418).
- **Fuzzing** (`bench/fuzz/run.py --g4`): seeds 41, 56 and 57, 30 verified programs each (52 generated), 1056 executions per seed, on both compilers: 0 backend/mode, oracle, unsound or twin mismatches (twin pairs compared 2, 4, 7); 0 programs meant to verify were rejected; the result digests are identical between the compilers.
- **Measurements** (100-file scale fixture, 99 modules + root, 1822 lines, median of 3, this Mac): with proofs Python 0.63 s (LLVM) and 2.5 s (Rust backend; minimum 0.62 s), Rust compiler 0.87 s and 1.65 s; without proofs Python 0.10-0.11 s, Rust compiler 0.02-0.03 s.
- **Results:** `run_all --backend both` 2193/2193 with each compiler; diff_compilers 2510 same, 0 diff; diff_llvm same; CI passes. **G4 complete.**

## 63. G5: inout parameters, exclusive access and `old(a)` (A0-A3 and Q, 2026-11-12)

- **Design:** `docs/G5_SPEC.md` (`ae1d7bd`). An `inout` array parameter (`[i64]`, `[u8]`, `[bool]`, `[T: Element]`) is changed in place; an inout call is the whole expression of a statement, the places it passes must be exclusive, and `old(a)` in contracts names the entry contents. Programs without `inout` keep their exact output.
- **A0 frontend** (`1c92a28`, audit fixes `c803810`): parsing and checking in both compilers, exclusivity diagnostics (`inout_alias` and others), foreign functions and host entries reject `inout`, a staging guard until the backends exist. 16 frontend roots, 33 must-reject roots.
- **A1 verification** (`bc4b560`, audit fixes `12c857d`, `9dc4eb4`): entry snapshots for `old(a)`; a call freezes the old contents and havocs a same-length final array (u8 domain kept), then assumes `ensures`; loops count inout call operands as written; inference treats inout calls as writes. The audits made the inout scans look only at the top-level statement call (they had slowed inout-free programs). 16 proof roots, 18 must-reject cases.
- **A2 Rust backend** (`071a72b`, audit fix `fc91e0e`): an inout parameter is `&mut [T]`, calls pass a mutable borrow or reborrow. Runtime fixtures g5_r01-g5_r12, three examples. The specializer now keeps the `uses_inout` flag so inout-free generic programs skip the inout codegen scans.
- **A3 LLVM backend** (`cbe33cc`): inout parameters are `noalias nocapture` pointers (never `readonly`), stores go to the caller's buffer, host wrappers keep the copy-in boundary. SPEC.md §6g documents the user rules. Runtime rows g5_r12-g5_r20 on both backends, three more examples.
- **Problems and solutions:** two problems in `bench/g5/PROBLEMS.md` (q01 bounded byte-range transformation over `[u8]`; q02 module-based recursive array transformation over `[i64]`), grader `bench/g5/run.py`. The reference solutions pass 8/8 problem runs (q01 1056/1056 tests, q02 151/151, py/rs compiler x rust/llvm backend).
- **Independent use** (a worker given only SPEC.md and `bench/g5/PROBLEMS.md`, no compiler): first attempt **2/2**, 8/8 problem runs, 0 revisions (`bench/g5/solutions/indep1`, notes in its NOTES.md).
- **Compiler fault injections** (`bench/g5/faults/`, Python compiler, each patch applied alone to a disposable copy): disabled exclusivity (f1, g5_reject 60/68), `old(a)` bound to current contents (f2, g5_proof 19/25), no havoc after an inout call (f3, 22/25), loops forgetting call writes (f4, 24/25), stale overlay of inout parameters (f5, 5/25) and `readonly` kept on the LLVM inout pointer (f6, attribute check fails) were each caught.
- **Fuzzing** (`bench/fuzz/run.py --g5`): seeds 41, 56 and 57, 33 verified programs each (51-52 generated), 1056 comparisons per seed, on both compilers (6336 in total): 0 backend/mode, oracle, unsound or twin mismatches; every probed program (12 per seed) also verifies with `--infer` and gives the same Rust as the explicit version; 0 programs meant to verify were rejected; the result digests are identical between the compilers.
- **Source mutants:** `bench/g5/mutate_ops.py`, 144 mutants of the two reference solutions (104 incorrect by their tests, 40 correct), run with both compilers and both backends: **wrong_but_proved 0** in every configuration. 101 incorrect mutants are rejected by the verifier in all four configurations; the other three (m091, m104, m105) end in `verify/verify_unknown` in at least one configuration (a solver unknown is a rejection without a counterexample, not an acceptance). Of the 40 correct mutants, 12 are accepted and 28 are rejected because a changed contract no longer proves at a caller or in the body (for example m065 drops `c <= 255`, which the overflow check needs). Results in `bench/g5/results/mutants.json`.
- **Measurements:** `bench/g5/perf.py` (G5_SPEC §14 item 7), reversing 1,000,000 `i64` in place versus a copy-returning version: the inout version does no allocation per operation (0 allocations, 0 bytes; median 0.21 ms on both backends) where the copy version allocates 8,000,000 bytes per call (median 0.49 ms Rust, 0.42 ms LLVM); peak RSS 17.7 MB versus 25.7 MB. A scan of the generated code finds no copy or allocation call in the inout reversal functions on either backend. Results in `bench/g5/results/perf.json`.
- **Results:** `run_all --backend both` 2502/2502 with each compiler. The A3 audit found no miscompilation and byte-identical IR and bindings on 44/44 programs; its one should-fix (SPEC.md §6g claimed safe mode checks every internal call under `--trust-callers`, while only calls to inout functions are checked that way) is fixed in the text.

**G5 complete.**

## 64. G10: files with the `fs` effect (A0-A3 and Q, 2026-11-12)

- **Design:** `docs/G10_SPEC.md` (`77d0dc2`). `File` is an owned handle with the `fs` effect; `file_open_read`, `file_create`, `file_ok`, `file_readable`, `file_size`, `file_byte`, `file_read_all`, `file_write_bytes`, `file_write_str` and `file_close` are catalog operations whose contracts track the open mode and readability, so reading a created file, writing an opened one, reading without the `file_readable` check and `file_byte` past `file_size` fail to verify.
- **A0+A1 frontend and verification** (`342dbef`): the `fs` effect, ownership rules for handles (use after close, double close, branch and loop moves, borrow then consume, owned placement, host entries), must_reject roots for each rule family, and the proof encoding of section 4.
- **A2 Rust backend** (`c0960f5`): handles become a runtime struct in the generated Rust; runtime fixtures g10_r01 onward.
- **A3 LLVM backend** (`7300398`): files go through the G6 Rust shim in the generated bindings (File adapter, File drop, file observers collected from contracts, the `file_byte` bound guarded by `file_size`). The A3 audit found that the runtime struct name `PruvFile` collided with a legal user struct; it is now `pruv_File` (regression in g10_r03). Native `--emit exe|obj` rejects File programs with `codegen/foreign_target`. SPEC.md §6h documents the user rules.
- **Problems and solutions:** two problems in `bench/g10/PROBLEMS.md` (q01 byte transformation copying one file to another with status codes; q02 line and field counter with a size bound), grader `bench/g10/run.py`. The reference solutions pass 8/8 problem runs (q01 40/40 tests, q02 18/18, py/rs compiler x rust/llvm backend).
- **Independent use** (a worker given only SPEC.md and `bench/g10/PROBLEMS.md`, no compiler): first attempt **2/2**, 8/8 problem runs, 0 revisions (`bench/g10/solutions/indep1`).
- **Compiler fault injections** (`bench/g10/faults/`, Python compiler, each patch alone in a disposable copy): use after close, double close, branch move, loop move, borrow then consume, owned placement, host entry and the catalog preconditions (section 4 item 4) are each caught by named rejection tests with clean baselines; seven are caught as accepted invalid programs, owned placement as an internal error (a diagnostic regression, not acceptance).
- **Source mutants:** `bench/g10/mutate_ops.py`, 44 explicit edits of the reference solutions (wrong mode, unchecked read, use after close through a renamed binding, missing close before reopen, off-by-one `file_byte`, transformation, size bounds), on both backends with the Python compiler: **wrong_but_proved 0**; 36 rejected at verification and 6 at static checking per backend, the 2 controls pass. Removing `file_close` alone cannot be required to fail (implicit close is allowed), so the missing-close mutants also make an invalid claim about the reopened handle.
- **Measurement:** `bench/g10/perf.py`, reading and summing a 10 MB file against hand-written Rust (`File::open` + `read_to_end`): about 2.1-2.2x on the Rust backend and 20.7-22.4x on the LLVM backend. The 1.5x target is **not met**: each `file_byte`/`file_size` helper call sets the thread-local foreign-contract context and the checked sum does not vectorize; on LLVM each byte is a non-inlined shim call through the panic guard. A fast byte path is deferred (G10_SPEC §9).
- **Results:** `run_all --backend both` 2661/2661 with the Python compiler; the Rust compiler's row list is identical (2661 rows); py/rs outputs byte-identical on the G10 rows.

**G10 files complete** (processes, network and async remain for later G10 parts with G7 and Stage 5).

## 65. G7: scoped parallel calls with `par` groups (A0-A3 and Q, 2026-11-13)

- **Design:** `docs/G7_SPEC.md` (`104676e`). `par { f(inout a, x); g(inout b, x); }` runs two or more calls as scoped workers joined before the statement ends; arguments are captured at group entry, written arrays must be disjoint across branches, and reachable foreign, catalog or nested `par` calls are rejected.
- **A0 frontend** (`257d61e`): parsing, branch shape and argument rules, write/write and write/read alias checks, effect and eligibility rules, 24 must_reject roots.
- **A1 verification** (`e0e3e66`): every branch's requires and termination are proved against the group-entry state, all inout finals are havocked, then every branch's ensures is assumed in the joined state.
- **A2 Rust backend** (`150c452`): `std::thread::scope` with `Builder::spawn_scoped`; every started worker is joined, a worker panic or spawn failure exits 101 after joining; safe calls keep their checks through `__pruv_par_chk` wrappers.
- **A3 LLVM backend** (`08d567f`): per-branch context types and private trampolines joined through `pruv_g7_v1_run`, a `std::thread::scope` shim in the bindings; native `--emit obj|exe` rejects par programs with `codegen/parallel_target`. Runtime was run on macOS arm64; Linux runs in CI.
- **Differential fuzzing** (`bench/fuzz --g7`, seeds 71-73, 30 programs x 8 inputs each): 720 program/input pairs x 2 compilers x 2 backends x verified/safe = **5,760/5,760 completed comparisons**, 0 oracle, mode or repeat mismatches (every binary runs twice per input), 0 verifier rejections, 0 oracle gaps. Coverage: two and three branches, loop groups, multi-array branches, shared immutable reads, generic helpers with two specializations, i64/u8/bool elements, exact frames, nested helpers. The oracle runs branches in source order with owner-based arrays.
- **Problems and solutions:** `bench/g7/PROBLEMS.md` (q01 two bounded byte transforms with exact frames; q02 two output arrays from one immutable input), grader `bench/g7/run.py`. The reference solutions pass all four configurations (q01 744/744 assertions, q02 52/52) and safe mode 8/8.
- **Independent use:** an author who read only `SPEC.md` and `bench/g7/PROBLEMS.md` (no compiler runs) wrote both solutions (`bench/g7/solutions/indep1/`); both verified and passed on the first attempt in all four compiler/backend configurations (q01 744/744, q02 52/52; `bench/g7/results/solutions_indep1.json`).
- **Source mutants:** `bench/g7/mutate_ops.py`, 59 explicit edits: 51 behaviourally incorrect (wrong target, index or value, missing branch or store, false frame, old/current confusion), 4 static (duplicate writable input, effect and foreign leakage), 2 bad contracts, 1 weak contract, 1 equivalent. **wrong_but_proved 0** on all four configurations; the 51 incorrect ones are rejected at verification, the 4 static ones by the checker, and only the equivalent mutant is accepted.
- **Compiler fault injections** (`bench/g7/faults/`, ten patches, each alone in a disposable copy): omitted write/read and write/write checks, omitted transitive foreign rejection, admitted nested par, early sibling ensures, omitted branch havoc, omitted loop call writes, a bridge that detaches instead of joining, an LLVM `readonly` on a mutable parameter and trampolines bypassing the safe wrapper. **All ten are detected** by named tests. Early sibling ensures was first a gap (the existing proof tests failed earlier for other reasons); the new must_reject root `g7_v_sibling_ensures_order` now catches it. The detached bridge is caught by a runtime signal in `g7_r10_join_return`, which depends on scheduling; a deterministic gated lifetime test remains desirable.
- **Measurement** (`bench/g7/perf.py`, a multi-round integer mix over two independent i64 arrays, ten repetitions after warmup, `-C opt-level=3`, macOS arm64, 10 logical cores): at 8,000,000 elements the Rust backend par group runs in 531.35 ms against 531.40 ms for hand-written `std::thread::scope` Rust (**1.00x**), with sequential Pruv at 1023.99 ms; the LLVM backend runs in 1173.72 ms against 1132.05 ms (**1.04x**), with sequential Pruv at 2841.32 ms. At 1,000 elements spawn overhead dominates both (0.082 vs 0.079 ms Rust backend, 0.197 vs 0.218 ms LLVM). The 1.5x target is met on both backends.
- **Results:** `run_all --backend both` 2899/2899 (py, row list compared; the rs row list is identical).

**G7 complete.**

## 66. Stage 5: cost bound contracts (design, A0-A3 and Q, 2026-11-13)

- **Design:** `docs/STAGE5_SPEC.md` (`4e94093`). A function may declare `cost C` over its parameters; the ghost `spent` counts one unit per loop iteration, `1 + C_callee` per call, `1 + n` per alloc and `1 + len` per copy, and par branches at group entry. `spent <= C` (C frozen at entry) must hold at every return.
- **A0 frontend** (`ea737a9`): `cost` clause and `spent` parsing, static rules (cost_type, cost_expr, spent_context, cost_callee, cost_unsupported), 26 must_reject roots.
- **A1 verification** (`6d3c717`): the ghost counter in the verifier, `verify/cost_bound` at every return, cost expressions proved defined at entry; 13 proof programs and 12 must_reject roots.
- **A2 Rust backend** (`9b23b71`): verified mode erases cost and spent (byte-identical to the clause-free program); safe mode (`--no-verify`) keeps a checked counter and panics `pruv: cost exceeded` (exit 101).
- **A3 LLVM backend** (`e2edf72`): the same erasure and safe-mode counter for LLVM; SPEC.md section 6j. The audit fix keeps a string literal in a `spent` assert from leaving the string prelude behind after erasure.
- **Differential fuzzing** (`bench/fuzz --s5`, seeds 81-83, 30 programs x 8 inputs, py and rs): nine shapes (single and nested loops, calls in loops, decreasing recursion, generics, length-dependent alloc/copy, par, slack bounds, branches). Originals: 5,760/5,760 comparisons over both backends and verified/safe modes, 0 mismatches and no `cost exceeded` panic in safe mode. Minus-one twins (each exact bound reduced by one, 27 per seed): **all 162 rejected by `verify/cost_bound`**, and in safe mode 2,520 of 2,592 runs panic `cost exceeded` as the oracle predicts (the other 72 take a cheaper branch and exit normally, also as predicted); 2,592/2,592 safe comparisons match.
- **Problems and solutions:** `bench/s5/PROBLEMS.md` (q01 a square-grid nested loop with `cost len(a) * (len(a) + 1)`; q02 elementwise doubling through a zero-cost helper with `cost 3 * len(src) + 1`), grader `bench/s5/run.py`. The reference solutions pass all four configurations (76/76 assertions each) and safe mode.
- **Independent use:** an author who read only `SPEC.md` and `bench/s5/PROBLEMS.md` (no compiler runs) wrote both solutions with `spent` loop invariants (`bench/s5/solutions/indep1/`); both verified and passed on the first attempt in all four configurations (76/76 each; `bench/s5/results/solutions_indep1.json`).
- **Source mutants:** `bench/s5/mutate_ops.py`, 50 explicit edits: 42 behaviourally incorrect (wrong value or index, missing loop or body, extra call, alloc or copy, loop one past the end, bound minus one, wrong multiplier), 6 contract-only unit changes, 1 weak contract, 1 equivalent. **wrong_but_proved 0** on all four configurations; all 42 incorrect ones are rejected at verification and only the equivalent mutant is accepted.
- **Compiler fault injections** (`bench/s5/faults/`, eight patches, each alone in a disposable copy): omitted loop unit, omitted call unit, assume instead of prove at return, no havoc of `spent` at loops, an erased safe-mode return check in each backend, wrong alloc/copy length units and cost clauses kept in verified output. **All eight are detected** by named tests (s5_proof, must_reject and runtime roots).
- **Measurement** (`bench/s5/perf.py`, a costed mix with exact bound `65 * n` against a twin without cost/spent lines, ten repetitions after warmup, macOS arm64): in verified mode the generated Rust, LLVM IR and bindings are **byte-identical** to the twin for both compilers, so there is no run-time cost. Safe-mode counter overhead at 20,000 outer iterations: Rust backend 1.95 vs 1.95 ms (**1.00x**), LLVM 2.28 vs 1.95 ms (1.17x); identical verified binaries already differ by about 15% between runs, so the LLVM figure does not isolate the counter.
- **Results:** `run_all --backend both` 3095/3095 at A3 (py, row list compared); Q adds no test rows.

**Stage 5 (cost bounds, first slice) complete.** Deferred: costs for foreign, collection and file operations, par span cost, amortized costs and inference (`docs/STAGE5_SPEC.md` section 9).

## 67. G8: a verified standard module written in Pruv (design, A0-A3, 2026-11-13)

- **Design:** `docs/G8_SPEC.md` (`caa0b50`). A root `mod std;` loads one bundled source module, `stdlib/std.pruv`, with twelve monomorphic i64/array functions; every module calls them as `crate::std::<name>`. The bundle is reproved on every verified compile; there is no cache or trusted copy.
- **A0 loading** (`c7e9aa4`): Python reads the bundle next to its package, Rust embeds the same bytes; logical file label `@std/std.pruv`; new codes `parse/std_import_location`, `parse/std_module_conflict`, `parse/std_source`; g8_frontend group and nine must_reject roots. Programs without `mod std;` keep byte-identical output (`tests/g8/compatibility.json`).
- **A1 verification** (`c4a2ab8`): all twelve bundled bodies, used or not, are proved before emission with inference off; callers see only the contracts. 14 positive proof clients on both backends and 12 must_reject roots; fault checks for an unused false postcondition, `--no-verify`, solver unknown and caller isolation, in a disposable Python package and in patched copies of the Rust binary. Rows 3141 -> 3187.
- **A2 Rust backend** (`38d0ca8`): std functions are emitted as ordinary bodies, never as host entries; safe mode keeps the internal `requires` checks also under `--trust-callers` (`pruv: requires violated: crate::std::<name>`). Twelve runtime roots `tests/runtime/g8_r01`-`g8_r12` and two examples. Rows 3187 -> 3222, py 3222/3222.
- **A3 LLVM backend:** removing the staging guard was enough; the existing LLVM generator needed no change. Std functions get internal linkage (`define internal i64 @pruv_m_f_2_3_std_7_min_i64__pruv_body`), only root entries are exported (`pruv_abi_*`) and bound. All twelve A2 roots also run on LLVM, plus four new roots: root-wrapper Option ABI (`g8_r13`), a reversed array owned and freed by the host through a root wrapper (`g8_r14`), two child modules with leaf names equal to std names (`g8_r15`), and `abs_i64(i64::MIN)` panicking inside std under `--no-verify --trust-callers` (`g8_r16`). `tests/g8/check_llvm.py` inspects linkage, exports, bindings and safe-mode checks; py and rs `.ll` and bindings are byte-equal for 39 G8 roots and examples in 116 modes. Example project `examples/g8_std_modules/`. User page: SPEC.md section 6k.
- **Packaging:** a copy of `pruvc/` and `stdlib/` in another directory and a copy of the Rust binary alone both compile `examples/g8_std_modules/` from an unrelated working directory to byte-identical Rust and LLVM output with no absolute path in it; a `pruvc/` copy without `stdlib/` fails with `parse/std_source` at the root declaration.
- **Results:** group checks on both compilers: g8_proof 35/35, examples 476/476, runtime 1680/1680, must_reject 585/585, g8_frontend 27/27 (py) and 39/39 (rs, adds native output checks). `run_all --backend both` 3265/3265 at A3 on the Python compiler with the 3277-row list compared (the 12 native-output rows are Rust-compiler only and marked `only_compiler`).

## 68. G8 Q: fuzzing, problems, mutants, faults and measurement (2026-11-13)

- **Differential fuzzing** (`bench/fuzz --g8`, seeds 41, 56 and 57, 30 programs x 8 inputs, py and rs): fifteen direct and fifteen cross-module clients per seed over all twelve std functions, including the compositions checked_add of two sums, clamp of array_max and index_of of reverse; inputs include MIN/MAX overflow pairs, `abs(MIN+1)`, empty arrays, duplicate and absent matches, descending and all-equal arrays and both sum element bounds. An independent arbitrary-precision oracle (`g8_value`) predicts every result. **180/180 programs verified and 5,760/5,760 comparisons** over both backends and verified/safe modes, 0 mismatches, 0 unknown or unsupported. Bundle sha256 recorded in `bench/fuzz/results_g8/`.
- **Problems and solutions:** `bench/g8/PROBLEMS.md` (q01 `first_duplicate` and `checked_total`; q02 `bounded_sum`, `maximum` and an allocating `reversed`), grader `bench/g8/run.py`. The reference solutions pass all four configurations (q01 141/141, q02 179/179) in verified and safe mode.
- **Independent use:** an author who read only `SPEC.md` and `bench/g8/PROBLEMS.md` (no compiler runs) wrote both solutions (`bench/g8/solutions/indep1/`): q01 with quantified prefix invariants and explicit existential witnesses, the rest as thin wrappers over `crate::std::`. Both verified and passed on the **first attempt** in all four configurations and in safe mode (`bench/g8/results/solutions_indep1*.json`).
- **Source mutants:** `bench/g8/mutate_ops.py`, 46 explicit edits: 44 behaviourally incorrect (flipped comparisons and clamps, wrong values, absolute values and checked results, overflow boundaries, skipped overflow checks, premature sums, skipped or flipped matches, last instead of first index, wrong sortedness, incorrect maximum, reversal index), 1 weak contract, 1 equivalent. **wrong_but_proved 0** on all four configurations; all 44 incorrect ones are rejected at verification.
- **Compiler fault injections** (`bench/g8/faults/`, five patches, each alone in a disposable copy): skipping proofs of unused std bodies, trusting the bundle, resolving `crate::std::` to a local module, and dropping the internal safe-mode `requires` check in each backend. **All five are detected**: the first two by `g8_proof/bundle`, the third by the bundle check and the `conflict`/`symlink_conflict` roots, the last two by runtime roots `g8_r03`, `g8_r04`, `g8_r07`, `g8_r11` and `g8_r16` under `--trust-callers`.
- **Measurement** (`bench/g8/perf.py`, macOS 26.5 arm64, 10 cores, rustc 1.99.1, Apple clang 17, medians): compile time without std / with `mod std;` only / with calls is 91 / 261 / 261 ms on the Python compiler and 10 / 128 / 130 ms on the Rust compiler, so the cost is reproving the twelve bodies once per compile and does not grow with calls. Run time over 1M elements against an identical local copy of the body: LLVM sum 0.193 vs 0.187 ms and reverse 0.291 vs 0.286 ms; Rust reverse 0.315 vs 0.318 ms. Rust sum showed 0.881 vs 0.565 ms, but the std body is textually identical to the local one, the checked entries take the same time (1.77 vs 1.71 ms) and another run of the report orders them the other way (0.885 vs 1.146 ms), so the gap is run-to-run and code-layout noise, not a cost of std. Reverse makes one 8 MB allocation per call on both sides.
- **Promotion** (seven roots from distinct findings): four rejection roots from incorrect mutants, written as user definitions that import std and keep the approved contracts: `g8_q_flipped_min` (m001, flipped comparison), `g8_q_add_boundary` (m014, inclusive overflow guard that loses the representable boundary), `g8_q_premature_sum` (m023, return before accumulation with valid invariants) all `verify/ensures`, and `g8_q_zero_maximum` (m038, zero start for a negative maximum, `verify/invariant_entry`). Two runtime roots on both backends in verified and safe mode: `g8_r17_first_duplicate` (the q01 solution shape, `[3,2,2,3] -> Some(0)`, extremes and checked-total boundaries) and `g8_r18_reverse_index` (fuzz seed 41 case 29: a child module passes a temporary std reversal into `index_of_i64`, allocation balance checked). One example `examples/g8_q_sum_add/` (fuzz seed 41 case 12: two bounded sums into `checked_add_i64`). Rows 3277 -> 3295.
- **Results:** `run_all --backend both` 3283/3283 on the Python compiler with the 3295-row list compared (missing 0, extra 0; the 12 native-output rows are Rust-compiler only).

**G8 complete.** Deferred work: `docs/G8_SPEC.md` section 15.

## 69. L0: side cross-check of Lair's `clipBytes` (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/backend/engine-import/tail.ts` `clipBytes(text, max)`, intended as the longest prefix of `text` within `max` UTF-8 bytes that ends on a character boundary. The body is copied verbatim to `bench/l0/clip_bytes.mjs`; Lair itself is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/clip_bytes.pruv` proves `len(result) <= max`, the result is a byte prefix ending on a character boundary, and maximality (no boundary of the source in `len(result)+1 ..= max` unless the whole source fits). It verifies on both compilers.
- **Driver:** `bench/l0/run.py` builds the Pruv function natively through the G2 harness on both compilers and both backends, runs the JS in one node process, and checks both against an independent forward-accumulating oracle. 20,083 cases (83 edge cases plus 20,000 random strings with frequent U+FFFD and cuts inside characters, seed 12345).
- **Results (each of py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv meets the spec in 20,083/20,083 cases; the JS meets it in 14,157. The 5,926 differences are all "JS shorter": the JS never exceeds `max` and never returns an invalid or non-prefix string. Cause: `.replace(/�+$/, '')` also strips genuine U+FFFD characters of the source that end the cut prefix. Minimal example: input `efbfbd61` (U+FFFD, `a`) with `max = 3` gives `""` in JS and `efbfbd` in Pruv. Reported to Lair as a bug; not fixed here.
- **G8 audit follow-ups (test-only):** `tests/g8/check_llvm.py` now also asserts every std symbol is defined once and every call to a std symbol targets a definition in the same module; `tests/g8/check_proof.py` asserts that a failed proof writes no `--rust-bindings`, `--also-llvm`, `--emit obj` or `--emit exe` output on either compiler. g8_proof 35/35 and g8_frontend 39/39 (rs) / 27/27 (py); no compiler defect found.

## 70. L0: side cross-check of Lair's `hostWithin` (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/shared/web-sources.ts` `hostWithin(host, sites)`, intended as "host equals a site or is a subdomain of it, ASCII case-insensitive". The body and `webHost` are copied verbatim to `bench/l0/host_within.mjs`; Lair itself is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/host_within.pruv` proves `host_within(host, site)` is true exactly when the ASCII-lowercased host equals the site, or ends with it and the byte before the suffix is `.`. It verifies on both compilers. Several sites are the OR of single calls in the driver.
- **Driver:** `bench/l0/run_host_within.py` reuses the clipBytes harness on both compilers and both backends, with an independent label-based Python oracle. Two modes, seed 12345: direct (20,020 random hosts, mixed case, empty labels, near-miss suffixes, non-ASCII) and real path (20,018 random URLs passed through `webHost`; 12,147 give a host, 7,871 give none and are skipped).
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv matches the oracle in all 32,167 cases. Direct mode: 551 differences, all "JS true, Pruv false", caused by JS `toLowerCase` folding non-ASCII letters to ASCII. Minimal example: host `K` (U+212A KELVIN SIGN, `e284aa`) with site `k`. Real path: 0 differences, because `new URL` normalizes the host first (`https://K/` gives `k`). So the difference is latent: it only matters if `hostWithin` is ever called with a raw host. A trailing-dot host such as `kakao.com.` is outside the sites in both versions (fail-closed). Reported to Lair; not fixed here.

## 71. L1 first bundle: Node-API bridge for verified Pruv functions (2026-11-13)

- **What:** `bridge/node/` is a Node-API native module (napi-rs 3, `cdylib`) that exposes the two L0 functions to Node and Electron as `clipBytes(text, max)` and `hostWithin(host, site)`. The Rust in `bridge/node/gen/` is generated by the Pruv Rust source backend from `bench/l0/clip_bytes.pruv` and `bench/l0/host_within.pruv`. The Python and Rust compilers produce byte-identical files. The only hand-written logic is a wrapper that checks each `requires` first and throws a JS `Error` (`InvalidArg`) instead of letting the generated code panic. JS strings reach Pruv as UTF-8 (Node-API turns lone surrogates into U+FFFD). Byte limits count UTF-8 bytes.
- **Staleness:** `bridge/node/regen.py --check` recompiles both sources with verification and fails if the committed `gen/` files differ, so an unverified or outdated file cannot be built in CI.
- **Test:** `bridge/node/test.mjs` (seeded, no npm dependencies) compares the native functions with independent JS oracles. It also counts, for information only, how often the verbatim Lair TS originals differ. It checks that `requires` violations throw instead of crashing the process.
- **Results (macOS arm64, seed 12345, 20,000 cases each):**
  - Node v26.4.0: 0 mismatches for either function. The TS originals differ from Pruv in 8,555 `clipBytes` cases (the dropped genuine U+FFFD from section 69) and 308 `hostWithin` cases (U+212A KELVIN SIGN folded to `k` by `toLowerCase`, section 70).
  - Electron 45.3.1 (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`, embedded Node v24.22.2, 5,000 cases): the same module loads unchanged, with 0 mismatches.
  - Error checks pass on both.
- **CI:** `.github/workflows/node-bridge.yml` runs `regen.py --check` on Ubuntu, then builds and tests on macOS arm64, macOS x64 (`macos-15-intel`), Windows x64 and Linux x64 (`ubuntu-24.04`, pinned like `ci.yml` because `ubuntu-latest` no longer offers Python 3.9). It uses only free standard runners. It runs only on manual dispatch and on pushes to `main` that touch `bridge/node/**`, `bench/l0/*.pruv` or the workflow (user decision 2026-11-13: keep CI rare). `ci.yml` and `windows-abi.yml` are unchanged.
- **Not done:** the module is not wired into Lair, and there is no prebuilt-binary packaging for Electron apps yet. Both wait for a user decision on touching the Lair product.

## 72. L1 second bundle: `versionAtLeast` and `colLetters` (2026-11-13)

- **Targets:** Lair `src/remote/cloudflare-route.ts` `versionAtLeast(a, b)` (with its helper `versionParts`: trim, match `^(\d+)\.(\d+)\.(\d+)$`, compare the three numbers; false if either side is malformed) and `src/documents/sheets.ts` `colLetters(col)` (1-based column number to spreadsheet letters, `0` gives `""`). Bodies are copied verbatim to `bench/l0/version_at_least.mjs` and `bench/l0/col_letters.mjs`; Lair itself is not changed.
- **Pruv versions:**
  - `bench/l0/version_at_least.pruv` (inputs up to 4,096 bytes) compares the digit runs as arbitrary-length decimal strings (leading zeros ignored), so it has no precision limit. Proved: memory and arithmetic safety and termination. The exact comparison semantics are *not* stated as an `ensures`; correctness rests on the cross-check below.
  - `bench/l0/col_letters.pruv`: `fn col_letters(col: i64) -> str ! alloc`, `requires 0 <= col <= 10^15`. Proved: every byte is `A`..`Z`, the result is empty exactly when `col = 0`, `len(result) <= col`, and `col < 26^k` implies `len(result) <= k` for k = 1..11.
  - Both verify on both compilers.
- **Driver:** `bench/l0/run_second_bundle.py` (shared), with `run_version_at_least.py` and `run_col_letters.py`. It reuses the L0 harness on both compilers and both backends with independent Python oracles (seed 12345).
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):**
  - `versionAtLeast`: Pruv matches the oracle in all 21,089 cases. The TS original differs in 4,637 of them, in two classes:
    - 3,615 Unicode whitespace: JS `trim()` strips non-ASCII spaces such as U+00A0. Minimal example: `a = "0.0.0"`, `b = " 0.0.0 "` gives TS `true`, Pruv `false`.
    - 1,022 numeric precision: `Number()` rounds parts above 2^53, so for example `9007199254740992.0.0` is "at least" `9007199254740993.0.0` in TS.
    - Lair's input comes from `parseCloudflaredVersion` on `cloudflared --version` output, so both are latent rather than live bugs.
  - `colLetters`: 120,073 cases (every column 0..100,000, ±3 around each power of 26, the 10^15 limit, and 20,000 random columns up to 10^15). 0 mismatches against the oracle, and 0 differences from the TS original.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` now also exports `versionAtLeast(a, b)` and `colLetters(col)`. The generated files `gen/version_at_least_gen.rs` and `gen/col_letters_gen.rs` are byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). `requires` violations (strings over 4,096 UTF-8 bytes, `col` outside `0..=10^15` or not an integer) throw `InvalidArg`.
- **Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, seed 12345):** 0 mismatches for all four functions (`versionAtLeast` 20,139 cases with 3,580 informational TS differences, `colLetters` 120,073 cases with 0); error checks pass.
- **Not done:** as in section 71, nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 73. L2 first bundle: terminal-text cleaning (`plainFromAnsi` + `applyCrLf`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/backend/engine-install.ts` `plainFromAnsi` and `applyCrLf` (with `C1_KIND`, `AnsState`, `resetAns`, `charStep`, `tryStEnd`). This streaming cleaner turns raw installer terminal output into plain text: it drops CSI sequences, OSC/DCS/SOS/PM/APC strings (ended by BEL, ST or ESC `\\`), other ESC sequences and C0/C1/DEL controls. It keeps tab, LF and CR, then folds CR and CRLF to a single LF, with state carried across chunks. The code is copied verbatim (TypeScript types removed) to `bench/l0/term_plain.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/term_plain.pruv`. The streaming state is one integer 0..11: six escape modes, plus 6 when an LF right after a CR must be skipped.
  - `term_plain(state, data) -> str ! alloc` builds the output with the G6 `String` builder.
  - `term_state(state, data)` returns the next state.
  - Proved on both compilers:
    - every slice cuts on a UTF-8 character boundary
    - `len(result) <= len(data)`
    - the state stays in 0..11
    - arithmetic safety and termination
  - Exact behaviour is not an `ensures`; it rests on the cross-check below.
  - No third-party crate is needed for this piece.
- **Driver:** `bench/l0/run_term_plain.py`, seed 12345.
  - Inputs: 8,748 edge cases (every initial state × pairs of 27 tricky chunks, such as ESC at the end of a chunk inside a string, or CR at the end of one chunk with LF at the start of the next) and 20,000 random cases of up to 40 characters, split into up to four chunks.
  - Pruv runs chunk by chunk.
  - The independent Python oracle processes the joined text in one pass: a mode machine plus a regex CR/LF fold. It therefore also checks that chunking does not change the result.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):**
  - 28,748/28,748 cases match the oracle in both text and final state, and so does the TS original.
  - All 12 final states occur.
  - Domain note: TS walks UTF-16 and drops lone surrogates, while Pruv strings are valid UTF-8. Through the bridge, Node-API turns a lone surrogate into U+FFFD, which is kept. That is the only source of difference.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `termPlain(state, data)` and `termState(state, data)`. `gen/term_plain_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). A state that is not an integer in 0..11 throws `InvalidArg`.
- **Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, seed 12345, 20,000 chunked cases including lone surrogates):** 0 mismatches against a JS oracle on the UTF-8 round trip. 4,741 informational TS differences, all from the lone-surrogate domain difference. Error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Larger L2 pieces that need third-party Rust crates (parsers, document conversion) come next.

## 74. G6b: owned `String` / `Vec<i64>` results from trusted Rust adapters (2026-11-13)

- **Why:** L2 pieces need third-party crates (for example `encoding_rs`) to hand text or lists back to Pruv. Before G6b a user `trusted rust fn` could return only scalars. Design: `docs/G6B_SPEC.md`.
- **Surface:** a trusted declaration may also return the catalog `String` or exactly `Vec<i64>`, and must then carry `! alloc` (so must every caller). Contracts may use `len(result)` and guarded `result[i]`. Still rejected: other result types (`Vec<bool>`, `HashMap`, `File`, `str`, a user type named `String`), nested owned types, an owned result together with a collection parameter, `take` or `inout`, and a missing `alloc` on the declaration or a caller.
- **Proof:** each call gives a fresh, independently owned model (byte array for `String`, i64-bounded array for `Vec<i64>`) constrained only by well-formedness and the declared `ensures`. `requires` are proved before the result is assumed; contract domains are checked in order.
- **Runtime:** after the adapter returns, the length is compared with 2^40 in every check mode, before any `ensures`. In `--foreign-checks all` the `ensures` run on the returned object; a lying adapter then fails with exit 101, while `requires`/`off` accept the trusted lie as documented.
- **Backends:** Rust source and LLVM on both compilers. In LLVM the result is boxed once as a `PruvH` and joins the existing ownership, temporary-cleanup and drop paths.
- **Tests:** 55 new rows: 13 rejection fixtures (`tests/must_reject/g6b_*`), one frontend and one proof fixture under `tests/g6/`, and five runtime fixtures (owned results, a String-only program, three lying adapters) across the check modes and both backends.
- **Full runs:** Python compiler 3,338/3,338 and Rust compiler 3,350/3,350 on both backends; row comparison: missing 0, extra 0, failed 0 (the 12 rows outside the Python run are Rust-compiler-only).
- **Audit:** one static audit of both compilers (Codex) found no must-fix or should-fix defects.

## 75. L2 second bundle: byte decoding with a third-party crate (`decodeBytes` + `looksBinary`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/decode.ts` `decodeBytes` (UTF-8/UTF-16LE/UTF-16BE BOM, otherwise strict UTF-8, otherwise EUC-KR, all through `TextDecoder`) and `looksBinary` (a NUL byte in the first 8 KiB). Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/decode_bytes.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/decode_bytes.pruv`, the first L2 piece that uses a third-party crate. Pruv decides the encoding: the BOM checks (both directions), a strict UTF-8 validator (no overlongs, surrogates or code points above U+10FFFF) and `looks_binary` (exact `exists`/`forall` contract) are proved on both compilers. The decoders are `encoding_rs` behind four G6b trusted adapters in `bench/l0/decode_adapter` that return owned `String` with length bounds; the valid-UTF-8 path promises a byte-for-byte copy.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/decode_oracle.py`, written from the WHATWG Encoding Standard algorithms without Python codecs, using the official EUC-KR index (`bench/l0/data/index-euc-kr.txt`, SHA-256 checked).
- **Results (seed 12345, 1,939 edge + 20,000 random = 21,939 byte strings, identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original run in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) and the oracle agree on text, encoding and binary flag in all 21,939 cases.
- **Finding:** plain Homebrew Node 26 (macOS) `TextDecoder('euc-kr')` is not WHATWG: byte 0x80 gives U+0080 instead of U+FFFD, which gives 6,571 informational TS differences in the bridge test. Lair is not affected, because it runs in Electron.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `decodeBytes(Uint8Array) -> {text, encoding}` and `looksBinary(Uint8Array)`; `gen/decode_bytes_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0): 0 mismatches against the oracle in 21,939 cases; error checks pass. CI paths now include the adapter, oracle and data.
- **Finding:** the addon cannot be loaded into the installed Lair Preview on macOS ("different Team IDs"): Lair's hardened runtime enables library validation, so a bridge shipped with Lair must be signed with Lair's signing identity.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 76. L2 third bundle: zip-bomb pre-check (`zipUncompressedTotal`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/zip.ts` `zipUncompressedTotal`: find the end-of-central-directory record in the last 65,535 + 22 bytes, then sum the uncompressed sizes of the central directory entries before anything is inflated. `null` = not a zip or unreadable directory, `Infinity` = zip64-shaped (count 0xffff, offset or size 0xffffffff). Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/zip_total.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/zip_total.pruv` (result -1 = null, -2 = Infinity, otherwise the total). Every little-endian read is proved in bounds on any input, so a hostile archive cannot make the guard itself fault. Proved bounds: the EOCD position is -1 or leaves 22 bytes; the total is between -2 and 65534 × (2^32 − 2) (below 2^53, so the JS number is exact); inputs shorter than 22 bytes give -1. No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_zip_total.py`, written from the PKWARE APPNOTE layout with `struct` and `bytes.rfind`.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 907 edge + 20,000 random = 20,907):** real archives from Python's `zipfile` with hostile edits (lying sizes, zip64 markers, wrong counts, moved offsets, corrupt signatures, huge name/extra/comment lengths, truncation, trailing and leading junk, byte flips, a planted later EOCD), every truncation and suffix of small archives, the 64 KiB search-window edge (65,512 to 70,000 trailing bytes), a 65,535-byte comment, an EOCD planted inside a comment, and up to 1,000 entries of 0xfffffffe bytes. Oracle outcomes: 9,396 totals, 9,514 null, 1,997 Infinity.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) and the oracle agree in all 20,907 cases. The TS original has no defect here; what Pruv adds is the proof that no read leaves the buffer.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `zipUncompressedTotal(Uint8Array) -> number | null` (Infinity for zip64); `gen/zip_total_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). `run_zip_total.py --emit` prints the oracle rows for the Node test. Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 TS differences in 20,907 cases; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 77. L2 fourth bundle: PDF page image to PNG (`encodePng`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/png.ts` `encodePng`: a pdf.js page image (kind 1 = 1-bit, 2 = RGB, 3 = RGBA) downsampled by an integer step so it has at most `maxPixels` pixels, RGBA blended onto white, 1-bit masks inverted when mostly black, then deflated into a PNG for OCR. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/encode_png.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/encode_png.pruv` does the whole pixel part: `png_step` (exact integer form of `ceil(sqrt(w*h/max))`, proved 1..=1,048,577), `blend` (exact integer form of TS's `Math.round`, proved 0..=255), `png_fill` and `png_invert` on an `inout` scanline buffer, and `pub png_scanlines` (`! alloc`) that returns the scanlines or an empty array for TS `null`. Every pixel read and scanline write is proved in bounds; a short `data` array reads as 0, as TS's `undefined` does. Domain: integers with 1 <= width, height, width × height <= 2^40, 1 <= maxPixels <= 2^40 (TS's `>>` would differ only past width 2^31). Deflate and PNG chunks stay in the host. No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_encode_png.py`, exact (`math.isqrt` step, `Fraction` rounding, bit operations); it also parses each TS PNG (signature, chunk CRCs, IHDR/IDAT/IEND order, IHDR fields) and inflates IDAT.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 873 edge + 6,000 random = 6,873):** all three kinds × sizes × data lengths (exact, short, empty, long, cut mid-pixel) × maxPixels, step boundaries, and huge dimensions with tiny outputs (e.g. 2^40 × 1 at maxPixels 1 and 2^20). Oracle outcomes: 5,612 PNGs (1,215 inverted 1-bit), 1,261 null.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv scanlines, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) and the oracle agree in all 6,873 cases. The TS original has no defect in this domain.
