- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports native `pngScanlines(data, width, height, kind, maxPixels) -> {width, height, raw} | null` (throws `InvalidArg` outside the domain; maxPixels is capped at 2^36 so the scanlines stay at most 2^38 bytes) and a JS `encodePng(image, maxPixels)` that keeps TS's null checks and builds the PNG with `node:zlib` exactly as TS does. `gen/encode_png_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0): 0 scanline mismatches against the oracle and 0 byte differences from the TS PNG in 6,873 cases; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 78. L2 fifth bundle: page text splitter (`splitText`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/chunker.ts` `splitText`: cut a page's text into pieces of at most `max` UTF-16 code units for document search, at the last newline, else the last space, at or before `max` but not before `max / 2`, else at `max` (moved back one unit inside a surrogate pair), with JS `trim` around every piece. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/split_text.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/split_text.pruv` works on the UTF-8 bytes and counts UTF-16 units (a 4-byte character is 2). `is_ws` matches the JS trim set (U+0009..000D, 0020, 00A0, 1680, 2000..200A, 2028, 2029, 202F, 205F, 3000, FEFF) from its UTF-8 bytes; `next`/`prev` are proved to land on character boundaries; `cut` is proved to return a boundary strictly after the start; `pub split_bounds` (`! alloc`) counts the pieces, then fills `[start, end]` byte ranges, and the host slices. Every read is proved in bounds and every cut on a character boundary. Domain: integer 2 <= max <= 2^40, text at most 5 * 10^11 UTF-8 bytes. No third-party crate.
- **Finding:** the TS original can loop forever when `max <= 1` (any nonblank text at `max <= 0`; at `max 1` when the rest starts with a 4-byte character, the cut becomes 0 and the same empty piece repeats). The Pruv domain and the bridge reject `max < 2`.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_split_text.py`, independent: a list of code points with UTF-16 widths and an explicit JS whitespace table.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 536 edge + 20,000 random = 20,536):** every JS whitespace character and near misses (U+0085, U+180E, U+200B, ...), newlines and spaces at, just below and just above `max / 2` and `max`, 4-byte characters around unit `max`, max 2..5, and 400 long pages (2,000..6,000 characters, max 50..1,600). Oracle pieces: 331,707.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) and the oracle agree in all 20,536 cases.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `splitText(text, max) -> string[]` (throws `InvalidArg` for max not an integer in 2..=2^40 or text over 5 * 10^11 bytes). `gen/split_text_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS original in 20,536 cases; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 79. L2 sixth bundle: line and block grouping (`chunkLines` / `chunkBlocks`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/chunker.ts` `groupNumbered`, behind `chunkLines` (text files) and `chunkBlocks` (DOCX): gather items from the top into pieces whose joined text stays within `max` UTF-16 units (each item counts its length + 1), flush a group when the next item would pass `max`, split an item longer than `max` alone with `splitText`, and trim every group. Copied verbatim (with `splitText`, `chunkLines`, `chunkBlocks`) to `bench/l0/group_text.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/group_text.pruv` reuses the section 78 helpers. The host joins the items with `\n` and passes the joined text and the byte end of every item (`[i64]`). `group_into` checks at run time that every end is in order, within the text and on a character boundary (else the result is `[-1]`); `split_item` and `flush` are proved to write only within the output and never past the record count bound. `pub group_bounds` (`! alloc`) counts, then fills `[from, to, start, end]` records: item indexes and the byte range of the trimmed text. Domain: integer 2 <= max <= 2^40, joined text at most 2.5 * 10^11 UTF-8 bytes. No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_group_text.py`, independent: groups on code-point lists with UTF-16 widths, reusing the section 78 code-point oracle for oversized items and for trimming.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 1,301 edge + 20,000 random = 21,301 lists, 157,241 lines):** empty lists and lines, whitespace-only lines and groups, lines of exactly `max`, `max - 1` and `max + 1` units, groups that fill to exactly `max`, 4-byte characters at the limits, lines holding newlines, random lines drawn from every JS whitespace character and near misses, edge max 2..5 and 8, and 400 long lists (20..200 lines, max 50..1,600). Oracle pieces: 114,935.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) and the oracle agree in all 21,301 cases. No defect found in the TS original beyond section 78's `max <= 1` loop, which the same domain excludes.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports the native `groupTexts(texts, max) -> {from, to, text}[]` (0-based item indexes) and JS `chunkLines(lines, max)` and `chunkBlocks(blocks, max)` returning Lair's `{loc, text}` shapes. `groupTexts` throws `InvalidArg` for max not an integer in 2..=2^40 or joined text over 2.5 * 10^11 bytes. `gen/group_text_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS `chunkLines` and `chunkBlocks` (sparse block numbers) in 21,301 cases; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 80. L2 seventh bundle: photo shrink before OCR (`shrinkJpeg`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/image-shrink.ts` `shrinkJpeg`: read the EXIF orientation from the first APP1 Exif segment (`readOrientation`, `orientationFromTiff`), cut the long side to 2000 by box averaging (`boxShrink`, `Math.round` sizes and averages) and turn the pixels upright (`applyOrientation`, all eight EXIF orientations). JPEG decoding and encoding (jpeg-js) stay in the host. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/image_shrink.mjs` (`shrinkDecoded` holds the shrinkJpeg lines between decode and encode); Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/image_shrink.pruv`. `pub jpeg_orientation` walks the markers with every byte read proved in bounds (TS `Math.min(pos + 2 + length, len)` segment end, `tiff + u32` IFD offsets up to 2^32) and is proved to return 1..8. `pub shrink_width` / `shrink_height` compute `Math.round(side * 2000 / long)` exactly as `(4000 * side + long) / (2 * long)`, proved in 1..2000. `pub shrink_pixels` (`! alloc`) returns an empty array where TS returns undefined (long side <= 2000, orientation 1); otherwise the box sums carry proved upper bounds (`255 * pixels in the box`), the average `min(255, (2s + n) / (2n))` matches `Uint8ClampedArray` rounding, and every read and write is proved in bounds (a read past the data gives 0). Domain: integer width and height 1..65535 (every JPEG), orientation 1..8. No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_image_shrink.py`, independent: `struct` parsing on slices for the orientation; `Fraction` arithmetic with `floor(q + 1/2)` rounding for sizes and averages, and the eight orientations built from list flips and transposes instead of index formulas.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 1,102 edge + 6,000 random = 7,102 cases):** EXIF in II and MM order, orientation values 0..9, 256, 257 and 65535 after other tags, Exif segments cut at every length, declared segment lengths shorter than the TIFF data, bad magic, mixed byte-order marks, IFD offsets 0..20 and near 2^31 and 2^32, entry counts past the end, fill bytes, RST/SOI/TEM markers, early SOS/EOI, segment lengths 0, 1 and 65535; images 1x1 to 8191x1 in all eight orientations, long sides 2000, 2001, 3999, 4000, 4001 and 6000, uniform and two-level pixels for exact-half averages. Oracle outcomes: orientations 1..8 found 2,343 / 92 / 120 / 8 / 8 / 219 / 8 / 104 times; 3,710 pixel results (208 shrunk), 490 untouched.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) and the oracle agree in all 7,102 cases. No defect found in the TS original.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports the native `jpegOrientation(bytes)` and `shrinkPixels(data, width, height, orientation) -> {width, height, data}` or null, and JS `shrinkJpeg(bytes, jpeg)` that takes the jpeg-js module and returns Lair's Buffer or undefined. `shrinkPixels` throws `InvalidArg` for width or height not an integer in 1..=65535, orientation not in 1..=8, or data not `4 * width * height` bytes. `gen/image_shrink_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy in 7,102 cases; `shrinkJpeg` is checked with a stub jpeg-js against the TS copy (orientation 6) and on its undefined paths; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

Oracle outcomes: 938 empty, 7,931 cut, 2,882 results of 396..400 characters, longest 400.

## 82. L2 ninth bundle: clip sheet pixels (`renderClipSheet`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/clip-sheet.ts` `renderClipSheet` (commit e0e70c08, unchanged at 698e1bd1): one to four scene pictures on a 1560 × 920 board, each box-fitted and centred in a 768 × 432 cell, with a black label strip (a 5 × 7 bitmap font at scale 2) and a black number box. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/clip_sheet.mjs` with pass-through jpeg-js stubs; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/clip_sheet.pruv`. `pub clip_sheet_background` (`! alloc`, proved 5,740,800 bytes) and `pub clip_sheet_cell` (`! alloc`) draw onto a copy of the sheet: the box-average fit (rounded `(2s + n) / (2n)`, every source read and sheet write proved in bounds), the rectangle fill, the font as integer rows, UTF-16 unit counting for the text width and one advance per code point. Domain: width and height 1..65535 (every JPEG), out size 1..768 × 1..432, label at most 10^6 and number at most 4,096 UTF-8 bytes. The fitted size stays in the host because TS computes it with doubles (`Math.round(width * ratio)`), which differs from exact rounding for some sizes. Verified by both compilers after three proof fixes (an `at + 3` overflow rewritten as `at < len(s) - 3`, explicit pixel weights instead of a doubling loop, a guard for characters without a glyph). No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_clip_sheet.py`, independent: box sums from integral images with `divmod` rounding, fills by clipped slices of the linear sheet, the font as bit strings; sheets compared by crc32.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 47 edge + 300 random = 347 cases, 867 cells):** sizes 1 × 1 to 65535 × 1 and 1 × 65535, exact cell sizes and one pixel over, the three sizes where the double fit differs from exact rounding (5632 × 55, 49 × 1568, 629 × 544), empty labels, time labels, digit runs wider than the cell (up to 5,000 units), characters without a glyph, Korean, emoji and a combining accent, numbers including 0, -1, 2^53 − 1, 2.5, 1e21 and 1.5e300.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) and the oracle agree in all 347 cases. Observation, not changed: a label wider than the cell starts left of the cell, and because TS writes by linear index, pixels left of the sheet edge land in rows above (and are dropped before the sheet start). Pruv reproduces this exactly (222 cells have labels wider than the cell).
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `clipSheetBackground()`, `clipSheetCell(...)` and `renderClipSheet(cells, jpeg)` (jpeg-js passed in, as for `shrinkJpeg`); `clipSheetCell` throws `InvalidArg` outside its domain. `gen/clip_sheet_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 67 cases at the default size): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** the JPEG decode and encode stay in jpeg-js; nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 83. L2 tenth bundle: document kind by extension (`kindOf`, `extensionOf`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/kinds.ts` `extensionOf` and `kindOf` (commit 5d974738, unchanged at 698e1bd1): the text after the last `.` of the last `/` or `\` segment (none if the dot is first), lowercased, mapped to pdf, docx, xlsx, csv, image, text, unsupported or unknown through three word sets. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/kind_of.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/kind_of.pruv`. `pub extension_at` returns the UTF-16 index of the extension text (or -1), proved in -1..2·len; `pub kind_of` (`! alloc` for the word literals) returns the kind code, proved in 0..7. Words are compared as base-129 keys of at most 8 characters, with the overflow bound carried as a loop invariant (`k < cap`, `cap` = 129^characters). No Unicode tables are needed: checked over every code point in Node, the only non-ASCII character whose JS `toLowerCase` is all ASCII is U+212A KELVIN SIGN ("k"), so ASCII is lowercased in Pruv and E2 84 AA reads as `k`; U+0130 lowercases to `i` + U+0307 and never matches. The general lowercasing of the returned extension text stays in the host. Verified by both compilers on the first complete draft (the first draft failed only on a missing `! alloc`). No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_kind_of.py`, independent: `re.split`, `str.rfind`, Python `str.lower` and set lookups; the extension index counted in UTF-16 units. The bridge glue `name.slice(at).toLowerCase()` is run in the same JS runtime with the oracle's index and compared with the TS `extensionOf`.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 576 edge + 20,000 random = 20,576 names):** every word in lower, upper and capitalized case, alone, as a dotfile, with a letter before or after; the Kelvin sign in `kt`, `key`, `markdown`; U+0130, combining dot, `ß`, `Σ`, Hangul, emoji, no-break space and BOM inside extensions; dotfiles, trailing dots, `..`, folders with dots, mixed `/` and `\` paths, extensions of 9 to 100 characters. Kinds in the oracle: unknown 12,121, text 5,245, unsupported 1,671, image 948, csv 236, xlsx 134, docx 117, pdf 104.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) and the oracle agree in all 20,576 cases; the glue agrees with the TS `extensionOf` in all of them.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `kindCode(name)` and `extensionAt(name)` (native) and `kindOf(name)` / `extensionOf(name)` (JS glue); names over 10^12 UTF-8 bytes throw `InvalidArg`. Lone surrogates become U+FFFD (one unit each), so the UTF-16 index still matches the JS string. `gen/kind_of_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,576 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 84. L2 eleventh bundle: sheet row pieces (`rowPieces`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/sheets.ts` `rowPieces` with `cellText` and `colLetters`, and `splitText` from `src/documents/chunker.ts` (commit b99c75b6, unchanged at 698e1bd1): one sheet row becomes pieces `"행 N: "` + the non-empty cells joined with `" | "`, a cell labelled `"{column letters}="` unless it continues the previous column (column 1 unlabelled at the start of a piece), a cell longer than `max(max - head - 12, 100)` split by `splitText` first, a piece closed when the next part would pass `max - head` UTF-16 units. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/row_pieces.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/row_pieces.pruv`, on the cell texts joined with `'\n'` and the byte end of every cell (as in section 79). `pub row_bounds` (`! alloc`) returns, per piece, `[col1, col2, nseg]` and per segment `[label column or 0, start, end]`; it counts in a first pass and fills in a second. Every read is proved in bounds and every cut on a character boundary (the splitText helpers of section 78), the output length is proved at most `6·len + 6`, and malformed ends give `[-1]`. The head length (5..20 units for row 1..10^15) and the label lengths (bijective base 26) are computed in Pruv; only the head text and the column letters are formatted by the host (the verified `col_letters` of the L1 bundle). Domain: integer `1 <= max <= 2^40`, `1 <= rowNo <= 10^15`, at most 1.8·10^11 bytes (the bound that keeps the output alloc within 2^40). Verified by both compilers after two bound fixes (`col <= 10^15` on the label length, the byte bound lowered from 2.5·10^11). No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_row_pieces.py`, independent: Python `cellText` (null, strings with `\r?\n` folded and JS whitespace trimmed, booleans, integers), a direct piece builder that measures with Python UTF-16 lengths and its own column letters, and the code-point splitText oracle of section 78.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 3,069 edge + 20,000 random = 23,069 rows, 283,049 cells, 167,338 pieces):** empty rows and cells, blank cells of JS whitespace, column 1 and gaps, booleans and integers, inner `\n` / `\r\n` / `\r`, 4-byte characters, parts landing exactly on, one under and one over `max - head`, `max` from 1 (below the head: every part alone) to 2,000, cells longer than the split width, columns at Z/AA, AZ/BA, ZZ/AAA and 731, rows 1, 9, 10, 99, 100, 10^15 - 1, 10^15 and long rows of 100 to 800 cells.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) and the oracle agree in all 23,069 rows, on the first verified build.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `rowTextPieces(texts, rowNo, max)` (native, on cell texts) and `rowPieces(row, rowNo, max)` (JS glue: Lair's `cellText`, verbatim, then `rowTextPieces`); out-of-domain `rowNo`, `max` or size throws `InvalidArg`. `gen/row_pieces_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 23,069 rows): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Number and date cells go through the JS `cellText` (the oracle covers integers only); the rest of `extractSheets` (grouping rows into ranges) is still TS.

## 85. L2 twelfth bundle: sheet chunk grouping (`extractSheets`, `rangeOf`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/sheets.ts` `extractSheets` (the grouping of row pieces into chunks) and `rangeOf` (commit b99c75b6, unchanged at 698e1bd1): the pieces of one sheet, in order, join the open chunk unless it is nonempty and either its size plus the piece length plus 1 passes `max` or the piece row is more than `FAR_ROWS = 50` rows after the last piece's row; the size starts at the UTF-16 length of the header `"[시트: name]\n"`. A chunk's range is the minimum and maximum row, the minimum `col1` and the maximum `col2` of its pieces, written `"A1"` for a single cell and `"A1:C9"` otherwise. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/sheet_chunks.mjs` (with the section 84 helpers); Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/sheet_chunks.pruv`. `pub sheet_groups` (`! alloc`) takes per piece the row, `col1`, `col2` and UTF-16 length, plus the header length and `max`, and returns per chunk `[from, to, row1, row2, col1, col2]`; it counts in a first pass and fills in a second. Malformed input (arrays of different lengths, a row or column outside 1..10^15, a length outside 0..2^40) gives `[-1]`; the chunk count is proved at most the piece count and the size proved within `header + 2^40 + 1`. `pub range_of` builds the range text in Pruv (the verified `col_letters` of the L1 bundle and its own decimal digits). Domain: integer `1 <= max <= 2^40`, header at most 2^40, at most 10^11 pieces. Verified by both compilers after two fixes (the full length ladder on `col_letters`, a size bound instead of a clamp). No third-party crate. The per-sheet cell and character limits of `extractSheets` stay in the host.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_sheet_chunks.py`, independent: the section 84 row oracle for the pieces, a direct Python grouping with Python UTF-16 lengths, and its own column letters for the ranges.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 4,230 edge + 20,000 random = 24,230 sheets, 4,652,209 rows, 1,112,011 pieces, 989,642 chunks; 4,489 ranges; 9 malformed arrays):** empty sheets and rows, empty, Korean and 4-byte sheet names, `max` 1 (every piece alone) to 3,000, row gaps of 1, 48 to 52 and 100, chunk sizes exactly at, one under and one over `max`, columns at Z/AA, AZ/BA, ZZ/AAA and 731; ranges with rows and columns from 1 to 10^15 including single cells.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv, the TS original in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) and the oracle agree in all 24,230 sheets and 4,489 ranges; all 9 malformed arrays give `[-1]`.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `cellRange(row1, row2, col1, col2)` and `sheetChunks(name, pieces, max)` (native) and `extractSheetChunks(name, rows, max)` (JS glue: `rowPieces` on every row, then `sheetChunks`, as Lair's chunk objects); out-of-domain values throw `InvalidArg`. `gen/sheet_chunks_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 6,230 sheets at a tenth of the case count, since the emitted text otherwise passes Node's string limit, and 4,489 ranges): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. The cell and character limits (`ctx.limits.cells`, `ctx.limits.chars`) and the missing-part notes are still TS.

## 86. L2 thirteenth bundle: the text window around a line (`textAround`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/preview.ts` `textAround` with `whole` and `failAround` (commit 60afe277, unchanged at 698e1bd1): the bytes shown around a target line of a text file, lines `max(1, line - before)` to `line + after` (defaults 200 and 200) within `maxBytes` (default 256 KiB). The target line is always shown (cut to `maxBytes` when longer, then back to a whole UTF-8 character); the rest of the budget is split half before and half after it, the unused part of one side going to the other, and a side cut by the budget is trimmed to whole lines. A target past the end shows the last line; a first line past the end gives `linePastEnd`. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/text_around.mjs` (types removed; the section 75 `decodeBytes` copy); Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/text_around.pruv`. `pub around_plan` (`! alloc`) takes the file bytes, the line, before, after and `maxBytes` and returns `[0, totalLines]` or `[1, totalLines, shown target, beforeStart, beforeEnd, targetStart, targetEnd, afterStart, afterEnd, newlines before, truncated, target cut]`; the host hashes, slices, decodes and counts the shown lines, the same tail steps as Lair. Every byte read is proved in bounds and every offset proved ordered; `[-2]` (an ordering the scans cannot produce) never appears in the tests. Lair finds the line offsets in one pass over 1 MiB blocks; the Pruv version uses separate scans (newline count, k-th newline, first/last newline in a range), since one function with all the offsets did not verify within 10 minutes and the split version verifies in about 4 seconds. Domain: integer `1 <= line <= 10^15`, `0 <= before, after <= 10^15`, `0 <= maxBytes <= 2^40`, files of at most 2^40 bytes. Verified by both compilers after two fixes (a guard for a file without a newline before the last-line fallback, a lower bound on the end offset). No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_text_around.py`, independent: line offsets from Python `bytes.split(b'\n')`, the trims by `find`/`rfind` on slices, and a regular expression for the UTF-8 cut at the end of the target line.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 7,020 edge + 20,000 random = 27,020 cases, 47,112,985 bytes; 16,564 shown, 7,140 with the target cut, 9,931 truncated):** empty files, files of only newlines, with and without a final newline, CRLF, a UTF-8 BOM, 2-, 3- and 4-byte characters cut at every budget from 0 to 29 bytes, invalid UTF-8 and EUC-KR bytes, targets and first lines past the end, before/after from 0 to 10^15, budgets of 0 to 10 bytes, 256 KiB and 2^40, and files over the 1 MiB read block with newlines one byte before, on and after its edge.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** the Pruv plan equals the oracle in all 27,020 cases, and the TextAround built from the Pruv plan equals the verbatim `textAround` on a temporary file in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) in all 27,020 (every field, including `sourceVersion` and `text`), on the first verified build.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `textAroundPlan(bytes, line, before, after, maxBytes)` (native) and `textAround(request)` (async JS glue: Lair's defaults, line flooring, open errors and `expectSource` check, then the plan, `decodeBytes` and Lair's tail steps). The glue reads the whole file into memory instead of 1 MiB blocks; after the defaults, out-of-domain values throw `InvalidArg`. `gen/text_around_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 9,020 plans at a tenth of the random cases, 8,822 glue requests on temporary files including defaults, a fractional line, `expectSource` and a missing file): 0 plan mismatches against the oracle, and every field but `text` equal to the TS copy; `text` differs in 316 requests, all from this Node's `TextDecoder('euc-kr')` (the section 75 cause; none in Electron); error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Reading in blocks (for files larger than memory) stays with the host.

## 87. L2 fourteenth bundle: the DOCX HTML parser (`parseHtml`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/docx.ts` `parseHtml` with `decode`, `ENTITIES` and `VOID` (commit b99c75b6, unchanged at 698e1bd1): the small parser over mammoth's HTML. Its token regex reads comments, opening and closing tags (with the attribute text up to the first `>`), text runs and a lone `<`; a closing tag cuts the open stack back to below its last element of that name, br/img/hr and `/>` tags are not pushed, attribute values `name="..."` and text are entity-decoded (`&#x..;`, `&#..;`, the six named entities, ASCII case-insensitive), and blank top-level text is dropped. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/parse_html.mjs` (types removed); Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/parse_html.pruv` (29 functions). `pub parse_html` (`! alloc`) takes the HTML and returns one flat record list in creation order: text `[0, parent, n, code points]`, element `[1, parent, n, lowercase tag code points, then for href, colspan and rowspan -1 or m and the decoded value]` (the attributes Lair's DOCX code reads; the last one of a name wins). The token scans follow the regex alternatives in order, including JS `\s` for the whitespace class and JS `trim` for the blank test; a count pass sizes the output exactly before the fill pass, and the open stack is sized by the number of `<` bytes. Domain: at most 2^38 UTF-8 bytes; `[-1]` would mean an output over 2^40 slots and never appears in the tests. Verified by both compilers after five fixes (a guard on the hex entity branch, a bound on the UTF-8 character length, a loop invariant on the closing-quote scan, the domain narrowed to 2^38 for the stack size, and the output bound check). No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_parse_html.py`, independent: Python `re` with the token and attribute regexes rebuilt on the explicit JS whitespace class, entity decoding by `re.sub`, and a list stack walk that writes the same records.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 74 edge + 20,000 random = 20,074 cases, 4,705,234 bytes, 2,517,860 record slots):** mammoth-like documents (paragraphs, headings, lists, tables with colspan/rowspan, links, images, strong/em), tag soup with unclosed and mismatched tags, comments with and without an end, `<` at every position, attributes with and without quotes or `=`, repeated names, uppercase names, whitespace of every JS `\s` code point, every entity form (valid, out of range, zero, uppercase, unknown names such as `&constructor;`), astral characters, and large cases (800-deep nesting, 4,000 open comments, 3,000 open `href` quotes, a 50,000-character attribute name, 400 joined documents).
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** the Pruv records equal the oracle records in all 20,074 cases, and the tree built from them equals the verbatim `parseHtml` tree in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) in all 20,074 (tag, own href/colspan/rowspan and children, compared as JSON); the TS tree also equals the oracle tree in all 20,074.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `parseHtmlRecords(html)` (native; over 2^38 bytes throws `InvalidArg`) and `parseHtml(html)` (JS glue that builds Lair's `{tag, attrs, children}` objects from the records). `gen/parse_html_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 2,074 cases at a tenth of the random cases): 0 record mismatches against the oracle and 0 tree differences against the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Attributes other than href, colspan and rowspan are not kept (Lair does not read them). The block walk over the tree (collectBlocks, inlineText, renderHtml) is the next bundle.

## 88. L2 fifteenth bundle: the DOCX block walk (`docxParts`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/docx.ts` `docxParts` past its `parseHtml` call, with `inlineText`, `collapse`, `collectBlocks`, `renderHtml`, `inlineRender`, `escapeHtml` / `escapeAttr` and the `<img\b` count (commit b99c75b6): the blocks of a DOCX (p, h1..h6, list items without their nested lists, table rows as cells joined by ` | `, each with the whitespace-collapsed text), and the preview HTML that keeps only the allowed tags, marks numbered blocks with `data-block`, keeps colspan/rowspan of 1..3 digits and links only to `https://` (ASCII case-insensitive). Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/docx_parts.mjs` (types removed; parseHtml from `parse_html.mjs`); Lair is not changed.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/docx_parts.pruv` (30 functions). `pub docx_parts` (`! alloc`) takes the parse_html record list (section 87), validates it record by record, builds a stride-10 child/sibling tree, and returns one flat list: `[nblocks, per block (kind 0 p / 1 h / 2 li / 3 tr, element number, n, text code points), hlen, preview HTML code points]`. The recursions of the TS (collectBlocks, inlineText, renderHtml) are explicit frame stacks sized by the element count; `collapse` is one pass with the JS `trim` whitespace class. Like parse_html, a count pass (output into a zero-length list) sizes the output before the fill pass. `pub img_count` counts `<img` not followed by an ASCII word byte, case-insensitive. Domain: record lists of at most 2^36 slots; a malformed list (a kind not 0/1, a parent not an earlier element, a length past the list, a code point outside 0..U+10FFFF, a cut record) or a longer one gives `[-1]`. Verified by both compilers after four fixes (a scaled bound on the attribute scan with an ensures on its result, which also cleared two overflow checks on index sums, and a length guard in the tree build). No third-party crate.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_docx_parts.py`, independent: a recursive Python walk over the tree rebuilt from the oracle records of `run_parse_html.py`, `re` for collapse with the JS whitespace class written out, and string formatting for the HTML.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 64 edge + 20,000 random = 20,064 cases, 26,405,946 bytes, 18,257,630 oracle slots; plus 14 malformed record lists):** documents with paragraphs, headings h1..h7 (h7 not a heading), nested lists with stray non-li children, li with p/div and text after nested lists, tables with thead/tbody/tfoot, empty cells and rows of only separators, cells with p and br, colspan/rowspan values in and out of 1..3 ASCII digits (Arabic-Indic digits, signs, spaces), links with https in any case, other schemes, the Kelvin sign and `ſ`, quotes and ampersands in href, script/style, br/img/`<img` boundary forms, sup/sub/u/strong/em, div, every JS whitespace around line breaks (U+0085 and U+1680 included), the parse_html mammoth-like and tag soup generators, and large cases (600-deep nested lists, 800-deep divs, a 3,000-cell row, 300 joined documents, 20,000 space-newline pairs).
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** the Pruv list equals the oracle list in all 20,064 cases, and the parts built from it (fromOutput) equal the verbatim `docxParts` in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) in all 20,064 (blocks with element preorder positions, HTML and image count, compared as JSON); the parts built from the oracle list also equal the TS in all 20,064; all 14 malformed lists give `[-1]`. A first malformed list in the test set was in fact valid (a top-level empty text) and was replaced.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `docxPartsRecords(html)` (native: `[len(rec), the parse_html records, the image count, the docx_parts list]`; over 2^38 bytes throws `InvalidArg`) and `docxParts(html)` (JS glue: Lair's `{blocks: [{n, kind, el, text}], html, images}` with `el` the El object built from the records). `gen/docx_parts_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 2,064 cases): 0 list mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences against the TS copy (block elements compared as subtrees); error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. The mammoth conversion itself stays in the host.

## 89. L2 sixteenth bundle: the search tokenizer (`tokenize`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/search.ts` `tokenize` with `CJK`, `WORD`, `RUN` and `WHOLE_MAX` (commit 5d974738): lowercase the text (`toLowerCase`), take the `[\p{L}\p{N}]+` words, split each word into runs of the four CJK scripts (Hangul, Han, Hiragana, Katakana) and runs of everything else; a run whose first UTF-16 unit is not CJK is one token, a one-character CJK run is one token, a CJK run of 3..=8 characters is also a token whole, and every adjacent character pair of a CJK run of two or more is a token. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/tokenize.mjs` (types removed); Lair is not changed.
- **Left out: `buildIndex` and `scoreIndex`.** They compute the average document length (`total / i`), the BM25 idf (`Math.log`) and the score by floating-point division. Pruv has only i64 arithmetic, so their results cannot be matched; they stay in the host.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/tokenize.pruv` (14 functions, 9,465 lines). `pub tokenize` (`! alloc`, input at most 2^38 bytes) decodes the UTF-8, lowercases with ICU's rules (the simple mapping, U+0130 to `i` + U+0307, and Final_Sigma for U+03A3: `ς` when a Cased character comes before and none after, skipping Case_Ignorable characters, Case_Ignorable tested first as ICU does), then a count pass into a zero-length list sizes the output for the fill pass. Output: `[count, per token: n, code points]`, `[-1]` past 2^40 slots (not reachable from a string input of the domain). The character tables `wc` (word / CJK word), `ct` (Cased / Case_Ignorable) and `lw1` (simple lowercase, constant shifts and upper/lower pairs) are `if c < X` search trees of 1,568, 1,184 and 305 segments generated from UCD 17.0.0 (the version of ICU 78.2 in Lair's Electron) by `gen_tokenize_tables.py` (`--check` tells stale tables; `tokenize_ucd.py` downloads the four UCD files into the uncommitted `bench/l0/work/ucd17` and checks their version). The word, CJK and lowercase data were swept over every code point except surrogates and U+03A3 against Node v26.4.0 (ICU 78.3, Unicode 17.0: `[\p{L}\p{N}]`, the CJK script class, `toLowerCase` of the one character): 0 differences; Cased and Case_Ignorable are covered by the sigma cases. One fix: `emit` was missing its `! alloc` effect. No third-party crate.
- **A TS quirk kept:** `CJK.test(part[0])` reads one UTF-16 unit, so a CJK run starting with a supplementary-plane character (CJK extension B and later, a lone high surrogate to the regex) is one whole token with no pairs. The Pruv version tests the first code point against the BMP only, the same result.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_tokenize.py`, independent: Python string slicing over code point lists with the UCD sets of `tokenize_ucd.py` (Python 3.9's own `unicodedata` is Unicode 13, so it is not used), lowercase and Final_Sigma written out by context scan.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 89 edge + 20,000 random = 20,089 cases, 1,901,008 bytes, 328,443 oracle tokens, 957,774 oracle slots):** mixed Hangul/Han/kana/Latin/Greek/Cyrillic/digits, every CJK run length 1..=12 around WHOLE_MAX, supplementary Han starts, U+0130, Σ in every Final_Sigma context (324 cases contain ς, 522 σ) with Case_Ignorable marks around it, combining marks, punctuation and separators, emoji, the empty string, and large cases. No malformed inputs: a JS string reaches Pruv as UTF-8 of its code points (lone surrogates as U+FFFD), so `[-1]` cannot be reached and invalid UTF-8 cannot occur.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** the Pruv list equals the oracle in all 20,089 cases, and the tokens built from it (fromOutput) equal the verbatim `tokenize` in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) in all 20,089; the oracle tokens also equal the TS in all 20,089. About 12.6 s.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `tokenizeRecords(text)` (native; over 2^38 bytes throws `InvalidArg`) and `tokenize(text)` (JS glue: the token strings). `gen/tokenize_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0 with Unicode 17.0, 2,089 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences against the TS copy, a lone surrogate gives the U+FFFD tokens, error checks pass. The CI job runs Node 22, whose ICU may be an older Unicode; there a TS difference is only counted, and it fails the test only when `process.versions.unicode` is `17.0`.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Indexing and scoring stay in the host (floating point).

## 90. L2 seventeenth bundle: sheet name and page count labels (`csvName`, `countList`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/sheets.ts` `csvName` and `src/documents/context.ts` `countList` (commit 698e1bd1): `csvName` takes the part of a file name after the last `/` or `\`, removes the extension matched by `/\.[^.]+$/` (from the last `.` when at least one character follows it; `[^.]` also matches newlines) and falls back to `"표"` when nothing is left; `countList` writes the first `max` (default 20) items as `"page (count)"` joined with `", "` and returns `more = max(0, items.length - max)`. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/doc_labels.mjs` (types removed); Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** nothing in these two functions uses floating point, async code or file I/O. The countList domain is integer pages and counts in -10^15..10^15 and an integer `max` in 0..10^9 (`slice` with a negative or fractional `max` is not modelled; the bridge rejects it).
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/doc_labels.pruv` (5 functions). `pub csv_name` scans bytes for the last separator and the last dot after it; `/`, `\` and `.` are ASCII and never occur inside a UTF-8 sequence, so the byte result equals the JS UTF-16 result for well-formed strings; proved `len(result) <= len(name) + 3`. `pub count_list` (proved at most 39 bytes per shown item) and `pub count_list_more` (proved equal to `max(0, n - max)`) reuse the decimal helpers of section 81. Both compilers verified it as written; no fix was needed.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_doc_labels.py`, independent: `rfind` on the separators and the dot (no regular expression) and a plain Python formatter for countList.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 123 edge + 20,000 random = 20,123 cases: 10,098 names, 10,025 lists):** names with separators and dots in every position, trailing dots, dot-only and separator-only names, newlines, U+2028, Korean, emoji and other supplementary characters, the empty name and names up to 6,000 characters (2,663 fall back to `"표"`); lists of 0..60 items with the domain ends, `max` 0..40, `max` around the list length and 10^9 (1,385 empty texts, 3,671 with `more > 0`).
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 20,123 cases and the verbatim TS in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) in all 20,123; the oracle equals the TS in all 20,123.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `csvName(name)`, `countListText(pages, counts, max)`, `countListMore(n, max)` (native; out-of-domain numbers or unequal lengths throw `InvalidArg`) and `countList(items, max = 20)` (JS glue: `{list, more}` from the two). `gen/doc_labels_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,123 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. A name with a lone surrogate reaches Pruv as U+FFFD, so `csvName` then differs from the TS in that character.

## 91. L2 eighteenth bundle: range part joining on general strings (`joinRangeParts`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** Lair `src/documents/context.ts` `joinRangeParts` (commit 698e1bd1), also used by `src/backend/evidence.ts` on arbitrary strings: the parts joined with `", "`; if the result is longer than 400 UTF-16 units, the longest prefix of whole parts whose length + 3 is at most 400 is kept and `", …"` is added. No separator is written while the kept text is empty, so leading empty parts vanish from a cut result. Copied verbatim to `bench/l0/join_ranges.mjs` (types removed); Lair is not changed. Section 84 ported it only for the ASCII range texts of `numberRanges`.
- **Left out:** nothing; the function has no floating point, async code or file I/O. Pruv has no string arrays, so the parts are passed as one concatenated text plus a list of byte end offsets.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/join_ranges.pruv` (4 functions). `units` counts UTF-16 units on UTF-8 (a 4-byte character is 2 units, every other character 1). One loop builds the full join and the kept prefix together and tracks whether the limit was passed. Requires ends on character boundaries, nondecreasing, the last one equal to the text length, and a text of at most 10^12 bytes; proved `len(result) <= 1200` (at most 400 units of at most 3 bytes per unit). Both compilers verified it as written; no fix was needed.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_join_ranges.py`, independent: counts units with Python's UTF-16-LE encoder and tries every prefix of whole parts in closed form instead of the loop.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 386 edge + 20,000 random = 20,386 cases):** empty lists and parts, mostly-empty part lists, Lair-shaped page ranges past the limit, ASCII, Korean, emoji and other supplementary characters (2 units), long parts, and parts tuned so the joined length lands at exactly 398..401 units. 8,632 results are the full join and 11,754 are cut (1,045 cut down to just `", …"`); 856 full joins are 398..400 units and 4,609 results are 396..400 units; the longest result is 400 units and 1,200 UTF-8 bytes.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 20,386 cases and the verbatim TS in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) in all 20,386; the oracle equals the TS in all 20,386.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `joinRangeParts(parts)` (native; concatenates the parts and builds the end offsets; more than 10^12 UTF-8 bytes in total throws `InvalidArg`, a non-array or non-string part is a type error). `gen/join_ranges_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,386 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. A part with a lone surrogate reaches Pruv as U+FFFD (still 1 unit), so the length count is unchanged but the text then differs from the TS in that character.

## 92. L2 nineteenth bundle: sheet window cell cut (`cutCell`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** the cell cut inside Lair `src/documents/preview.ts` `sheetWindow` (commit 698e1bd1): a cell text longer than `CELL_CUT` = 200 UTF-16 units becomes `text.slice(0, 200) + "…"`, a shorter one is kept. `CELL_CUT` and the cut expression are copied verbatim into a one-argument function in `bench/l0/cut_cell.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** the rest of `sheetWindow` (file reading, parse cache, sha256, the window arithmetic over rows and columns) and the `cut` list of cut cell positions it fills. A UTF-8 string cannot hold the lone high surrogate that TS keeps when unit 200 is the first half of a pair, so the Pruv version writes U+FFFD there, which is what the TS string becomes once encoded as UTF-8; results are compared on that UTF-8 form.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/cut_cell.pruv` (3 functions). One loop walks the characters while at most 200 units are behind it (a 4-byte character is 2 units, every other character 1) and stops at a pair that would straddle unit 200; a character longer than 3 bytes per unit (impossible in valid UTF-8) also stops it, which gives the byte bound. The termination measure is `2 * (len - i) + go`, since the stopping steps do not move `i`. Requires a text of at most 10^12 bytes; proved `len(result) <= 606` (200 units of at most 3 bytes, U+FFFD and "…"). The first draft failed the termination check because the stopping steps kept `i`; with the measure above both compilers verified it.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_cut_cell.py`, independent: encodes the text as UTF-16-LE, keeps the first 400 bytes when there are more, decodes them with lone surrogates allowed, maps a lone surrogate to U+FFFD and appends "…".
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 137 edge + 20,000 random = 20,137 cases):** the empty string, ASCII, Korean, accents, emoji and other supplementary characters, "…" and U+FFFD inside the text, newlines and U+2028, texts around 200 units, texts with an emoji at or across unit 200, a 201-unit text that already ends in "…", and long texts. 8,384 results are unchanged (1,018 of 198..200 units) and 11,753 are cut, 4,992 of them through a surrogate pair; the longest result is 201 units and 603 UTF-8 bytes.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 20,137 cases and the verbatim TS in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) after UTF-8 encoding in all 20,137; the oracle equals the TS in all 20,137.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `cutCell(text)` (native; more than 10^12 UTF-8 bytes throws `InvalidArg`, a non-string is a type error). `gen/cut_cell_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,137 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy after UTF-8 encoding; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. The JS string from `cutCell` has U+FFFD where the TS string has a lone high surrogate (same UTF-8). A lone surrogate in the input reaches Pruv as U+FFFD (still 1 unit), so the cut point is unchanged but that character differs from the TS. The `cut` flag is not returned: a 201-unit text that already ends in "…" after its first 200 units is cut to itself, so the flag cannot be read off the result.

## 93. L2 twentieth bundle: sheet window arithmetic (`sheetRange`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** the window arithmetic inside Lair `src/documents/preview.ts` `sheetWindow` (commit 698e1bd1): from the sheet size and the request fields `row`, `col`, `rows`, `cols` it computes the first row and column (`Math.max(1, Math.floor(v ?? 1))`), the wanted counts (default 200 rows / 50 columns, clamped to 1..500 / 1..100 by `MAX_ROWS` / `MAX_COLS`) and the shown counts cut to what is left of the sheet (`Math.max(0, Math.min(want, total - start + 1))`). `MAX_ROWS`, `MAX_COLS` and those six lines are copied verbatim into `sheetRange(totalRows, totalCols, request)` in `bench/l0/sheet_range.mjs`, which returns `[row, col, rowCount, colCount]`; Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** file reading, the parse cache and sha256, the sheet lookup by name, the cell loop and the cell cut (section 92). `Math.floor` on a fraction, NaN and infinities are outside the domain: request fields are integers with |v| <= 2^53 - 1 (where `Math.floor` is the identity) and sheet sizes are 0..10^12.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/sheet_range.pruv` (4 functions). An absent field is passed as a bit of `given` (1 row, 2 col, 4 rows, 8 cols) and takes its default; `window_start` is proved to return 1..2^53 - 1, `window_count` to return 0..cap and to keep the window inside the sheet (`result == 0 || start - 1 + result <= total`), and `sheet_range` returns 4 values. Both compilers verified the first draft.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_sheet_range.py`, independent: counts the wanted span `[start, start + want - 1]` inside `[1, total]` as `len(range(max(lo, 1), min(hi, total) + 1))` and clamps the wanted count with `sorted((1, v, cap))[1]`, not by copying the TS min/max chain. Absent and null fields both take the default.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 2,038 edge + 20,000 random = 22,038 cases):** sheet sizes 0..1200, 10^12 and random up to 10^12; fields absent, null, negative, 0, 1, around the defaults and caps (49..51, 99..101, 199..201, 499..501), near the sheet end, and +/-(2^53 - 1). Outcomes: 8,079 empty row windows and 8,714 empty column windows, 2,228 full 500-row and 2,565 full 100-column windows, 2,336 default 200-row and 2,128 default 50-column windows, 5,764 row windows cut by the sheet end, 3,294 row starts clamped to 1; the largest start is 2^53 - 1.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 22,038 cases and the verbatim TS in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) in all 22,038; the oracle equals the TS in all 22,038.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `sheetRange(totalRows, totalCols, request = {})` (native; a missing, undefined or null field takes its default, a non-integer or out-of-range number throws `InvalidArg`, a non-number is a type error). `gen/sheet_range_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 22,038 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Fractional, NaN or infinite request fields (which `Math.floor` and `??` handle in the TS) are rejected by the bridge instead of floored.

## 94. L2 twenty-first bundle: Korean page range text (`rangeText`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** `rangeText` in Lair `src/documents/pdf.ts` (commit 698e1bd1), the text used in the Korean missing-part and note messages for PDF pages (time limit, unreadable images, OCR limit, blank and blurry pages): page numbers as `"3~5쪽, 8쪽"`, where a run of consecutive numbers becomes `first~last쪽`, a single number `n쪽`, joined with `", "` and with no length cap. It is copied verbatim (types removed, exported) into `bench/l0/page_ranges.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** the message sentences around it and the `numberRanges` key arguments next to it (ported in an earlier bundle). Non-integers, NaN and infinities are outside the domain: at most 10^7 integers in -10^15..10^15 (where JS prints plain decimal).
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/page_ranges.pruv`, reusing the decimal writer of `number_ranges.pruv` (`digits`, `num`) and a new `page_ranges` with `ensures len(result) <= 40 * len(pages)` (each range adds at most `", "` + two 17-byte numbers + `"~"` + the 3-byte `쪽`). The first draft lacked a length bound and failed the `string_push_str` precondition; adding `requires len(pages) <= 10000000` and the loop invariant `len(out) <= 40 * i` made both compilers verify it, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_page_ranges.py`, independent: marks the indices where a new run starts (index 0 and every k with `nums[k] != nums[k-1] + 1`), slices between those marks and formats each slice, not by copying the TS scan.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 2,072 edge + 20,000 random = 22,072 cases):** empty, single numbers, -0, the domain ends and runs at them, runs through zero, unsorted lists and duplicates (the TS does not require order), every gap pattern of 1..11 numbers (2,047 lists), lists up to 3,000 numbers, random page-like ascending lists, free numbers, descents and duplicates. Outcomes: 956 empty results, 17,088 with at least one run, 4,028 with single pages only; up to 1,000 ranges and 8,443 UTF-8 bytes in one result.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 22,072 cases and the verbatim TS in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) in all 22,072; the oracle equals the TS in all 22,072.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `pageRanges(pages)` (native; more than 10^7 elements or a nonfinite, fractional or out-of-range element throws `InvalidArg`, a non-array is a type error). `gen/page_ranges_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 22,072 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Fractional or non-finite page numbers (which the TS would print) are rejected by the bridge.

## 95. L2 twenty-second bundle: grouped integer text (`groupedNumber`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** `groupedNumber` in Lair `src/documents/context.ts` (commit 698e1bd1), `value.toLocaleString('en-US')`, used for the limits quoted in the Korean missing-part and note messages (the text character limit, the tiny-image pixel count): an integer as decimal text with a `,` between each group of three digits counted from the right (`"1,000,000"`, `"-1,234"`). It is copied verbatim (types removed) into `bench/l0/grouped_number.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** fractions (Lair only passes integers; `toLocaleString` would round them to three fraction digits), NaN, infinities and -0 (which prints `"-0"`). The domain is integers in -10^15..10^15.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/grouped_number.pruv`: `grouped_digits` writes the digits from the 10^15 place down, skipping leading zeros and putting `","` before each digit at place 2, 5, 8, 11 or 14 once something is written; `grouped_number` adds the sign. The loop invariant `len(out) <= 2 * k` gives `ensures len(result) <= 33` (the longest real result is 22 bytes), which covers the `string_push_str` precondition. Both compilers verify it on the first draft, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_grouped_number.py`, independent: Python's `format(n, ',')`, not a copy of the TS or of ICU.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 111 edge + 20,000 random = 20,111 cases):** zero, ±1, every power of ten from 10^0 to 10^15 and its neighbours on both signs, the group boundaries 999/1000/1001 and 999,999/1,000,000, the domain ends, and random integers of every digit count on both signs. Outcomes: 10,064 negative, 16,407 with at least one comma, up to 5 commas and 22 bytes in one result.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 20,111 cases and the verbatim TS in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) in all 20,111; the oracle equals the TS in all 20,111.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `groupedNumber(value)` (native; a nonfinite, fractional, out-of-range or -0 value throws `InvalidArg`, a non-number is a type error). `gen/grouped_number_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,111 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Fractional values and -0, which the TS would format, are rejected by the bridge.

## 96. L2 twenty-third bundle: path-under-root check (`under`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** `under` in Lair `src/documents/archive.ts` (commit 698e1bd1), `path === root || path.startsWith(root.endsWith(sep) ? root : root + sep)` with `sep` from `node:path`, used by `isRootEnded` (refuse reads under an ended temporary chat root) and `dropArchiveCaches` (drop cached tables and indexes under a root). It is copied verbatim (types removed, exported) into `bench/l0/under_root.mjs`; Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** the separator is not read from the platform inside Pruv; `under(path, root, sep)` takes it as an ASCII byte, and the bridge passes `std::path::MAIN_SEPARATOR` (`/`, or `\` on Windows), which is what `node:path` `sep` is. Strings with lone surrogates are out of the domain (the bridge receives them as U+FFFD).
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/under_root.pruv`: a byte prefix loop over `root`, then equal length, or `root` ending in `sep`, or `sep` at `path[len(root)]`. The `ensures` states the whole predicate with a `forall` over the prefix bytes, so the result is proved equal to the specification, not just bounded. On well-formed strings a UTF-16 prefix is a UTF-8 byte prefix and an ASCII byte is only ever that character, so the byte predicate is the TS one. Both compilers verify it on the first draft, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_under_root.py`, independent: a regular expression over code points (`re.escape(root)`, then `sep` unless `root` already ends with it, then anything) or exact equality, not a copy of the TS. Pruv is checked with both separators; the TS copy with its platform's own (`/` here).
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 32 edge + 20,000 random = 20,032 cases):** empty paths and roots, roots ending in a separator, doubled and mixed separators, sibling names sharing a prefix (`/ab` under `/a`), drive-style Windows paths, Korean, accented and emoji names, a 4,096-byte root, and random roots and paths over `/ \ a b . : space é 가 😀`. Outcomes: 7,752 under with `/`, 7,698 under with `\`, 3,055 with path equal to root, 13,483 with non-ASCII text, up to 4,098 bytes in one path.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 20,032 cases with each separator and the verbatim TS in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2) in all 20,032; the oracle equals the TS in all 20,032.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `under(path, root)` (native, platform separator; a path or root over 1e12 UTF-8 bytes throws `InvalidArg`, a non-string is a type error). `gen/under_root_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,032 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy; error checks pass. The emitted rows carry the oracle for both separators, so the Windows CI job checks the `\` case against its own TS.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Lone surrogates, which the TS compares as UTF-16 units, are not checked.

## 97. L2 twenty-fourth bundle: the cut of `capChars` (`capKeep`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** `capChars` in Lair `src/documents/extract.ts` (commit 698e1bd1), which adds up the extracted chunks' `text.length` from the front and drops the first chunk whose running total goes over the extracted-text limit, with everything after it. It is copied verbatim (types removed, exported) into `bench/l0/cap_keep.mjs` together with `groupedNumber` from `context.ts`; `docText`, `addMissing` and `setPartialReason` are stand-ins that record their arguments. Lair is not changed.
