- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/cap_keep.pruv`: `cap_keep(lens, max)` keeps a running total with the invariant `used == sum(k in 0..i: lens[k])` and returns `i` when it goes over `max`. The `ensures` say the total through the kept chunks is within `max` and the total through one more chunk is not; with nonnegative lengths this fixes the result exactly. Domain: at most 10^7 chunks of 0..10^9 characters (every running total stays exact in a JS double), `max` in -10^15..10^15. Both compilers verify it on the first draft, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_cap_keep.py`, independent: `bisect_right` of `max` over the prefix totals, not a copy of the TS loop.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 35 edge + 20,000 random = 20,035 cases):** no chunks, empty chunks, negative `max`, `max` exactly at a running total and one below, 1,000 equal chunks, chunks of 10^6 and 10^9 characters, 10,000 chunks of 10^9 (total 10^13), `max` at -10^15 and near 10^15, and random lists of 0 to 12 chunks with `max` drawn around their running totals. Outcomes: 6,872 keep all, 4,700 keep none, 8,463 cut inside.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle in all 20,035 cases. The verbatim TS builds real strings, so it runs on the 20,026 cases whose total is at most 10^6 characters, in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2); Pruv equals it in all 20,026 and the oracle equals it in all 20,026.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `capKeep(lens, max)` (native; more than 1e7 lengths, a length that is not an integer in 0..=1e9, or a `max` that is not an integer in -1e15..=1e15 throws `InvalidArg`). `gen/cap_keep_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,035 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy on 20,026 cases; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. The dropped-range note text is not checked.

## 98. L2 twenty-fifth bundle: the choice of `evict` (`evictStop`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** `evict` in Lair `src/documents/archive.ts` (commit 698e1bd1), which, when the stored versions go over the archive limit, walks them oldest first (by mtime, stable), skips pinned versions and the one to keep, and deletes the rest until the total left is within the limit. It is copied verbatim (types removed) into `bench/l0/evict_stop.mjs` together with `readMeta`, `missing`, `STALE_MS` and `VERSION_PATTERN`; `readdir`, `stat`, `readFile`, `rm` and `join` are replaced by an in-memory folder, so no disk is touched. Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** only the walk (where it stops) is in Pruv. Reading the folder, which entries count as stored versions (version-name pattern, a readable `meta.json`), removing stale temp folders, the `readdir` error path and the real `rm` stay in TS.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/evict_stop.pruv`: `evict_stop(bytes, free, limit)` takes the stored versions in walk order, `bytes[k]` the size and `free[k]` what deleting it frees (`bytes[k]` when deletable, 0 when pinned or kept), and returns the stop index `s`; evict deletes exactly the deletable versions among the first `s`. The loop keeps `left == total - sum(k in 0..s: free[k])` and a `forall` invariant that the total left was over the limit at every earlier index. The `ensures` say every index below `s` was over the limit and at `s` the total left is within it unless the walk ran off the end, which fixes `s` exactly. Domain: at most 10^7 versions of 0..10^9 bytes (every total stays exact in a JS double), `limit` in -10^15..10^15. Both compilers verify it, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_evict_stop.py`, independent: it filters and stably sorts the folder itself, then does `bisect_left` of the limit over the (decreasing) totals left before each version, not a copy of the TS loop. The deleted names follow from `s`.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 35 edge + 20,000 random = 20,035 cases):** empty folders, the limit at the total and one below, negative limits, everything pinned, the kept version first, zero-byte versions, equal mtimes (folder order kept), folders without `meta.json`, temp and non-version names (including an upper-case hex name), versions of 10^9 bytes, 10,000 versions (total 10^13), limits at -10^15 and 10^15, and random folders of 0 to 13 entries with limits drawn around the totals left. Outcomes: 9,093 delete nothing, 11,538 stop inside, 6,705 walk to the end, 6,541 skip a pinned or kept version; at most 5,000 deletions in one case.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv's deleted names equal the oracle's in all 20,035 cases. The verbatim TS runs on the 20,031 folders of at most 2,000 entries, in Lair's Electron (44.11.2, Node 24.22.2); Pruv equals it (same names in the same order) in all 20,031 and the oracle equals it in all 20,031. No difference records.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `evictStop(bytes, free, limit)` (native; more than 1e7 sizes, `bytes` and `free` of different lengths, a size that is not an integer in 0..=1e9, a `free` entry that is neither 0 nor its size, or a `limit` that is not an integer in -1e15..=1e15 throws `InvalidArg`). `gen/evict_stop_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,035 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the TS copy on 20,031 in-memory folders; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. Stale temp-folder removal, the `readdir` error path, the real `fs` calls and how `stat` supplies the mtime are not checked.

## 99. L2 twenty-sixth bundle: index term frequencies (`indexTerms`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** `buildIndex` in Lair `src/documents/search.ts` (commit 698e1bd1, last changed at 5d974738), left out of the tokenize bundle (section 89) for its float. Its integer part is the per-chunk term frequencies: a `tf` Map counts each token and is walked in insertion order (first appearance) to append `(chunk, count)` to each term's posting list. `tokenize` and `buildIndex` are copied verbatim (types removed) into `bench/l0/index_terms.mjs`. Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** the host numbers the token strings (a string-keyed map), appends to the posting lists, pushes `lens` and computes `avg` (a float division); those stay in TS/the host. `scoreIndex` (BM25, floats) is not ported.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/index_terms.pruv`: `index_terms(tok, vocab)` takes one chunk's token ids and returns `[m, id_1, count_1, ..., id_m, count_m]`. A first loop counts into `cnt[vocab]` and records the position of each first appearance in `at`; a second loop writes `tok[at[p]]` and its count. Proved: the shape, `m <= len(tok)`, every id in range and a token of the chunk, ids pairwise distinct, every count in `1..=len(tok)`. Not proved: that each count is exactly the number of occurrences and the first-appearance order. A count tied to a quantified id does not instantiate at the concrete token in the solver, and an earlier version with an extra slot array in the invariants timed out, so the contract was cut to what verifies (with only `cnt` and `at`) and the oracle carries the rest. Domain: at most 10^7 tokens, `vocab` in 1..10^7. Both compilers verify it, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_index_terms.py`, independent: it tokenizes with the tokenize oracle (section 89), counts with `collections.Counter`, finds first positions by a reversed walk and sorts the distinct tokens by them, not a copy of the TS Map loop. The per-chunk Pruv results are compared in order; the host assembles them into `terms`/`lens`/`count`/`avg` and compares with the TS `buildIndex` output. `Object.fromEntries` puts integer-like keys ("12") first in the JSON, so `terms` is compared as a dict.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 24 edge + 20,000 random = 20,024 corpora, 80,277 chunks):** no chunks, empty chunks, one word repeated, Hangul bigrams, integer-like tokens, `constructor`/`tostring`/`hasownproperty` as tokens (`__proto__` tokenizes to `proto`), Greek sigma forms, fullwidth digits, a 100,000-token chunk, a 49,999-bigram chunk, the tokenize edge texts, and random corpora of 0 to 8 chunks from a small word pool (many repeats) or from the tokenize runner's random texts. Outcomes: 2,213 empty corpora, 3,252 empty chunks, 43,910 chunks with a repeated token; at most 99 distinct tokens in a chunk and 199 in a corpus.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle on all 80,277 chunks, and the assembled index equals the verbatim TS `buildIndex` (run in Lair's Electron 44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) on all 20,024 corpora; the oracle equals the TS copy on all 20,024. No difference records.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `indexTerms(tok, vocab)` (native; more than 1e7 ids, `vocab` not an integer in 1..=1e7, or an id not an integer in 0..vocab throws `InvalidArg`). `gen/index_terms_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,024 corpora): 0 mismatches against the oracle per chunk and 0 differences from the TS `buildIndex` (counted only when the runtime has Unicode 17.0, like tokenize); error checks pass.
- **Found (reported, not fixed):** `scoreIndex` looks terms up with `index.terms[token]` on a plain object, so a query token `constructor` that is not in the index finds the inherited `Object` function: `list.length` is its arity, so for `constructor` the loop runs once with `cell` and `tf` undefined and the scores map gets the entry `undefined -> NaN` (checked with Node on the copied loop). Lair is not changed.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet. The exact-count and order properties rest on the oracle runs, not on the proof.

## 100. L2 twenty-seventh bundle: note key pairs (`notePairs`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** `addNote`, `addMissing` and their helper `padded` in Lair `src/documents/context.ts` (commit 698e1bd1, last changed at 8da07603). Each call pushes one text to a draft list (`notes` or `missing`) and keeps the optional key list beside it (`notesI18n` or `missingI18n`): the key list stays absent until the first call that carries a key, and from then on every call pads it with nulls up to the text list's length and pushes its key, or null. The Draft type documents the key list as the same length as its text list, filled only by these two functions. The three functions are copied verbatim (types removed) into `bench/l0/note_pairs.mjs` with a JSON line driver that runs a list of calls on a fresh draft. Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** the texts themselves and the `WireText` objects; the host numbers the keys (`{"key": "k7"}` is 7, no key is 0) and checks the text list in the TS run. The 20 callers in `src/documents` are not ported.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/note_pairs.pruv`: `note_pairs(n0, has, pre, keys)` takes the number of texts already in the list, whether the key list exists (`has`) with its entries `pre` (0 = null), and the key of each call (`keys[j]`, 0 = none). It returns `[0]` when the key list is still absent after all calls, else `[1, entries...]`. A first loop decides whether the list exists at the end (with a witness call when it was created by one); the result is then built in closed form, with `base` the larger of `n0` and `len(pre)` (or `len(pre)` when there are no calls): the old entries, nulls up to `base`, then every call's key at `base + j`. The ensures pin the result down entry by entry: absent exactly when there was no list and no call carried a key; with no calls the old entries as they are; otherwise the old entries, nulls up to the old text count, then each call's key. Domain: `n0`, `len(pre)` and `len(keys)` at most 10^7, keys and entries 0..10^9. Both compilers verify it, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_note_pairs.py`, written from the ensures, not from the TS loop: `[0]` when there is no list and no key, else `[1] + pre + nulls up to n0 (only when there are calls) + keys`. The TS copy runs each case alternately as `addNote` and `addMissing`; its final text list must be the old texts followed by the call texts.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 20 edge + 20,000 random = 20,020 cases, 120,480 calls):** no calls (with no list, an empty list and a shorter list), calls without keys, a key only on the last call, an existing key list shorter than, as long as and longer than the text list, a 100,000-text list with a key on the sixth call, a 50,000-entry key list beside 10 texts, keys of 10^9, and random runs of 0 to 12 calls (key density 0, 0.1, 0.5 or 1) over lists of up to 8 texts with an existing key list 40% of the time. Outcomes: the key list stays absent in 5,363 cases, is created by a call in 6,665, ends as long as the text list in 11,644, longer in 2,687 and shorter in 326 (no calls); at most 100,006 texts and 100,006 key entries.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle and the verbatim TS (run in Lair's Electron 44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) on all 20,020 cases; the oracle equals the TS copy on all 20,020. No difference records.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `notePairs(n0, has, pre, keys)` (native; `n0` not an integer in 0..=1e7, `has` not 0 or 1, more than 1e7 entries or calls, entries with `has` 0, or an entry or key not an integer in 0..=1e9 throws `InvalidArg`). `gen/note_pairs_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,020 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the verbatim TS `addNote`/`addMissing`; error checks pass.
- **Found (noted, not a bug in reachable code):** `padded` only pads; it never trims a key list that is longer than its text list, so such a list stays misaligned after further calls. With no calls, an existing shorter key list also stays short. `addNote`/`addMissing` alone never build either state, so the documented same-length rule holds for drafts built only through them. Lair is not changed.
- **Not done:** nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 101. L2 twenty-eighth bundle: spreadsheet date cell text (`cellText` Date branch, `cellDate`) (2026-11-13)

- **Target:** the Date branch of `cellText` in Lair `src/documents/sheets.ts` (commit 698e1bd1). A spreadsheet date cell (from read-excel-file) becomes `"YYYY-MM-DD"` from the UTC calendar fields, plus `" HH:MM"` when the UTC hour, minute or second is nonzero; the year is `String(getUTCFullYear())` with no padding, the other fields are padded to two digits by `pad`. `cellText` feeds every sheet chunk and the sheet window preview. `pad` and the whole `cellText` are copied verbatim (types removed) into `bench/l0/cell_date.mjs` with a JSON line driver that calls `cellText(new Date(ms))`. Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** the other branches of `cellText`: strings (`\r?\n` to space and JS `trim`), numbers (`toPrecision(15)`, a float), booleans and the `String(value)` fallback. Invalid Dates (`NaN` time) go to the fallback and are outside the domain.
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/cell_date.pruv`: `cell_date(t)` takes the time value in milliseconds. It shifts the time by 685 whole 400-year cycles (100,076,445 days) so every division is on nonnegative numbers, runs the March-based civil-from-days walk, undoes the 274,000-year shift and writes the fields with a small decimal writer (`dec(n, width)`, zero padded). Domain: every valid Date, `|t| <= 8.64e15` (years -271821..275760). Proved: no overflow, every digit index in range, and the result at most 35 bytes (the longest is 19, `"-271821-04-21 23:59"`). The calendar itself is checked by the oracle, not proved. Both compilers verify it, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_cell_date.py`, independent of the TS and of the Pruv walk: Python's `datetime.date.fromordinal` after moving the day by whole 400-year cycles into datetime's years 1..400 (the Gregorian calendar repeats every 146,097 days), with the cycles added back to the year; the time part from floor division of the millisecond of the day.
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 340 edge + 20,000 random = 20,340 cases):** the domain ends and one day inside them, the epoch, year 0 and the years around it, 9999/10000, the leap days of 0, -4, 1900, 2000, 2024 and 2100, the first and last valid days, years -99999 and 100000, each with offsets of 1 ms, 999 ms, 1 s, 59.999 s, 1 min, 1 h and the last millisecond of the day on both sides; random values over the whole range, within about 127 years of the epoch, on whole days, on whole seconds and on days with a sub-minute offset. Outcomes: 8,198 negative years, 7,667 years above 9999, 13,850 with a time part, 6,490 date only, at most 19 bytes.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle and the verbatim TS (run in Lair's Electron 44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) on all 20,340 cases; the oracle equals the TS copy on all 20,340. No difference records.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `cellDate(ms)` (native; `ms` nonfinite, fractional or outside -8.64e15..=8.64e15 throws `InvalidArg`). `gen/cell_date_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,340 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the verbatim TS `cellText`; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** the non-Date branches of `cellText`; nothing is wired into Lair yet.

## 102. L2 twenty-ninth bundle: spreadsheet string cell text (`cellText` string branch, `cellStr`) (2026-11-14)

- **Target:** the string branch of `cellText` in Lair `src/documents/sheets.ts` (commit 698e1bd1): `value.replace(/\r?\n/g, ' ').trim()`. Every `"\r\n"` and every `"\n"` becomes one space (a lone `"\r"` stays), then ECMAScript `trim` cuts white space and line terminators from both ends. Most spreadsheet cells are strings, so this is the common path of every sheet chunk and the sheet window preview. `pad` and the whole `cellText` are copied verbatim (types removed) into `bench/l0/cell_str.mjs` with a JSON line driver that calls `cellText(text)`. Lair is not changed.
- **Left out:** numbers (`toPrecision(15)`, a float), booleans and the `String(value)` fallback. Strings with lone surrogates are outside the domain (a UTF-8 string cannot hold them).
- **Pruv version:** `bench/l0/cell_str.pruv`: `cell_str(s)` makes one pass over the characters that writes a space for `"\r\n"` and `"\n"` and copies every other character, then a second pass that finds the start of the first and the end of the last character that is not trim white space, read from its UTF-8 bytes (U+0009..U+000D, U+0020, U+00A0, U+1680, U+2000..U+200A, U+2028, U+2029, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000, U+FEFF), and returns that slice. Domain: well-formed strings of at most 10^12 bytes. Proved: every byte index in range, every slice on character boundaries, and the result no longer than the input. Both compilers verify it, with byte-identical output.
- **Oracle:** `bench/l0/run_cell_str.py`, independent of the TS expression: `str.replace("\r\n", " ")` then `str.replace("\n", " ")`, then `str.strip` with an explicit list of the ECMAScript white space and line terminator characters (Python's own `strip()` set differs: it trims U+001C..U+001F and U+0085 and keeps U+FEFF).
- **Inputs (seed 12345, 241 edge + 20,000 random = 20,241 cases):** the empty string; lone, doubled and mixed `"\r"` and `"\n"` (`"\r\r\n"`, `"\n\r"`); every trim character alone, at either end and inside; near misses that must stay (U+0085, U+180E, U+200B, U+2060, U+001C..U+001F, U+00A1, U+1681, U+200C, U+2030, U+3001, U+FEFE and others); long runs of up to 100,000 spaces; random mixes of white space, near misses, ASCII, Korean and emoji up to 2,000 characters. Outcomes: 4,888 unchanged, 3,568 changed only by newlines, 10,456 trimmed, 1,329 empty; 7,332 results keep a lone `"\r"`; inputs up to 150,001 UTF-8 bytes.
- **Results (identical on py/rust, py/llvm, rs/rust, rs/llvm):** Pruv equals the oracle and the verbatim TS (run in Lair's Electron 44.11.2, Node 24.22.2, ICU 78.2) on all 20,241 cases; the oracle equals the TS copy on all 20,241 (so this runtime does not trim U+180E). No difference records.
- **Bridge:** `bridge/node` exports `cellStr(text)` (native; a non-string is a type error, more than 10^12 UTF-8 bytes throws `InvalidArg`). `gen/cell_str_gen.rs` is byte-identical from both compilers (`regen.py --check`). Node test (macOS arm64, Node v26.4.0, 20,241 cases): 0 mismatches against the oracle and 0 differences from the verbatim TS `cellText`; error checks pass.
- **Not done:** the number, boolean and fallback branches of `cellText`; nothing is wired into Lair yet.
